# Entire CLI — Documentation Digest (docs.entire.io, 8 pages)

Pages covered: `/index.md`, `/overview.md`, `/quickstart.md`, `/installation.md`, `/updating.md`, `/platforms/overview.md`, `/platforms/cli.md`, `/platforms/entire-io.md`

Every page begins with the same header block:
&gt; `## Documentation Index` — "Fetch the complete documentation index at: https://docs.entire.io/llms.txt / Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further."

---

## 1. What Entire is

From `/overview.md` (titled **Introduction**, tagline "Git shows what changed. Entire shows why."):

&gt; "Entire is a CLI-first system of record that captures the context behind agent-assisted code changes and links it to Git."

**Why Entire Exists** — when an agent changes code, the final diff leaves out:
- The prompt that started the work
- Files the agent inspected
- Tools it ran
- Decisions it made
- Checkpoints it created along the way (links to `/guides/checkpoints/overview`)

"Entire records that context so teams can review, search, and continue agent work with the same confidence they expect from Git history."

**What Entire Helps You Do**
- Review the prompt, transcript, and tool calls behind a commit, not just the diff.
- Browse Checkpoints from an agent session as the work evolves.
- Search prompts, sessions, and repository history.
- Continue from prior agent work instead of starting cold.
- Share agent context with teammates through Git-linked history.

**How It Works** (numbered, verbatim)
1. Install the Entire CLI in your repository.
2. Connect Entire to the coding agent you use.
3. Entire captures sessions and Checkpoints as the agent works.
4. Use the CLI or Entire.io to inspect the history behind commits.

Core concepts named on `/index.md` (one-line descriptions only, each linking elsewhere):
- **Checkpoints** — "Versioned records of agent-assisted changes." (`/guides/checkpoints/overview`)
- **Repositories** — "Distributed Git repositories for agent-driven development." (`/guides/repositories/overview`)
- **Sessions** — "The prompts and tool calls behind a run." (`/guides/sessions/overview`)
- **Search** — "Find past sessions, commits, prompts, files, and reasoning." (`/guides/search/overview`)

*Note on thinness:* `/index.md` is a landing page built entirely of nav cards (JSX `el-card` markup). It contains **no commands, no config keys, no file paths** — only link targets and card blurbs. `/overview.md` likewise contains **zero command lines**.

---

## 2. Installation

### System requirements (`/installation.md` only)
- **Operating System:** macOS, Linux, Windows
- **Git:** Version 2.25 or later
- **Shell:** Bash or Zsh (for hook installation)

Quickstart's prerequisites are looser and differently worded:
- Git installed and configured
- An AI coding agent installed, such as Claude Code, Codex, Copilot CLI, Cursor, Factory, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, or Pi
- macOS, Linux, or Windows
- Optional: GitHub CLI (`gh`) installed and authenticated, if you want Entire to create and push a private GitHub repository during setup

### Release channels
Stated identically on `/installation.md` and `/updating.md`:
- `stable`: recommended for most users. **This is the default channel for Homebrew, Scoop, and `install.sh`.**
- `nightly`: prerelease builds for users who want the latest changes earlier.

Quickstart adds: "Nightly is available through Homebrew and the Linux install script. Scoop currently supports stable only."

Installation notes: "Entire uses Homebrew casks so the CLI can be installed from the same signed release artifacts used by other install methods."

### macOS — Homebrew
One-time tap + trust:
```bash
brew tap entireio/tap
brew trust entireio/tap
```
&gt; **Note (installation.md):** "Homebrew 5.2.0 and 6.0.0 and later require trusting a third-party tap with `brew trust` before installing its casks." Links to https://docs.brew.sh/Tap-Trust

Install stable:
```bash
brew install --cask entire
```
Install nightly:
```bash
brew install --cask entire@nightly
```
Upgrade:
```bash
brew upgrade --cask entire
```
```bash
brew upgrade --cask entire@nightly
```

### Linux — install script
Stable is the default:
```bash
curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash
```
Nightly:
```bash
curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash -s -- --channel nightly
```

### Linux — direct binary download (installation.md only)
```bash
# Download the latest release
curl -LO https://github.com/entireio/cli/releases/latest/download/entire-linux-amd64.tar.gz

# Extract and install
tar -xzf entire-linux-amd64.tar.gz
sudo mv entire /usr/local/bin/
```
For ARM64 systems:
```bash
curl -LO https://github.com/entireio/cli/releases/latest/download/entire-linux-arm64.tar.gz
```
*(The docs give the ARM64 download line only — no separate extract/move lines are shown for ARM64.)*

### Windows — Scoop
"Scoop currently supports the stable channel only:"
```bash
scoop bucket add entire https://github.com/entireio/scoop-bucket.git
scoop install entire/cli
```
Update:
```bash
scoop update entire/cli
```

### From source
Requires Go 1.21+:
```bash
go install github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire@latest
```
Or clone and build manually:
```bash
git clone https://github.com/entireio/cli.git
cd cli
go build -o entire ./cmd/entire
sudo mv entire /usr/local/bin/
```
`/updating.md` classifies source as: "manual/development flow, not a managed release channel."

### Verify installation
```bash
entire version
```
Expected output shape:
```
entire version 1.0.0 (abc1234)
```
&gt; "The `entire` binary needs to be on your `$PATH` for agent hooks and Git hooks to work."

### Shell completion
Bash — add to `~/.bashrc`:
```bash
source &lt;(entire completion bash)
```
Zsh — add to `~/.zshrc`:
```bash
source &lt;(entire completion zsh)
```
*(Only Bash and Zsh are documented. No fish/PowerShell completion is mentioned anywhere.)*

### Uninstalling
Homebrew:
```bash
brew uninstall --cask entire
```
Manual:
```bash
sudo rm /usr/local/bin/entire
```
"Also remove any configuration:"
```bash
rm -rf ~/.config/entire
```
*(No Scoop uninstall command is documented. No uninstall path for `entire@nightly` is documented.)*

---

## 3. Upgrade paths (`/updating.md`)

Purpose line: "Use this page when you need to update the Entire CLI, switch release channels, or disable interactive update prompts."

Check version first:
```bash
entire version
```
&gt; "Use the update command that matches how you installed Entire."

| Install method | Stable update | Nightly update |
|---|---|---|
| Homebrew | `brew upgrade --cask entire` | `brew upgrade --cask entire@nightly` |
| Scoop | `scoop update entire/cli` | Not available — "Nightly is not currently available via Scoop." |
| Install script | `curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh \| bash` | `curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh \| bash -s -- --channel nightly` |
| From source | `go install github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire@latest` | (same command; no channel concept) |

Install-script wording is "Update or reinstall the stable channel" / "Update or reinstall the nightly channel."

**Supported install paths by channel** (verbatim list from `/updating.md`):
- Homebrew stable: `brew install --cask entire`
- Homebrew nightly: `brew install --cask entire@nightly`
- Scoop stable: `scoop install entire/cli`
- `install.sh` stable: `curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash`
- `install.sh` nightly: `curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash -s -- --channel nightly`
- From source: manual/development flow, not a managed release channel

### Disabling update prompts
"To disable interactive auto-update prompts, set:"
```bash
ENTIRE_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1
```
"Use this in scripts, CI, or environments where the CLI should not prompt while running."

This is the **only environment variable documented on any of the eight pages**. The docs show it as a bare `ENTIRE_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` in a bash block — they do **not** show an `export` form or say where to set it.

*Gap:* no page documents how to switch channels in place (e.g. stable → nightly), beyond installing the other cask/channel. No downgrade or version-pinning instructions exist.

---

## 4. Quickstart, in order (`/quickstart.md`)

Framing: "This quickstart walks you from installing Entire to creating your first checkpoint: a record of the prompts, transcript, tool calls, and file changes behind an AI-assisted commit."

**Step 1 — Install Entire.** Same four tabs as the installation page (Homebrew stable/nightly, Linux script stable/nightly, Scoop, source). Homebrew tab is annotated `# One-time setup: tap and trust`. Then verify with `entire version`.

**Step 2 — Create a Demo Project**
```bash
mkdir entire-demo
cd entire-demo
```
&gt; **Note:** "Already in a Git repository? Skip this step and run the remaining commands from the repository root."

**Step 3 — Enable Entire.** "Choose the agent you already use, then run its setup command."

| Agent | Command |
|---|---|
| Claude Code | `entire enable -y --agent claude-code` |
| Codex | `entire enable -y --agent codex` |
| Copilot CLI | `entire enable -y --agent copilot-cli` |
| Cursor | `entire enable -y --agent cursor` |
| Factory | `entire enable -y --agent factoryai-droid` |
| Gemini CLI | `entire enable -y --agent gemini` |
| OpenCode | `entire enable -y --agent opencode` |
| Pi | `entire enable -y --agent pi` |

What it does, verbatim: "This installs Git hooks that capture checkpoints when you commit and installs hook configuration for your selected agent. It also creates a `.entire/` directory for project and local settings."

Config file guidance: "For shared projects, commit `.entire/settings.json` and `.entire/.gitignore`. Keep `.entire/settings.local.json` local for personal overrides."

&gt; **Note:** "Because this starts in a new folder, `entire enable -y` initializes Git and creates an initial commit. If `gh` is installed and authenticated, Entire can also create a private GitHub repository and push the initial commit."

**Step 4 — Start an Agent Session.** Launch commands per agent: `claude`, `codex`, `copilot`, `cursor`, `droid` (Factory), `gemini`, `opencode`, `pi`.

Suggested prompt (verbatim text block):
```text
Create a file named hello-entire.md with a short note explaining that this repo is my first Entire checkpoint demo.
```
"When the agent finishes, exit the agent session if needed."

**Step 5 — Commit the Change**
```bash
git status
```
```bash
git add hello-entire.md
git commit -m "Add first Entire demo file"
```
"Because Entire detected recent agent work, it may ask whether to link this commit to the session context. The agent name will match the integration you enabled:"
```text
Entire: Active &lt;agent&gt; session detected
  Last prompt: Create a file named hello-entire.md with a short note...

Link this commit to session context?
  [Y]es / [n]o / [a]lways (remember my choice):
```
"Press `Enter` or type `y` to link the commit. After the commit succeeds, you have created your first checkpoint."
&gt; **Note:** "If you choose `always`, Entire remembers that preference and links future matching commits automatically."

**Step 6 — View Your First Checkpoint**
```bash
entire checkpoint list
```
"You should see the checkpoint linked to your commit."

Storage branch — "Entire stores permanent checkpoint data on a Git branch named:"
```text
entire/checkpoints/v1
```
"Your working branch stays clean. Entire does not add extra commits to it."

**Step 7 (Optional) — Push Your Code and Checkpoint**
```bash
git push
```
"Entire's pre-push hook also syncs checkpoint data to `entire/checkpoints/v1`."

&gt; **Privacy note (important):** "Checkpoint data can include session context. If your repository is public, pushed checkpoint history is public too." Links: `/security`, `/guides/configuration/checkpoint-storage#checkpoint-remote`.

Next-step links: `/glossary`, `/cli-reference`, `/guides/checkpoints/overview`, `/agents/overview`.

---

## 5. CLI vs Entire.io

`/platforms/overview.md`: "Entire has two main surfaces: the CLI and Entire.io."

- CLI: "runs locally in your development environment. It captures agent sessions, creates checkpoints, connects work to Git commits, and gives agents a way to search, review, and reuse prior context."
- Entire.io: "the web app for reviewing and sharing that history. Use it to browse repositories, inspect checkpoints and sessions, search past work, and understand what happened across a team."
- "Most workflows use both. The CLI records the work as it happens. Entire.io makes that work easier to review, search, and collaborate on."

### The CLI (`/platforms/cli.md`)
"The Entire CLI is the local capture and automation layer for Entire. It runs in your Git repository, installs the hooks needed to observe supported agents, and records the context behind agent-assisted development. When you commit, the CLI connects that captured context to Git so future reviewers and agents can understand why the code changed."

"Use the CLI when you are setting up Entire, working from a terminal, scripting a workflow, or giving an agent access to Entire history."

**What the CLI Does** (verbatim bullets):
- Enables Entire in a local repository.
- Captures agent sessions as you work.
- Creates checkpoints and links them to commits.
- Searches prior checkpoints, sessions, and commits.
- Generates dispatches from repository history.
- Manages repository mirrors from the terminal.
- Provides JSON output for automation and agent workflows.

*Thin-page flag:* `/platforms/cli.md` contains **no commands or flags at all** — it defers everything to `/cli-reference`, `/guides/checkpoints/review-in-cli`, `/guides/search/search-in-cli`, `/guides/dispatches/dispatches-in-cli`.

### Entire.io (`/platforms/entire-io.md`)
"Entire.io is the web app for reviewing, searching, and sharing the history captured by Entire."

"Use Entire.io when you want a visual way to understand agent work: what changed, which prompt or session produced it, how a checkpoint relates to a commit, and what happened across a repository or team."

Explicit boundary: "**Entire.io does not replace the CLI. The CLI captures the work locally. Entire.io helps you browse and collaborate on that captured history.**"

**What Entire.io Is For** (verbatim bullets):
- Browse repositories connected to Entire.
- Review checkpoints by repository and branch.
- Inspect the sessions behind a checkpoint.
- Search checkpoints, sessions, and commits visually.
- Review dispatches and share summarized work.
- Understand agent activity without reconstructing it from Git alone.

**Activity Overview:** "After you sign in, Entire.io shows activity across connected repositories, including recent commits, repository activity, and captured checkpoint history." (Accompanied by a hero walkthrough video and a dashboard screenshot; no sign-up/pricing/auth instructions appear anywhere.)

### Capability split as documented

| Capability | CLI | Entire.io |
|---|---|---|
| Enable Entire in a repo / install hooks | yes | not mentioned |
| Capture agent sessions | yes | no (capture is local) |
| Create checkpoints, link to commits | yes | no |
| Review checkpoints | yes (`review-in-cli`) | yes, "visually", by repository and branch |
| Inspect sessions | yes | yes |
| Search checkpoints/sessions/commits | yes | yes, "visually", open results in context |
| Dispatches | generate from repository history | create, review, and share summaries |
| Repository mirrors | yes ("Manages repository mirrors from the terminal") | not mentioned |
| JSON output for automation | yes | n/a |
| Configure storage | listed on `/index.md` card ("configure storage") | not mentioned |
| Share context with team | via Git-linked history | yes, explicit |

Small wording difference worth knowing (not a contradiction): `/index.md`'s CLI card says "Capture sessions, create Checkpoints, configure storage, and automate workflows," while `/platforms/cli.md`'s own bullet list omits "configure storage" and adds mirrors + JSON output. Likewise `/platforms/overview.md`'s CLI card mentions "generate dispatches" and "inspect sessions," and its Entire.io card mentions "create dispatches" — whereas `/platforms/entire-io.md` says "Review dispatches and share summarized work" (review vs create). Treat "create dispatches in Entire.io" as stated only on the overview card.

---

## 6. Files, paths, branches, and env vars actually named

| Item | Source page | What the docs say |
|---|---|---|
| `.entire/` | quickstart | Directory created by `entire enable`, "for project and local settings" |
| `.entire/settings.json` | quickstart | Shared project settings — commit it |
| `.entire/.gitignore` | quickstart | Commit it |
| `.entire/settings.local.json` | quickstart | Personal overrides — keep local |
| `entire/checkpoints/v1` | quickstart | Git branch holding permanent checkpoint data; pre-push hook syncs to it |
| `~/.bashrc` / `~/.zshrc` | installation | Where to add shell completion |
| `/usr/local/bin/entire` | installation | Manual install/uninstall location |
| `~/.config/entire` | installation | Configuration dir to remove on manual uninstall |
| `$PATH` | installation | Binary must be on it for agent hooks and Git hooks to work |
| `ENTIRE_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` | updating | Disables interactive auto-update prompts |

**No individual config keys or values** inside `settings.json` / `settings.local.json` are documented on any of these eight pages — only the file names and the commit/don't-commit split.

## 7. Verbatim command index

Every command line that appears across the eight pages, with what the page says it does.

**Install — macOS/Homebrew**
```bash
brew tap entireio/tap          # one-time: add the Entire tap
brew trust entireio/tap        # one-time: trust the third-party tap (required by Homebrew 5.2.0 / 6.0.0+)
brew install --cask entire     # install stable
brew install --cask entire@nightly   # install nightly
brew upgrade --cask entire           # upgrade stable
brew upgrade --cask entire@nightly   # upgrade nightly
brew uninstall --cask entire         # uninstall
```

**Install — Linux**
```bash
curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash                              # install/update stable (default)
curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash -s -- --channel nightly      # install/update nightly
curl -LO https://github.com/entireio/cli/releases/latest/download/entire-linux-amd64.tar.gz   # direct binary, amd64
tar -xzf entire-linux-amd64.tar.gz                                          # extract
sudo mv entire /usr/local/bin/                                              # install to PATH
curl -LO https://github.com/entireio/cli/releases/latest/download/entire-linux-arm64.tar.gz   # direct binary, arm64
```

**Install — Windows/Scoop**
```bash
scoop bucket add entire https://github.com/entireio/scoop-bucket.git   # add the bucket
scoop install entire/cli                                               # install stable (nightly unavailable)
scoop update entire/cli                                                # update stable
```

**Install — from source (Go 1.21+)**
```bash
go install github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire@latest   # install/update from source
git clone https://github.com/entireio/cli.git          # manual build: clone
cd cli
go build -o entire ./cmd/entire                        # manual build: compile
sudo mv entire /usr/local/bin/                         # manual build: install to PATH
```

**Uninstall — manual**
```bash
sudo rm /usr/local/bin/entire   # remove binary
rm -rf ~/.config/entire         # remove configuration
```

**Entire CLI commands (only four distinct ones appear across these pages)**
```bash
entire version                              # print installed version, e.g. "entire version 1.0.0 (abc1234)"
entire completion bash                      # emit bash completion script (used as: source &lt;(entire completion bash))
entire completion zsh                       # emit zsh completion script (used as: source &lt;(entire completion zsh))
entire enable -y --agent claude-code        # install Git hooks + agent hook config + create .entire/; -y = non-interactive
entire enable -y --agent codex
entire enable -y --agent copilot-cli
entire enable -y --agent cursor
entire enable -y --agent factoryai-droid
entire enable -y --agent gemini
entire enable -y --agent opencode
entire enable -y --agent pi
entire checkpoint list                      # list checkpoints; shows the checkpoint linked to your commit
```

**Shell completion (as written in the docs)**
```bash
source &lt;(entire completion bash)   # add to ~/.bashrc
source &lt;(entire completion zsh)    # add to ~/.zshrc
```

**Git commands used in the quickstart flow**
```bash
mkdir entire-demo
cd entire-demo
git status                                  # see what the agent changed
git add hello-entire.md
git commit -m "Add first Entire demo file"  # triggers the "link this commit to session context?" prompt
git push                                    # pre-push hook also syncs checkpoints to entire/checkpoints/v1
```

**Agent launch commands**
```bash
claude     # Claude Code
codex      # Codex
copilot    # Copilot CLI
cursor     # Cursor
droid      # Factory
gemini     # Gemini CLI
opencode   # OpenCode
pi         # Pi
```

**Environment variable**
```bash
ENTIRE_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1   # disable interactive auto-update prompts (scripts, CI)
```

## 8. Contradictions and gaps

**Contradictions found: none material.** The two overlapping pages (`installation.md` and `updating.md`) state the channel definitions and per-manager commands identically, word for word. Quickstart's install tabs match installation's commands exactly.

Minor tensions / soft inconsistencies to be aware of:
1. **Git version.** `installation.md` requires Git 2.25+; `quickstart.md` says only "Git installed and configured." Use 2.25+ as the authoritative floor.
2. **Dispatch creation.** `/platforms/overview.md`'s Entire.io card says "create dispatches"; `/platforms/entire-io.md`'s own bullets say "Review dispatches and share summarized work." The CLI page says the CLI "Generates dispatches from repository history."
3. **"Configure storage"** is attributed to the CLI on `/index.md` but is absent from `/platforms/cli.md`'s capability list.
4. **Windows shell support.** Requirements list Windows as a supported OS, but the shell requirement is "Bash or Zsh (for hook installation)" and completion is documented for Bash/Zsh only. The docs never explain how hooks work under PowerShell/cmd.

**Pages that are thin (state nothing substantive on their nominal topic):**
- `/index.md` — pure nav landing page. No commands, no config, no behavior.
- `/overview.md` — conceptual only; no commands.
- `/platforms/overview.md` — three paragraphs plus nav cards; no commands.
- `/platforms/cli.md` — describes purpose and a 7-bullet capability list; **no commands, flags, or syntax whatsoever**.
- `/platforms/entire-io.md` — purpose, 6-bullet list, one line about the post-sign-in activity view, plus a video and screenshot. No auth, pricing, org/team setup, or repo-connection instructions.

**Topics not covered anywhere in these eight pages** (need `/cli-reference`, `/agents/overview`, `/security`, `/guides/*`):
- Full command/flag reference beyond `version`, `completion`, `enable -y --agent`, `checkpoint list`
- Any `.entire/settings.json` key names or values
- How to disable/uninstall Entire from a repository (there is an `entire enable` but no documented inverse)
- Checkpoint remote / storage configuration (linked as `/guides/configuration/checkpoint-storage#checkpoint-remote` but not explained)
- Search, dispatch, and mirror command syntax
- Security/privacy detail beyond the one public-repo warning
- Scoop uninstall; nightly uninstall; channel switching in place
- macOS direct-binary download (only Linux archives are given)</result>

# Entire CLI — Configuration Reference (verbatim digest)

Source pages (all fetched as raw `.md`):
`/guides/configuration/` → `overview`, `settings-layers`, `entire-files`, `checkpoint-storage`, `git-hooks`, `agent-hooks`, `privacy-and-redaction`, `logging-and-diagnostics`, `review-profiles`

Every claim below is quoted or paraphrased tightly from those pages. Gaps are flagged **NOT IN DOCS**.

---

## 1. Files Entire generates or touches

### 1a. Commit vs. keep local (from `entire-files.md`, verbatim table)

| Path | Commit? | Use for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `.entire/settings.json` | Yes, when Entire is part of the project | Shared project settings, such as checkpoint storage, team redaction rules, and default repository behavior |
| `.entire/.gitignore` | Yes | Keeping local Entire files out of normal commits |
| `.entire/redactors/` | Yes, for shared rule packs | Team redaction rules that should apply to everyone working in the repository |
| `.entire/settings.local.json` | No | Personal overrides, debug logging, private rules, and machine-specific settings |
| `.entire/redactors/local/` | No | Personal redaction rule packs |
| `.entire/logs/` | No | Local diagnostic logs |
| `.entire/tmp/` | No | Temporary runtime files |
| `.entire/metadata/` | No | Local metadata generated while Entire runs |
| `.entire/current_session` | No | Pointer to your current local session |

The stated governing principle: "Commit files that define shared project behavior. Keep personal overrides, logs, temporary files, and machine-local state out of Git."

Caveat quoted: "If you are trying Entire locally before adopting it for the project, keep `.entire/settings.json` local until the team decides to share Entire configuration."

`.entire/settings.local.json` is "automatically ignored by Git" (`settings-layers.md`, Local Settings tab) — presumably via `.entire/.gitignore`, but the docs never show that file's contents. **NOT IN DOCS: the literal contents of `.entire/.gitignore`.**

### 1b. Files stored elsewhere (`entire-files.md`)

- Checkpoint data: "not committed from your working tree. Entire stores checkpoint metadata on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch or in a configured checkpoint remote."
- Global settings: `~/.config/entire/settings.json`
- Agent hook files: "live in agent-specific folders, such as `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, and `.gemini/`."

### 1c. Git hooks in `.git/hooks`

The docs never enumerate the files written under `.git/hooks`. See §3 — this is the largest gap in the set.

---

## 2. Full settings schema

### 2.1 Precedence (from `overview.md`, verbatim)

&gt; Entire merges settings from multiple places. More specific settings win:
&gt; 1. Local settings: `.entire/settings.local.json`
&gt; 2. Project settings: `.entire/settings.json`
&gt; 3. Global settings: `~/.config/entire/settings.json`
&gt; 4. Defaults

Guidance: "Use project settings for behavior the whole team should share. Use local settings for personal preferences, machine-specific setup, debug logging, or private redaction rules."

Merging is partial, not wholesale replacement: "Local settings are merged with project settings. You only need to specify the settings you want to override."

One documented exception to file-based precedence: `ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL` "takes precedence over settings files."

### 2.2 Project settings schema — `.entire/settings.json` (verbatim table with defaults)

Initial file created by Entire:

```json
{
  "enabled": true
}
```

| Setting path | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `enabled` | boolean | `true` | Whether Entire is active in this repository |
| `commit_linking` | string | `"prompt"` | Controls whether Entire prompts before linking a Git commit to recent agent session context |
| `strategy_options.push_sessions` | boolean | `true` | Automatically push checkpoint data when someone runs `git push` |
| `strategy_options.checkpoint_remote` | object | `null` | Push checkpoint data to a separate repository |
| `strategy_options.summarize.enabled` | boolean | `false` | Auto-generate checkpoint summaries at commit time |
| `redaction.pii.enabled` | boolean | `false` | Enable optional PII redaction on top of always-on secret redaction |
| `redaction.custom_redactions` | object | `{}` | Add shared regex rules that replace matches with `REDACTED` |
| `telemetry` | boolean/null | `null` | Send anonymous usage analytics for this repository |
| `sign_checkpoint_commits` | boolean | `true` | Sign checkpoint commits using your existing Git signing key |
| `log_level` | string | `"info"` | Log verbosity: `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error` |
| `external_agents` | boolean | `false` | Enable discovery of external agent plugins from `$PATH` |
| `absolute_git_hook_path` | boolean | `false` | Embed the full `entire` binary path in Git hooks for GUI Git clients |

Plus, from `review-profiles.md`:

| Setting path | Type | Use for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `review_default_profile` | string | The default review profile name |
| `review_profiles` | object | Named review profiles and their reviewer, judge, output, task, model, and slot settings |

**NOT IN DOCS:** the allowed values of `commit_linking` beyond the default `"prompt"`; the inner schema of `review_profiles`. On the latter the docs say only: "The profile JSON can contain nested reviewer and model settings, so the CLI is the safest way to keep the shape valid."

### 2.3 Local settings — `.entire/settings.local.json`

"Local settings have the highest priority and are used for personal preferences, private rules, machine-specific paths, and temporary debugging."

Example given:

```json
{
  "log_level": "debug",
  "telemetry": false
}
```

The local-settings reference table lists the same paths *minus* `enabled` and `strategy_options.summarize.enabled`: `commit_linking`, `strategy_options.push_sessions`, `strategy_options.checkpoint_remote`, `redaction.pii.enabled`, `redaction.custom_redactions`, `telemetry`, `sign_checkpoint_commits`, `log_level`, `external_agents`, `absolute_git_hook_path`. (Local review profiles are also supported per `review-profiles.md`.) No defaults column is given for the local table.

Nesting rule, verbatim: "Top-level settings are written directly" / "Dotted setting paths are written as nested objects":

```json
{
  "redaction": {
    "custom_redactions": {
      "private_codename": "PROJECT_[A-Z]{4}"
    }
  }
}
```

### 2.4 Global settings — `~/.config/entire/settings.json`

| Setting path | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `telemetry` | boolean | `false` | Default telemetry setting for new repositories |

That single row is the entire documented global schema. Note the default differs from the project layer (`false` global vs `null` project).

Creating it:

```bash
mkdir -p ~/.config/entire
vim ~/.config/entire/settings.json
```

```json
{
  "telemetry": false
}
```

Important: "`entire configure` updates repository settings. There is no global flag for `entire configure`, so edit `~/.config/entire/settings.json` directly."

"Global settings apply only when the same setting is not set in `.entire/settings.json` or `.entire/settings.local.json`."

### 2.5 CLI writes

```bash
entire configure --local --telemetry=false
entire configure --local --skip-push-sessions
entire configure --local --absolute-git-hook-path
```

```bash
entire configure --project --checkpoint-remote github:owner/repo
entire configure --project --skip-push-sessions
entire configure --project --telemetry=false
```

"If a setting does not have a CLI flag, edit `.entire/settings.local.json` [or `.entire/settings.json`] directly."

### 2.6 Checkpoint storage specifics

Effective default (checkpoints on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch of the code repo, no config needed):

```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "push_sessions": true
  }
}
```

Separate checkpoint repo:

```bash
entire configure --checkpoint-remote github:owner/repo
```

writes to `.entire/settings.json`:

```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "checkpoint_remote": {
      "provider": "github",
      "repo": "owner/repo"
    }
  }
}
```

&gt; Note: Currently, `github` is the supported checkpoint remote provider.

Owner constraint, verbatim: "Use a checkpoint repository with the same owner or organization as your code repository." Same owner → "checkpoints push normally" (example: code at `github.com/acme/app`, checkpoints at `github:acme/app-checkpoints`). Different owner → "Entire won't push checkpoint data to that repository." Rationale: "The different-owner case protects fork workflows, so contributors do not need write access to the upstream project's checkpoint repository."

Local-only:

```bash
entire configure --local --skip-push-sessions
```

```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "push_sessions": false
  }
}
```

Project-wide variant: `entire configure --project --skip-push-sessions`.

Summaries:

```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "summarize": {
      "enabled": true
    }
  }
}
```

```bash
entire configure --summarize-provider PROVIDER
entire configure --summarize-model MODEL
```

**NOT IN DOCS:** which providers/models are valid for `--summarize-provider` / `--summarize-model`, and the settings keys those flags write.

---

## 3. Git hooks

What they do, verbatim:

&gt; * `git commit` links captured agent work to the commit and creates an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.
&gt; * `git push` syncs checkpoint data after your code push.

Install / reinstall:

```bash
entire enable
```

```bash
entire configure --force
```

("If hooks were removed, overwritten, or stopped running, reinstall them.")

GUI clients (Xcode, Tower, GitKraken, Sourcetree "may not load your shell `$PATH`"):

```bash
entire configure --local --absolute-git-hook-path
entire enable --local --absolute-git-hook-path
```

Effect on the hook body — the only glimpse of actual hook content in the docs:

```sh
# Default
entire hooks git post-commit

# With --absolute-git-hook-path
'/Users/you/go/bin/entire' hooks git post-commit
```

Persisted as:

```json
{
  "absolute_git_hook_path": true
}
```

"Absolute Git hook paths are machine-specific. If you move the `entire` binary, rerun: `entire configure --local --absolute-git-hook-path`."

Non-interactive:

```bash
entire enable --yes
entire enable --yes --agent claude-code
```

Verify: `entire status --detailed`, then a test commit and push.

Distinction (repeated on both pages, in a Note):

&gt; Git hooks are different from agent hooks. Agent hooks capture the AI session while you work; Git hooks connect that captured session to commits and pushes.

**NOT IN DOCS — three explicit gaps here:**
1. The docs never list the hook filenames installed in `.git/hooks`. Only `post-commit` is ever named, and only inside the `--absolute-git-hook-path` example. A `pre-push`/`post-push`-style hook is implied by "`git push` syncs checkpoint data," but no hook name is stated. Do not assume names.
2. The docs never state "git hooks are never shared through git" nor explain why. They only say hooks are installed per-repository by `entire enable` and that `absolute_git_hook_path` is "machine-specific." (`.git/hooks` being outside version control is general Git knowledge, not a claim these pages make.)
3. No hook script source is shown beyond the single `entire hooks git post-commit` line.

---

## 4. Agent hooks

What agent hooks enable capture of, verbatim:

&gt; * prompts and transcripts
&gt; * tool activity
&gt; * changed files
&gt; * token usage, when available
&gt; * session start and session end events

Commands:

```bash
entire agent add AGENT_NAME
```

```bash
entire agent add codex
entire agent add claude-code
```

```bash
entire enable --agent codex
```

Reinstall a stale or hand-edited hook:

```bash
entire agent add AGENT_NAME --force
```

### Agent hook file locations (verbatim table)

| Agent | Hook location | Format |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Claude Code | `.claude/settings.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Codex | `.codex/hooks.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Copilot CLI | `.github/hooks/entire.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Cursor | `.cursor/hooks.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Gemini CLI | `.gemini/settings.json` | JSON hooks config |
| OpenCode | `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` | TypeScript plugin |
| Factory | `.factory/settings.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Pi | `.pi/extensions/entire/index.ts` | TypeScript extension |

"You can enable multiple agents at the same time. Each agent's hooks are independent."

External agents:

```json
{
  "external_agents": true
}
```

"Entire looks for executables named `entire-agent-&lt;name&gt;`." Protocol/security details are deferred to `/agents/external-agent-plugins/overview`.

Verify:

```bash
entire agent list
entire status --detailed
```

"Then start a fresh session with the configured agent from inside the repository. If the agent was already running before hooks were installed, restart it."

**NOT IN DOCS — significant gap for your question 4:** these pages never show what Entire writes *inside* `.claude/settings.json`. No hook event names (no `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`/`SessionStart`/`Stop`, etc.), no hook command strings, no matcher syntax, no JSON snippet. The capture list above ("session start and session end events", "tool activity") describes capability, not event names. Agent-specific detail is deferred to `/agents/overview`, which was not in the fetch list — fetch that page if you need the actual `.claude/settings.json` payload.

## 5. Privacy and redaction

### Always-on baseline

&gt; By default, Entire redacts common secrets such as API keys, access tokens, credentialed URLs, database connection strings, and private keys before checkpoint metadata is stored.

### PII redaction (opt-in, default off)

Project:

```json
{
  "redaction": {
    "pii": {
      "enabled": true,
      "email": true,
      "phone": true,
      "address": false
    }
  }
}
```

Local:

```json
{
  "redaction": {
    "pii": {
      "enabled": true
    }
  }
}
```

Note the sub-keys `email`, `phone`, `address` appear only in the project example; the settings tables list just `redaction.pii.enabled`. Their defaults are **NOT IN DOCS** on this page — deferred to `/security#pii-redaction-optional` for "categories, defaults, and limitations."

### User-defined redaction

Inline rules (map of rule name → regex; matches replaced with `REDACTED` per the settings table):

```json
{
  "redaction": {
    "custom_redactions": {
      "acme_token": "ACME_TOKEN_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}",
      "internal_id": "INTERNAL_[a-z]{6}_[0-9]{4}"
    }
  }
}
```

"Put shared rules in `.entire/settings.json`. Put private rules in `.entire/settings.local.json`."

Rule packs, for "larger reusable sets": shared packs go in `.entire/redactors/`, personal packs in `.entire/redactors/local/`.

**NOT IN DOCS:** rule pack file format, filename convention, and supported regex flavor — explicitly deferred to `/security#user-defined-redaction` for "rule pack format, supported regex behavior, and validation details."

Failure mode, verbatim: "Invalid regexes or malformed rule packs are skipped and written to the logs instead of stopping the CLI."

### Telemetry

"Telemetry controls anonymous CLI usage analytics. It does not send prompts, transcripts, source code, file paths, repository names, or flag values."

```json
{
  "telemetry": false
}
```

```bash
ENTIRE_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT=1 entire status
```

Local file variant supported. Project default `null`; global default `false`.

### Checkpoint commit signing

"Checkpoint commits are signed by default when your Git signing setup is available."

```json
{
  "sign_checkpoint_commits": false
}
```

Details deferred to `/security#checkpoint-commit-signing` for "requirements and best-effort behavior."

---

## 6. Logging and diagnostics

Log location: "Entire writes local diagnostic logs under `.entire/logs/`." "Keep logs local unless someone asks for a specific file or bundle while troubleshooting."

`log_level`: default `info`; supported values `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error`.

Three ways to set it, in precedence order:

```bash
ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL=debug entire status
```
&gt; `ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL` takes precedence over settings files.

```json
{
  "log_level": "debug"
}
```
(in `.entire/settings.local.json` for machine-specific, or `.entire/settings.json` "only when the whole repository should use the same log level")

Diagnostic commands, verbatim:

```bash
entire status --detailed     # inspect active settings; shows which settings file supplied a value
entire doctor                # repository health check
entire doctor logs           # show recent operational logs
entire doctor logs --follow  # stream new log lines
entire doctor bundle         # create diagnostic bundle
entire doctor trace          # hook performance trace
```

- `entire status --detailed` — "confirm Entire is reading the value you changed and to see which settings file supplied it." This is the verification command recommended on every single page.
- `entire doctor` — "when sessions look stuck, Git hooks are not firing, checkpoint data is not syncing, or `entire status` shows warnings. It checks repository setup, hook state, checkpoint metadata, and common session problems."
- `entire doctor bundle` — "Diagnostic bundles are redacted by default. Use `--raw` only when support has asked for an unredacted bundle."
- `entire doctor trace` — "Hook performance traces are only available at debug log level. Turn on debug logging, reproduce the slow Git command, then run `entire doctor trace`."

Documented environment variables across all nine pages: exactly two — `ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL` and `ENTIRE_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT`.

---

## 7. Review profiles (bonus page)

```bash
entire review --configure                       # interactive; saves to .entire/settings.json by default
entire review --configure \
  --set-agents codex,claude-code \
  --set-judge codex \
  --set-output local                            # non-interactive
entire review --configure --local               # saves to .entire/settings.local.json
entire review --edit
entire review --list                            # list profiles
entire review --agents                          # reviewers for the selected profile
entire review general                           # run a named profile
```

Keys: `review_default_profile` (string), `review_profiles` (object). "Use `entire review --configure` or `entire review --edit` to update review profiles when possible."

Troubleshooting note: "If the review uses the wrong agents or output mode, check whether a local profile in `.entire/settings.local.json` is overriding the shared profile in `.entire/settings.json`."

**NOT IN DOCS:** allowed values for `--set-output` beyond the `local` example; the nested shape of a profile object.

3. The JSON payload Entire writes into `.claude/settings.json` — no hook event names, no hook commands, no matchers. Deferred to `/agents/overview`.
4. Contents of `.entire/.gitignore`.
5. Allowed values of `commit_linking`.
6. Redaction rule pack format, filenames, regex flavor, validation rules — deferred to `/security`.

# Entire CLI — SESSIONS Reference

**Primary sources (the 9 assigned guide pages):** `docs.entire.io/guides/sessions/{overview, sessions-in-entire-io, list-sessions, inspect-sessions-in-cli, stop-sessions, attach-sessions, resume-sessions, import-past-agent-history, troubleshooting}.md`

**Supplementary sources (fetched to answer questions the 9 pages do not cover; marked `[REF]` throughout):** `/cli-reference/sessions.md`, `/cli-reference/attach.md`, `/cli-reference/resume.md`, `/cli-reference/import.md`, `/cli-reference/doctor.md`, `/glossary.md`

---

## 1. Session vs. Checkpoint, and lifecycle

### Session — definition (verbatim, overview.md)

&gt; A session is the complete record of an AI coding interaction: the prompts, responses, tool calls, file changes, metadata, token usage, and checkpoint links Entire captured while the agent worked.

&gt; Sessions show what the agent did, why it did it, and how the work came together.

**What sessions capture** (overview.md, verbatim list):
- prompts and agent responses
- tool calls, shell commands, file reads, and file edits
- files created, modified, or deleted during the session
- agent, model, worktree, timing, and token metadata
- checkpoints and commits linked to the session
- line attribution showing how much of a change came from the agent versus a human

### Checkpoint — definition `[REF glossary.md]`

&gt; **Checkpoint**: A save point for your work. Entire creates checkpoints around agent activity and links committed checkpoints to Git commits.

&gt; **Committed checkpoint**: A checkpoint permanently linked to a Git commit and stored on the checkpoints branch.

&gt; **Checkpoint linking**: The connection between a Git commit and the Entire checkpoint that captured the session context behind it.

&gt; **Session** (glossary): A complete interaction with an AI coding agent from start to finish. Sessions include prompts, responses, code changes, checkpoints, token usage, metadata, and line attribution.

### Session↔Checkpoint relationship (overview.md, table verbatim)

| Scenario | How it looks in Entire |
| --- | --- |
| One session, one checkpoint | Most common. You ask the agent to do something, it does the work, and you commit. |
| One checkpoint, multiple sessions | Several sessions contribute to the same change before you commit. |
| One session, multiple checkpoints | One longer session keeps going while you commit multiple checkpoints along the way. |
| Multiple sessions, multiple checkpoints | Multiple sessions and commits are linked across a larger or parallel piece of work. |

&gt; Entire links these records so you can move from code, to checkpoint, to the session context behind it.

### How sessions are created (overview.md, verbatim)

&gt; After you enable Entire for a repository, agent hooks capture session context while you work. When you commit, Entire can link the relevant session context to a checkpoint.

&gt; You can also attach a session later if capture started late, hooks did not fire, or the session was useful even though it did not produce file changes.

Also, from troubleshooting.md: "A normal committed checkpoint needs both captured session context and a Git commit."

### ⚠️ Lifecycle — what the docs actually state vs. do not

**The docs do NOT publish a formal session state machine.** No page in the sessions guide enumerates a `start → active → end → checkpoint` transition list. Do not present one as documented. What *is* documented:

- Two named phases exist, but only in `[REF doctor.md]`: **`ACTIVE`** and **`ENDED`**.
- `entire session list` shows "sessions tracked by Entire, **including ended sessions**" (list-sessions.md), implying ended is a persisted terminal state.
- `entire session stop` "**marks sessions as ended**" (stop-sessions.md warning) — this is the only documented explicit transition, and it explicitly **does not** create a checkpoint.
- Resume operates on "**stopped sessions**" (resume-sessions.md) — "stopped" is used as a session state in the picker.
- Storage transition `[REF glossary.md + doctor.md]`: **Shadow branch** = "A temporary local branch used to store checkpoints during active sessions. Shadow branches are named like `entire/&lt;sessionID&gt;-&lt;worktreeID&gt;` and are not pushed to remote repositories." **Checkpoints branch** = "The permanent Git branch where Entire stores checkpoint and session metadata. The branch is named `entire/checkpoints/v1`." `doctor`'s **Condense** action moves data from the former to the latter.
- **Nested session** `[REF glossary.md]`: "A child session created when an agent spawns another agent or sub-agent during the same body of work." Behavior (sessions-in-entire-io.md): "When an agent spawns a sub-agent, Entire can capture that child session separately and roll its context into the parent checkpoint."
- **Agent run** `[REF glossary.md]`: "One execution of an agent inside a session." So a session can contain multiple runs.

---

## 2. Every session subcommand with exact flags

Base form `[REF sessions.md]`:

```bash
entire session SUBCOMMAND
```

&gt; `entire session` is also available as `entire sessions`.

### `session current`
Show the most recently active session for the current worktree.

```bash
entire session current
entire session current --json
entire session current --transcript &gt; session.jsonl
```

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `--json` | Output as JSON |
| `--transcript` | Stream raw agent transcript bytes to stdout |

Guide notes: "the most relevant session for the current worktree" (list-sessions.md); "Transcript format depends on the agent." (inspect-sessions-in-cli.md)

### `session list`
List all sessions tracked by Entire, including ended sessions.

```bash
entire session list
entire session list --json
```

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `--json` | Output as JSON |

`[REF]`: "For active sessions only, use `entire status`."

### `session info`

```bash
entire session info SESSION_ID
entire session info SESSION_ID --json
entire session info SESSION_ID --transcript &gt; session.jsonl
```

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `--json` | Output as JSON |
| `--transcript` | Stream raw agent transcript bytes to stdout |

Shows (both guide and REF, near-identical wording): "the agent, model, status, worktree, timing, token usage, checkpoint links, and files touched."

### `session tokens`

```bash
entire session tokens
entire session tokens SESSION_ID
entire session tokens --agent-brief
```

`[REF]` signature: `entire session tokens [SESSION_ID] [flags]` — "When no session ID is provided, Entire reports on the most recently active session for the current worktree."

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `--agent-brief` | Output compact next-step guidance for agents |
| `--current` | Prefer the current worktree's most recent session |
| `--json` | Output as JSON |

Guide wording: "inspect token usage and optimization recommendations"; `--agent-brief` = "compact guidance that an agent can act on."

&gt; `--current` and `--json` for `tokens` appear **only** in the CLI reference, not in the nine guide pages.

### `session stop`

```bash
entire status
entire session stop SESSION_ID
entire session stop --all
entire session stop --all --force
```

`[REF]` signature: `entire session stop [SESSION_ID] [flags]`

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `--all` | Stop all active sessions |
| `-f, --force` | Skip confirmation prompt |

&gt; **Warning (stop-sessions.md, verbatim):** `entire session stop` marks sessions as ended. It does not create a checkpoint or flush pending work. Commit first when you want session context linked to code changes.

### `session attach`

```bash
entire session attach SESSION_ID --agent AGENT
entire session attach SESSION_ID --agent AGENT --force
entire session attach SESSION_ID --agent codex --review
entire session attach SESSION_ID --agent codex --review --skills review
```

`[REF attach.md]` signature: `entire session attach SESSION_ID [flags]`

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `-a, --agent NAME` | Agent that created the session. Defaults to `claude-code`. Run `entire agent list` to see registered agents, including external agents. |
| `-f, --force` | Skip confirmation and amend the last commit with the checkpoint trailer |
| `--review` | Tag the attached session as an agent review. |
| `--skills SKILL` | Declare which review skills were run. Repeatable; only used with `--review`. |

**When to attach** (attach-sessions.md, verbatim bullets):
- hooks were not installed when the session started
- hooks did not fire
- a research session should be linked to the latest commit
- a completed agent review should be preserved as review context

**Behavior:** "Attach reads an existing agent transcript, creates or updates a checkpoint, and links that context to the last commit. If the last commit already has an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer, the session is added to that checkpoint. Otherwise, Entire creates a checkpoint and can amend the last commit with the trailer."

&gt; **Note `[REF attach.md]`:** `entire session attach` requires the repository to already have at least one commit. If Entire cannot find the transcript for the agent you specify, it will try to auto-detect the correct agent from known transcript locations.

`[REF]` examples, verbatim:

```bash
# Attach a Codex session and amend HEAD automatically
entire session attach 019d6c43-1537-7343-9691-1f8cee04fe59 -a codex -f

# Attach a Claude Code session using the default agent
entire session attach 9b1deb4d-3b7d-4bad-9bdd-2b0d7b3dcbfe

# Attach a Codex session as a review
entire session attach 019d6c43-1537-7343-9691-1f8cee04fe59 -a codex --review
```

### `session adopt`

Only appearance in the nine guides is troubleshooting.md:

```bash
entire session adopt SESSION_ID --from ../source-worktree
```

`[REF sessions.md]` signature: `entire session adopt [SESSION_ID] --from SOURCE_WORKTREE [flags]` — "Adopt an active session from another worktree into the current repository. Use this when an agent starts in one repository or worktree, then moves and makes changes in another."

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `--from PATH` | Source worktree that already tracks the session |
| `--force` | Replace an existing local state file for the same session |
| `--yes` | Confirm same-store adoption and replacement without prompting |

Guide note: "After adoption, the next commit can link to the session normally."

### `session resume`

```bash
entire session resume
entire session resume BRANCH
entire session resume BRANCH --force
```

`[REF resume.md]` signature: `entire session resume [BRANCH] [flags]`

| Argument | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `branch` | Optional branch to resume the agent session from |

| Flag | Description `[REF]` |
| --- | --- |
| `-f, --force` | Resume from older checkpoint without confirmation |

**Picker mode (no argument):** "opens an interactive picker of stopped sessions across all worktrees. After you choose a session, Entire checks out the related branch, restores the session log if needed, and asks whether to start the agent."

**Branch mode — exact numbered steps `[REF resume.md]`:**
1. Checks out the specified branch
2. Finds the session ID from commits unique to this branch
3. Restores the session log if it does not exist locally
4. Shows or starts the command to resume the agent session

(Guide wording: "Entire finds the session ID from checkpoint-linked commits on that branch.")

&gt; **Note `[REF]`:** If the branch doesn't exist locally but exists on origin, you'll be prompted to fetch it.

&gt; **Warning (resume-sessions.md, verbatim):** If newer commits without checkpoints exist on the branch, resume can reset Git state to the most recent commit with a checkpoint. Entire prompts before doing this unless you force it.

&gt; **Warning `[REF]`, fuller:** If newer commits without checkpoints exist on the branch (e.g., after merging main or cherry-picking), this operation will reset your Git status to the most recent commit with a checkpoint. You'll be prompted to confirm.

### Finding a session ID (list-sessions.md)

```bash
entire session info SESSION_ID
entire session stop SESSION_ID
entire session tokens SESSION_ID
```

&gt; Use `entire status`, `entire session current`, or `entire session list` when you need to copy that ID.

---

## 3. Importing past agent history (predating Entire)

Command family is **`entire import AGENT`** — not a `session` subcommand.

**When to use** (import-past-agent-history.md, verbatim bullets):
- you enabled Entire after useful agent work already happened
- you want past local agent sessions to become searchable in Entire
- you want to import sessions from an agent's local transcript store

| If you want to | Use |
| --- | --- |
| Bring past local agent sessions into Entire | `entire import AGENT` |
| Link one missed session to the latest commit | `entire session attach` |
| Continue working from an earlier session | `entire session resume` |

### Commands

```bash
entire import claude-code --dry-run
entire import claude-code
entire import claude-code --session SESSION_ID
entire import claude-code --path TRANSCRIPT_DIRECTORY
```

- Run import **from inside the repository** whose agent history you want to bring in.
- Replace `claude-code` with the agent you want to import from.
- `--dry-run` preview "shows how many sessions and turns Entire found, what would be imported, and what was already imported."
- "You can repeat `--session` to import more than one session."
- "Run `entire import --help` or see the import command reference for supported agents and flags."

| Flag `[REF import.md]` | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--dry-run` | Report what would be imported without writing checkpoints |
| `--path DIRECTORY` | Override the transcript directory to import from |
| `--session ID` | Import only this session ID. Repeatable. |

### Supported agents `[REF import.md]` — "In Entire CLI `0.7.8`, import supports:"

| Agent | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Claude Code | `entire import claude-code` |
| Codex | `entire import codex` |
| Copilot CLI | `entire import copilot-cli` |
| Cursor | `entire import cursor` |
| Factory | `entire import factoryai-droid` |
| Gemini CLI | `entire import gemini` |
| Pi | `entire import pi` |

### Limits — the critical ones

**⚠️ Time window (only stated in `[REF import.md]`, NOT in the guide page):**

&gt; `entire import` scans the agent's local transcript store for the current repository and imports **history from the past month**.

**⚠️ Experimental status `[REF import.md]`:**

&gt; `entire import` is experimental and hidden from the top-level help in Entire CLI `0.7.8`. Run `entire import --help` to see the agents supported by your installed CLI version.

**Imported checkpoints are (guide page, verbatim):**
- searchable
- explainable
- idempotent, so re-running import skips turns that already exist
- **not linked to a Git commit**
- **not rewindable**

Plus: "Imported sessions are stored as read-only checkpoints on Entire's checkpoint metadata branch." `[REF]` adds **read-only** as an explicit first bullet.

---

## 4. Stuck / orphaned sessions

**Terminology caveat:** the docs never use the word "orphaned." The nine guides use "**stale**"; the CLI reference uses "**stuck**." Treat them as the documented vocabulary.

### How they happen
- Guides state only the situational cause: "Stop a session when Entire still considers it active, but the agent run is finished or no longer relevant." (stop-sessions.md)
- Wrong-worktree case: "If an agent started in one worktree and continued in another…" (troubleshooting.md)

### Detection — exact criteria `[REF doctor.md]`

&gt; A session is considered stuck if:
&gt; * It is in ACTIVE phase with no interaction for over 1 hour
&gt; * It is in ENDED phase with uncondensed checkpoint data on a shadow branch

Detection commands:

```bash
entire status          # find active and stale sessions
entire doctor          # scan for stuck sessions and offer fixes
```

### Clearing

```bash
entire session stop SESSION_ID
entire session stop --all
entire session stop --all --force
entire doctor
```

`entire doctor` flags and choices `[REF doctor.md]`:

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `-f, --force` | Fix all issues without prompting (condense if possible, otherwise discard) |

&gt; For each stuck session, you can choose to:
&gt; * **Condense**: Save session data to permanent storage (`entire/checkpoints/v1` branch)
&gt; * **Discard**: Remove the session state and shadow branch data
&gt; * **Skip**: Leave the session as-is

Guide framing: "If status suggests a repository health issue, run: `entire doctor`."

Wrong-worktree fix:

```bash
entire session adopt SESSION_ID --from ../source-worktree
```

**Not documented:** no page states an automatic timeout that ends sessions on its own, nor any retention/GC policy for stale session state beyond `doctor`'s manual condense/discard.

---

## 5. Troubleshooting — symptom → cause → fix (verbatim from troubleshooting.md)

### No Active Session Appears
Check repository and hook status:
```bash
entire status
```
If Entire is not enabled, start with:
```bash
entire enable --agent AGENT
```
If Entire is already enabled but the agent is not configured, add it:
```bash
entire agent add AGENT
```
&gt; Then start your agent from inside the enabled repository. Agent hooks only capture work for agents that are configured for that repo.

### The Session Did Not Link to a Commit
&gt; A normal committed checkpoint needs both captured session context and a Git commit.

If the session exists but was not linked, attach it to the latest commit:
```bash
entire session attach SESSION_ID --agent AGENT
```
&gt; If the commit already has a checkpoint, Entire adds the session to that checkpoint. Otherwise, Entire can create a new checkpoint and amend the commit with an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.

### The Session Is Attached to the Wrong Worktree
&gt; If an agent started in one worktree and continued in another, adopt the session into the current repository:
```bash
entire session adopt SESSION_ID --from ../source-worktree
```
&gt; After adoption, the next commit can link to the session normally.

### Stale Sessions Keep Showing Up
&gt; Use `entire status` to find active and stale sessions. If a session is no longer running, stop it:
```bash
entire session stop SESSION_ID
```
For multiple stale sessions:
```bash
entire session stop --all
```
If status suggests a repository health issue, run:
```bash
entire doctor
```

### Token Usage Is Missing
&gt; Token usage depends on what the agent exposes and when the session data is available. Some agents only expose full token usage after the session ends.

Check the session details first:
```bash
entire session info SESSION_ID
```
Then check token usage:
```bash
entire session tokens SESSION_ID
```

### The Raw Transcript Is Missing
&gt; Entire reads transcript data from the agent's session storage. If the agent deleted or moved its local transcript, Entire may still have metadata but not the raw transcript bytes.

Try:
```bash
entire session info SESSION_ID --json
```
&gt; If metadata exists but the transcript cannot be streamed, inspect the relevant agent guide (`/agents/overview`) for that agent's transcript requirements.

---

## Appendix A — Reviewing sessions in Entire.io (sessions-in-entire-io.md)

- **Open a session:** "Open the checkpoint that contains the session you want to inspect, then use the session list to choose the agent work behind that checkpoint." Each session row shows **the agent, step count, opening prompt, and relative timestamp**.
- **Timeline:** "shows the conversation in order, including prompts, agent responses, expandable tool calls, and runtime tags. The metadata panel summarizes the agent, model, duration, and token usage when available." Filters: "prompts, responses, intermediate steps, checkpoints, or specific tool-call types."
- **Files and attribution:** "Files modified during a session are reviewed through the checkpoint that owns the session. When a checkpoint includes multiple sessions, the diff shows the combined change, while session details preserve the per-session context." Attribution breakdown can include: agent-written lines; human-added, modified, or removed lines; agent percentage for the checkpoint or session.
- **Sub-agent sessions:** captured separately, context rolled into the parent checkpoint; each sub-agent has its own transcript, tool calls, and token usage.

## Appendix B — Named artifacts and paths appearing in these docs

| Item | Value | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Permanent checkpoints branch | `entire/checkpoints/v1` | `[REF doctor.md]`, `[REF glossary.md]` |
| Shadow branch naming | `entire/&lt;sessionID&gt;-&lt;worktreeID&gt;` (local only, never pushed) | `[REF glossary.md]` |
| Commit trailers | `Entire-Checkpoint`, `Entire-Attribution` | attach/troubleshooting pages; `[REF glossary.md]` |
| Checkpoint ID format | 12-character hex, e.g. `8a513f56ed70` | `[REF glossary.md]` |
| Transcript export file used in examples | `session.jsonl` | inspect-sessions-in-cli.md |
| Settings layers (from the Configuration overview page I hit via a cache mixup) | `.entire/settings.local.json` → `.entire/settings.json` → `~/.config/entire/settings.json` → defaults | `/guides/configuration/overview.md` |
| Debug logging | `ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` or `"log_level": "DEBUG"` in `.entire/settings.json` | `[REF doctor.md]` |
| Docs index | `https://docs.entire.io/llms.txt` | every page header |

---

## Appendix C — Explicitly NOT answered by these docs

1. **No formal session lifecycle state machine.** Only `ACTIVE` and `ENDED` are named (in `doctor.md`), plus informal "stopped"/"stale". No documented transition diagram, no list of all states, no rules for what moves a session from active to ended other than `session stop` and (implicitly) the agent finishing.
2. **No documented auto-expiry.** The 1-hour figure is a *stuck-detection threshold* for `doctor`, not an automatic end.
3. **No default transcript paths.** `--path` exists but no page states where each agent's transcripts live by default; the docs redirect to per-agent guides under `/agents/`.
4. **No `session info` / `session current` flags beyond `--json` and `--transcript`.**
5. **No `session delete`/`purge` command in the sessions reference.** A separate `entire clean` ("Clean up Entire session data") is listed in `llms.txt` but was not part of this research scope and is not referenced by any of the nine session pages.
6. **`entire import` time window and experimental status are absent from the guide page** — they appear only in the CLI reference. If your team relies on the guide alone, they will not know about the one-month scan limit.
7. **No stated maximum session count, transcript size limit, or retention period.**
8. **Version scoping:** the import agent list and experimental note are pinned to Entire CLI `0.7.8`. No other page states a version.</result>
