# Entire CLI — Checkpoints Reference (sourced verbatim from docs.entire.io)

Sources fetched: the 10 `guides/checkpoints/*` pages, plus `guides/configuration/checkpoint-storage.md`, `guides/configuration/git-hooks.md`, `cli-reference/checkpoint.md`, `glossary.md`, `security.md`, `cli/faq.md` (the last six only where noted, to attempt the ref/refspec questions).

---

## 1. What a checkpoint is

From `guides/checkpoints/overview.md`:

&gt; "A checkpoint is an addressable record of the context behind an AI-assisted change. After you [enable Entire](/quickstart) in a repository, the [CLI](/platforms/cli) uses Git hooks and [agent hooks](/agents/overview) to capture session data as you work. When you or your agent commit, Entire saves that captured context as a checkpoint and links it to the commit."

Why they matter (verbatim bullets):
* Review agent work without guessing what happened
* Trace a commit back to the prompt, transcript, and session that produced it
* Understand what changed, why it changed, and who edited what
* Search past agent work later

How checkpoints connect to Git:

&gt; "Each checkpoint has a 12-character ID. Entire adds that ID to the commit message with an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer and stores checkpoint metadata on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch.
&gt; Your normal Git history stays clean. Entire does not add extra commits to your working branch."

What a checkpoint stores (verbatim bullets):
* the linked Git commit
* the agent session or sessions behind the change
* prompts and transcript
* tool activity
* changed files
* token usage, when available
* AI attribution for the changed lines in the checkpoint

Glossary definitions (from `glossary.md`):
* **Checkpoint** — "A save point for your work. Entire creates checkpoints around agent activity and links committed checkpoints to Git commits."
* **Checkpoint ID** — "A 12-character hexadecimal identifier for a checkpoint, such as `8a513f56ed70`."
* **Checkpoint linking** — "The connection between a Git commit and the Entire checkpoint that captured the session context behind it."
* **Checkpoint remote** — "A Git remote used to store checkpoint metadata separately from your code remote. By default, Entire pushes checkpoint metadata to your code remote."
* **Checkpoints branch** — "The permanent Git branch where Entire stores checkpoint and session metadata. The branch is named `entire/checkpoints/v1`."
* **Entire-Checkpoint** — "A commit trailer that records the checkpoint ID associated with a commit."

---

## 2. Question 1 — Exact conditions for capture; what is skipped and why

From `guides/checkpoints/capture-checkpoints.md`.

### The two-part mechanism

&gt; "Checkpoint capture has two parts:
&gt; 1. Agent hooks collect session context, including prompts, transcript, tool activity, changed files, and token usage when available.
&gt; 2. Git hooks link that captured context to a commit by adding an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.
&gt;
&gt; After the commit, Entire stores checkpoint metadata on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch. Your working branch stays clean."

### Required conditions

&gt; "Entire needs captured session context and a Git commit to create a normal committed checkpoint."

### What does NOT create a checkpoint (verbatim list)

&gt; "A checkpoint may not appear when:
&gt; * Entire has not been enabled in the repository
&gt; * agent hooks were not installed for the agent you used
&gt; * the agent did not change files in the repository
&gt; * no commit was created
&gt; * a GUI Git client could not find the `entire` command from its Git hooks"

Note the doc's own hedge: it says "may not appear," not "is skipped." The docs do **not** state a rule-based skip list (e.g. size thresholds, ignored paths, empty commits) beyond the five bullets above.

### Setup for capture

New repository, enable with the agent you use:
```bash
entire enable --agent AGENT
```
Repository where Entire is already enabled, add another agent:
```bash
entire agent add AGENT
```
List supported built-in agents:
```bash
entire agent list
```

### Commit flow (verbatim numbered steps)

&gt; 1. Start your agent from an Entire-enabled repository.
&gt; 2. Ask the agent to make a change.
&gt; 3. Stage and commit the change with Git.

```bash
git add .
git commit -m "Describe the change"
```

&gt; "If Entire detects recent agent work, it may ask whether to link the commit to the session context. Choose `yes` to create the checkpoint. Choose `always` if you want Entire to remember that preference for future matching commits."

```
Entire: Active Codex session detected
  Last prompt: Add a user settings page...

Link this commit to session context?
  [Y]es / [n]o / [a]lways (remember my choice):
```

### Automatic linking of future commits

&gt; "When the 'Link this commit to session context?' prompt appears during `git commit`, choosing `yes` or `no` only applies to that one commit.
&gt; To link future matching commits in this repository without being prompted during `git commit`, set `commit_linking` to `always`.
&gt; Set `commit_linking` in one of these files:
&gt; * `.entire/settings.local.json` if this preference should only apply to you
&gt; * `.entire/settings.json` if this should be the team setting for the repository"

```json
{
  "commit_linking": "always"
}
```

Note: `commit_linking` is a **top-level** key, not under `strategy_options`.

### Agent-authored commits

&gt; "Your agent can also create commits for you. ... Entire still uses the Git hooks to link the commit to the captured session context. If the agent makes several commits in one session, each commit gets its own checkpoint."

### Cardinality rules

* **Multiple commits from one session:** "A single agent session can produce multiple checkpoints. This happens when a long session creates several commits, or when you split the agent's work into smaller commits yourself. Each commit gets its own checkpoint ID. The checkpoints can share the same session context..."
* **One checkpoint from multiple sessions:** "One checkpoint can also include more than one session. This happens when several agent sessions contribute to the work before you make a single commit. In Entire.io, those sessions appear together inside the checkpoint's Sessions tab. The checkpoint diff shows the combined change, while session details show the context behind each contribution."

### Supporting detail (from `guides/configuration/git-hooks.md`)

&gt; "* `git commit` links captured agent work to the commit and creates an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.
&gt; * `git push` syncs checkpoint data after your code push."

---

## 3. Question 2 — Storage modes and exact settings keys/values

From `guides/checkpoints/store-checkpoint-data.md`:

&gt; "Checkpoint storage involves two choices:
&gt; * **How checkpoint data is organized inside Git:** on one shared branch or in one Git ref per checkpoint.
&gt; * **Where checkpoint data is synchronized:** with your code, in a separate repository, or nowhere outside your machine."

These are **two orthogonal axes**. The backend (branch vs refs) is separate from the destination (code repo / separate repo / local-only).

### Axis A — Checkpoint backend (`--checkpoint-backend`)

From `store-checkpoints-with-your-code.md`, verbatim table:

| Backend | How it stores checkpoints | Checkpoint ID |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Branch-based (default)** | Stores all checkpoints on the shared `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch | 12-character hexadecimal ID, such as `a3b2c4d5e6f7` |
| **Ref-based** | Stores each checkpoint in its own Git ref | 26-character, time-sortable ID, such as `01KVBJCWYA4YW6J5M9GP655HZN` |

Branch-based:
&gt; "Branch-based storage requires no additional configuration. Entire writes checkpoint data to `entire/checkpoints/v1` and pushes that branch alongside your code."
```bash
entire configure --checkpoint-backend branch
```

Ref-based:
&gt; "Ref-based storage lets Entire push and fetch checkpoints independently instead of updating one shared branch. This keeps checkpoint operations fast as agent history grows and lets multiple agents save checkpoints at the same time without competing to update the same branch."

New repository:
```bash
entire enable --checkpoint-backend refs
```
Existing Entire-enabled repository:
```bash
entire configure --checkpoint-backend refs
```

&gt; "Changing the backend affects new checkpoints. Existing checkpoints remain on `entire/checkpoints/v1` and continue to work alongside new ref-based checkpoints. Your workflow and Entire commands stay the same."

Note block: "Add `--local` to a configuration command when the backend choice should apply only to your clone."

**DOCS GAP:** The docs never show the `.entire/settings.json` key that `--checkpoint-backend` writes. Only the CLI flag is documented. Do not guess a key name.

### Axis B — Destination / sync

**B1. Store with your code (default).** From `guides/configuration/checkpoint-storage.md`, the effective default:
```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "push_sessions": true
  }
}
```
&gt; "By default, Entire stores checkpoint data in the same Git repository as your code on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch. No configuration is required for the default setup."

**B2. Separate repository (checkpoint remote).** From `store-checkpoints-in-another-repo.md`:
&gt; "Create a separate, empty repository for checkpoint data, then run this from your project:"
```bash
entire configure --checkpoint-remote github:owner/repo
```
&gt; "The value uses the format `provider:owner/repo`. Currently, `github` is the supported provider."

Writes to `.entire/settings.json`:
```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "checkpoint_remote": {
      "provider": "github",
      "repo": "owner/repo"
    }
  }
}
```
&gt; "Commit and push `.entire/settings.json` when the checkpoint remote should apply to the whole project. Entire.io uses that setting to locate checkpoint data."

When to use it (verbatim):
* checkpoint data should follow a different sharing boundary than source code
* your team keeps development metadata outside the source repository
* you want to keep the source repository focused on source code

Push behavior:
&gt; "When you push code, Entire pushes the configured checkpoint data to the checkpoint repository separately. It uses the same protocol as your Git remote, such as SSH or HTTPS.
&gt; If the checkpoint repository cannot be reached, your main Git push still succeeds. Entire keeps the checkpoint data locally and warns that it could not sync."

Owner rule:
&gt; "* **Same owner** — checkpoints push normally. If your code is at `github.com/acme/app`, a checkpoint repo like `github:acme/app-checkpoints` works because both are owned by `acme`.
&gt; * **Different owner** — Entire won't push checkpoint data to that repository.
&gt; The different-owner case is an intentional design to protect forks, keeping fork contributors from needing write access to the upstream project's checkpoint repository."

**B3. Local-only.** From `keep-checkpoints-local.md`:
```bash
entire configure --local --skip-push-sessions
```
&gt; "Entire will still capture checkpoint data on your machine, but it will not push that data to GitHub or another remote.
&gt; This is useful for local experiments or repositories where you do not need checkpoint history available from another device. The tradeoff is that teammates cannot review local-only checkpoints unless you share them another way."

Writes `push_sessions: false` to `.entire/settings.local.json`:
```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "push_sessions": false
  }
}
```
For everyone in the repo:
```bash
entire configure --project --skip-push-sessions
```
&gt; "This writes the same setting to `.entire/settings.json`. Commit that file when the behavior should apply to the whole project."

**B4. Cloud.** **DOCS GAP:** There is no "cloud" storage mode for checkpoints. `security.md` states the opposite explicitly:
&gt; "Transcripts and checkpoint metadata go to a dedicated branch in **your** Git repository (`entire/checkpoints/v1`), not to a service Entire controls. The web app at entire.io reads from that branch — what users see in the web app is the *already-redacted* content from the branch."

### Bonus setting — auto-generated summaries

From `checkpoint-storage.md`:
```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "summarize": {
      "enabled": true
    }
  }
}
```
```bash
entire configure --summarize-provider PROVIDER
entire configure --summarize-model MODEL
```

### Where settings land

&gt; "Storage settings are written to `.entire/settings.json` by default, so they can be shared with the repository.
&gt; For personal preferences that should not affect the rest of the team, use the `--local` flag to write to `.entire/settings.local.json` instead.
&gt; Local settings apply only to your clone and take precedence over project settings."

### Verify storage config

```bash
entire status --detailed
```
Check effective settings for: `strategy_options.push_sessions`, `strategy_options.checkpoint_remote`, `strategy_options.summarize.enabled`. Then:
```bash
git push
```
&gt; "* **Source code repository**: Entire pushes checkpoint data to the same remote as your source code.
&gt; * **Separate checkpoint repo**: Entire pushes checkpoint data to the configured `checkpoint_remote`.
&gt; * **Local-only checkpoints**: Entire does not push checkpoint data because `push_sessions` is `false`."

---

## 4. Question 3 — Exact git refs and branches; push/fetch; custom refspec

### What the docs DO state

* One branch name only: **`entire/checkpoints/v1`**. Described as "the permanent Git branch where Entire stores checkpoint and session metadata" and "a dedicated Git branch in your code repository."
* Ref-based backend: "Stores each checkpoint in its own Git ref" and "lets Entire push and fetch checkpoints independently instead of updating one shared branch."
* Push mechanism: Git hooks — "`git push` syncs checkpoint data after your code push" (`git-hooks.md`); "Entire writes checkpoint data to `entire/checkpoints/v1` and pushes that branch alongside your code"; with a checkpoint remote, "Entire pushes the configured checkpoint data to the checkpoint repository separately. It uses the same protocol as your Git remote, such as SSH or HTTPS."
* Push is gated by `strategy_options.push_sessions`.
* Verification command found in `learn/investigate-why-code-exists.md`: `git branch -r | grep 'entire/checkpoints/v1'` — "to confirm the remote checkpoint branch is present."
* `cli-reference/clean.md`: "The permanent `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch is preserved."
* `cli-reference/doctor.md`: "**Condense**: Save session data to permanent storage (`entire/checkpoints/v1` branch)".
* `cli/faq.md` explains why a trailer is used instead of git notes: "They are not pushed by normal Git pushes unless note refs are configured separately; They are attached to commit SHAs, which change during rebases, amends, and cherry-picks; Hosted Git platforms do not consistently support them..."

### DOCS GAPS — explicitly not answered

1. **The exact ref namespace for the ref-based backend is never given.** I grepped the entire fetched docs corpus (all pages listed in `llms.txt`) for `refs/` and `refspec`: **zero matches** anywhere. There is no documented `refs/entire/*` pattern. Do not assume one.
2. **No custom fetch refspec is documented.** The docs never mention `remote.&lt;name&gt;.fetch`, `git config --add remote.origin.fetch`, or any refspec configuration. Whether a custom fetch refspec is needed for ref-based checkpoints is **not addressed anywhere in the docs**.
3. **No documented explicit fetch command** for checkpoint data (no `entire fetch`, no documented `git fetch origin entire/checkpoints/v1`). The only mention of fetching is the sentence "push and fetch checkpoints independently."

---

## 5. Question 4 — How teammates see each other's checkpoints; per-clone/machine steps

### What the docs DO state

* The visibility path is Git push → branch → Entire.io: "Entire.io reads checkpoint data from the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch after it has been pushed." (troubleshooting)
* `security.md`: "Anyone with access to your repository can view the transcript branch. If your repository is public, that branch is public."
* Sharing is broken by local-only mode: "The tradeoff is that teammates cannot review local-only checkpoints unless you share them another way." (`keep-checkpoints-local.md`)
* For a separate checkpoint repo, the setting must be committed: "Commit and push `.entire/settings.json` when the checkpoint remote should apply to the whole project. Entire.io uses that setting to locate checkpoint data." Troubleshooting lists as a failure cause: "`.entire/settings.json` was not committed, so teammates or Entire.io cannot discover the setting."
* Entire.io also needs GitHub App access: "Confirm the Entire GitHub App can access the repository."
* Per-clone/per-machine settings: `.entire/settings.local.json` is **not committed** and applies only to your clone; `absolute_git_hook_path` is explicitly called out as machine-specific — "Absolute Git hook paths are machine-specific. If you move the `entire` binary, rerun: `entire configure --local --absolute-git-hook-path`."
* Hook installation is per-repository: "For a new repository, run `entire enable` to install the Git hooks Entire needs for commit and push behavior." Reinstall with `entire configure --force`.
* Verification that a clone has the remote branch: `git branch -r | grep 'entire/checkpoints/v1'`.

### DOCS GAP — explicitly not answered

There is **no page and no checklist stating what each teammate must do per clone or per machine to see others' checkpoints**. In particular, the docs never state:
* whether a teammate must run `entire enable` in their own clone in order to *read* someone else's checkpoints (only that `entire enable` installs hooks for *capture*),
* any command to pull/fetch teammates' checkpoint data into a fresh clone,
* whether the checkpoints branch arrives automatically with a normal `git clone` / `git fetch`,
* any per-machine step for reading checkpoints from a separate checkpoint remote.

The only reading requirements the docs assert are: repository access, the branch having been pushed, and (for Entire.io) GitHub App access.

---

## 6. Question 5 — Troubleshooting, verbatim (symptom → cause → fix)

From `guides/checkpoints/troubleshooting.md`.

### Run a checkpoint health check
&gt; "Start with `entire doctor` when `entire status` shows a stale or exited session, checkpoint commands seem to miss data, or you see warnings about metadata branches."
```bash
entire doctor
```
&gt; "`entire doctor` can repair some metadata branch issues and condense stuck sessions into permanent checkpoint storage. It does not fix every checkpoint issue: use Attach Sessions for missing commit trailers, and check remote or GitHub permissions for sync problems."

### Symptom: No checkpoint appears after a commit
Cause framing: "A checkpoint is created when Entire can connect a Git commit to captured agent session context."
Fixes (verbatim):
* Run `entire status` to confirm Entire is enabled in the repository
* Confirm the repo was set up with `entire enable` or `entire configure`
* If you committed from a GUI Git client, configure absolute Git hook paths
* If the agent session started before hooks were installed, start a new session after setup

Manual attach:
```bash
entire session attach SESSION_ID -a AGENT
```
&gt; "See Attach Sessions for the full flow."

### Symptom: The commit is missing the checkpoint trailer
&gt; "Entire links a commit to a checkpoint with an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer."
Causes (verbatim):
* The commit may have happened before Entire was enabled
* The prompt to link the session may have been declined
* The session may have been created in another folder
* Git hooks may not have run

Fix: "Use `entire checkpoint list` to see whether the checkpoint exists locally. If the session exists but the commit is not linked, use Attach Sessions."

### Symptom: The checkpoint appears locally but not in Entire.io
Cause: "Entire.io reads checkpoint data from the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch after it has been pushed."
Fix (verbatim numbered steps):
1. Run `git push`
2. Watch for any Entire warnings during the push
3. Confirm `push_sessions` is enabled in checkpoint storage settings
4. Confirm the Entire GitHub App can access the repository

### Symptom: Checkpoint data is going to the wrong place
Cause: "By default, Entire pushes checkpoint data to the same remote as your code. If your team stores checkpoint data in a separate repository, configure a checkpoint remote."
Fix: "Check `.entire/settings.json` for:"
```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "checkpoint_remote": {
      "provider": "github",
      "repo": "myorg/checkpoints-private"
    }
  }
}
```

### Symptom: Checkpoint remote did not sync
&gt; "Checkpoint remote failures do not block your main Git push. Entire saves checkpoint data locally and warns when it cannot push the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch."
Common causes (verbatim):
* You do not have write access to the checkpoint remote
* The checkpoint remote repository does not exist
* `.entire/settings.json` was not committed, so teammates or Entire.io cannot discover the setting
* You are pushing from a fork, and fork detection skipped the organization checkpoint remote

### Symptom: A squash merge lost the checkpoint link
&gt; "Some Git hosts can remove commit trailers during squash merges. If the `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer is removed, Entire cannot link the squashed commit back to its checkpoint metadata.
&gt; For GitLab squash merges, configure the squash commit message template under Settings &gt; Repository &gt; Merge requests.
&gt; Use a template that includes the squashed commit content, for example:"
```text
%{title} (%{reference})

%{all_commits}
```

### Still stuck?
Include these details when asking for help (verbatim):
* `entire version`
* the agent you were using
* whether the checkpoint appears in `entire checkpoint list`
* the checkpoint ID, if there is one
* any warning printed during `git commit` or `git push`

&gt; "You can file an issue at github.com/entireio/cli or ask in the Entire Discord."

Related, from `git-hooks.md` — GUI Git client fix:
```bash
entire configure --local --absolute-git-hook-path
entire enable --local --absolute-git-hook-path
```
Hook line shape:
```sh
# Default
entire hooks git post-commit

# With --absolute-git-hook-path
'/Users/you/go/bin/entire' hooks git post-commit
```
```json
{
  "absolute_git_hook_path": true
}
```
Non-interactive setup: `entire enable --yes`, `entire enable --yes --agent claude-code`. Reinstall hooks: `entire configure --force`.

---

## 7. Question 6 — Review commands and flags

### Choosing a surface (`review-checkpoints.md`, verbatim table)

| Use this | When you want to |
| --- | --- |
| **Entire.io** | Browse checkpoints by repository and branch, inspect sessions visually, review file diffs, or share context with a team. |
| **Entire CLI** | Review from a terminal, explain a checkpoint or commit, inspect transcript output, or export JSON. |

What to look for (verbatim): the original prompt or task; the transcript and tool calls; files changed by the agent; file diffs and line attribution; token usage and session metadata; whether the checkpoint is linked to the commit you expected.

### CLI (`review-in-cli.md`)

```bash
entire checkpoint list
```
```bash
entire checkpoint search "what changed"
```
```bash
entire checkpoint explain CHECKPOINT_ID
```
```bash
entire checkpoint explain COMMIT_SHA
```
```bash
entire checkpoint explain CHECKPOINT_ID --generate
```
&gt; "The `--generate` flag requires the configured provider CLI to be installed and authenticated."

### Full flag reference (`cli-reference/checkpoint.md`)

```bash
entire checkpoint SUBCOMMAND
```

**`checkpoint list`** — "List checkpoints on the current branch."
```bash
entire checkpoint list [flags]
```
| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--no-pager` | Disable pager output |
| `--session ID` | Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix) |

**`checkpoint explain`** — "Generate an AI-powered explanation of a session or commit."
```bash
entire checkpoint explain [CHECKPOINT_ID | COMMIT_SHA] [flags]
```
| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `-c, --checkpoint ID` | Explain a specific checkpoint (ID or prefix) |
| `--commit SHA` | Explain a specific commit (SHA or ref, "commit-ish") |
| `--force` | Regenerate summary even if one already exists (requires `--generate`) |
| `--full` | Show full parsed transcript (all prompts/responses) |
| `--generate` | Generate an AI summary for the checkpoint |
| `--no-pager` | Disable pager output |
| `--raw-transcript` | Show raw transcript file (JSONL format) |
| `--search-all` | Search all commits (no branch/depth limit, may be slow) |
| `--session ID` | Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix) |
| `-s, --short` | Show summary only (omit prompts and files) |

Note: "The `--generate` flag requires the configured provider CLI, such as Claude CLI, to be installed and authenticated."

**`checkpoint search`** — "Search checkpoints using semantic and keyword matching."
```bash
entire checkpoint search [QUERY] [flags]
```
| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--author NAME` | Filter by author name |
| `--branch NAME` | Filter by branch name |
| `--date PERIOD` | Filter by time period (`week` or `month`) |
| `--json` | Output as JSON |
| `--limit N` | Maximum number of results per page |
| `--page N` | Page number, 1-based |
| `--repo OWNER/REPO` | Filter by repository. Use `*` to search all accessible repos. |

**`checkpoint rewind`** — "Rewind to a previous checkpoint within the current session."
Warning: "`entire checkpoint rewind` is deprecated and will be removed in a future release."
```bash
entire checkpoint rewind [flags]
```
| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--to COMMIT` | Rewind to specific commit ID (non-interactive) |
| `--list` | List available rewind points (JSON output) |
| `--logs-only` | Only restore logs, don't modify working directory (for logs-only points) |
| `--reset` | Reset branch to commit (destructive, for logs-only points) |

Warning: "Rewinding discards changes made after the selected checkpoint. Make sure to commit any changes you want to keep before rewinding. The `--reset` flag is destructive and will reset your branch."

### Entire.io review (`review-in-entire-io.md`)

Open entire.io, then open the repository that contains the checkpoint.

Verbatim numbered steps:
1. Choose the branch you want to review.
2. Find the commit with the checkpoint.
3. Open the checkpoint detail view.
4. Use **Sessions** to inspect the prompt, transcript, tool activity, and session metadata.
5. Use **Changes** to review the files and diff connected to the checkpoint.

Branch view — each checkpoint row shows: commit SHA; commit message; agent; code changes; checkpoint count, when more than one checkpoint is attached; author.

Checkpoint detail — "The header shows the commit SHA, branch, author, agent, file count, AI percentage, and token usage when available." Two tabs: **Sessions** ("prompts, transcript, tool activity, session metadata, and any sessions that contributed to the checkpoint") and **Changes** ("modified files, with a file tree and diff view").

Keyboard: "Use **Alt + Left Arrow** and **Alt + Right Arrow** to move to the previous or next checkpoint on the same branch."

Open from a commit URL:
```text
https://entire.io/gh/&lt;org&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;/commit/&lt;commit-sha&gt;
```
Trailer in commit message:
```text
fix: handle empty repos in dispatch generation

Entire-Checkpoint: a1b2c3d4e5f6
```

---

## 8. Complete inventory of documented identifiers (nothing beyond this is documented)

**Branch:** `entire/checkpoints/v1`
**Trailer:** `Entire-Checkpoint`
**Files:** `.entire/settings.json`, `.entire/settings.local.json`, `.entire/.gitignore`, `.entire/redactors/`
**Settings keys seen in checkpoint docs:** `commit_linking` (top level, value `"always"`), `absolute_git_hook_path` (top level, boolean), `strategy_options.push_sessions` (boolean, default `true`), `strategy_options.checkpoint_remote.provider` (`"github"`), `strategy_options.checkpoint_remote.repo` (`"owner/repo"`), `strategy_options.summarize.enabled` (boolean)
**Commands:** `entire enable`, `entire enable --agent AGENT`, `entire enable --checkpoint-backend refs`, `entire enable --yes`, `entire enable --local --absolute-git-hook-path`, `entire agent add AGENT`, `entire agent list`, `entire configure --checkpoint-backend branch|refs`, `entire configure --checkpoint-remote github:owner/repo`, `entire configure --local --skip-push-sessions`, `entire configure --project --skip-push-sessions`, `entire configure --local --absolute-git-hook-path`, `entire configure --force`, `entire configure --summarize-provider PROVIDER`, `entire configure --summarize-model MODEL`, `entire status`, `entire status --detailed`, `entire doctor`, `entire version`, `entire session attach SESSION_ID -a AGENT`, `entire checkpoint list|explain|search|rewind`
**ID formats:** 12-char hex (branch backend, e.g. `a3b2c4d5e6f7`, `8a513f56ed70`); 26-char time-sortable (refs backend, e.g. `01KVBJCWYA4YW6J5M9GP655HZN`)

# Entire CLI — Agent Integrations Reference

Source: `docs.entire.io` raw markdown, fetched 2026-08-21. Everything below is verbatim from the docs. Gaps are marked **NOT DOCUMENTED**.

---

## 1. Supported agents and exact slugs

The authoritative enumeration is the `--agent` flag on `entire enable` (`/cli-reference/enable.md`):

&gt; `--agent NAME` — Agent to setup hooks for: `claude-code`, `codex`, `gemini`, `opencode`, `cursor`, `copilot-cli`, `factoryai-droid`, or `pi`. Enables non-interactive mode.

The same slugs are used for `entire agent add NAME`. Confirmed per-page:

| Agent | Exact slug | Overview status | Hook location (from `/guides/configuration/agent-hooks`) | Format |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Code | `claude-code` | Stable (default agent) | `.claude/settings.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Codex | `codex` | Stable | `.codex/hooks.json` (+ `.codex/config.toml`) | JSON hooks config |
| Copilot CLI | `copilot-cli` | Stable | `.github/hooks/entire.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Cursor | `cursor` | Stable | `.cursor/hooks.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Factory | `factoryai-droid` | Stable | `.factory/settings.json` | JSON hooks config |
| Gemini CLI | `gemini` | Stable | `.gemini/settings.json` | JSON hooks config |
| OpenCode | `opencode` | Stable | `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` | TypeScript plugin |
| Pi | `pi` | Preview | `.pi/extensions/entire/index.ts` | TypeScript extension |

Two slugs are traps: **Factory is `factoryai-droid`, not `factory` or `droid`** (page URL is `/agents/factory-droid` but the slug is `factoryai-droid`), and **Gemini is `gemini`, not `gemini-cli`** (page URL is `/agents/gemini-cli`). Copilot **is** `copilot-cli`.

&gt; The default agent is Claude Code. To choose a different one, run `entire agent add NAME`.

**Contradiction to flag:** `/agents/overview.md` labels Codex, Copilot CLI, Cursor, Factory, Gemini CLI and OpenCode as **Stable**, but each of those agents' own pages says the integration "is in preview and may contain bugs." Pi is Preview in both places.

---

## 2. Claude Code — full detail

### Setup

```bash
entire agent add claude-code
claude
```

Non-interactive alternative (`/cli-reference/enable.md`):

```bash
entire enable --yes --agent claude-code
```

Prerequisites: Entire CLI installed, Claude Code installed, a Git repository with Entire enabled.

### What gets written where

`.claude/settings.json` — "JSON hooks config" (`/guides/configuration/agent-hooks.md`). And from `/cli-reference/enable.md`:

&gt; Entire always modifies the agent's **project** settings (e.g., `.claude/settings.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts`, `.factory/settings.json`, or `.pi/extensions/entire/index.ts`).

The `--local` / `--project` flags on `entire enable` control only which **Entire** settings file is written (`.entire/settings.local.json` vs `.entire/settings.json`) — they do not change the agent settings target, which is always the project-level agent file.

### Which Claude Code hook events are registered — **NOT DOCUMENTED**

I grepped all 109 documentation pages for `SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStop`, `PreCompact`. They appear **only** on three pages, none of which is the Claude Code page:

- `/agents/codex.md` — those are *Codex's* hooks (`SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `PreToolUse`)
- `/agents/external-agent-plugins/data-model.md` and `/lifecycle.md` — the generic external-plugin event model

**The docs never state which Claude Code hook events Entire registers, and never show the hook command lines written into `.claude/settings.json`.** Do not guess these — read an actual `.claude/settings.json` after running `entire agent add claude-code` if you need them.

The Claude Code page describes the mechanism only in these terms:

&gt; Entire automatically detects when Claude Code is active and begins capturing the session via Git hooks:
&gt; 1. **Session Detection** — when Claude Code starts, Entire detects the process.
&gt; 2. **Hook Triggers** — Git hooks fire on file changes and commits.
&gt; 3. **Checkpoint Creation** — checkpoints are created when you or the agent make a Git commit.
&gt; 4. **Metadata Capture** — session data is stored on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch.

### What gets captured

| Data | Description |
|---|---|
| Conversation | Full transcript of prompts and responses |
| File changes | All files modified during the session |
| Tool calls | Commands executed, files read, etc. |
| Token usage | Input, output, and cache tokens |
| Timestamps | Start time, checkpoints, end time |

### Features unique to / strongest on Claude Code

**Nested subagent capture** — when Claude Code spawns sub-agents via the Task tool, Entire captures these as nested sessions:

```
Main Session
├── Checkpoint 1
├── Sub-agent Session (Task: "Run tests")
│   ├── Sub-checkpoint 1
│   └── Sub-checkpoint 2
└── Checkpoint 2
```

**Rewind during sessions** — `entire checkpoint rewind` works while Claude Code is running.
**Resume** — `entire session resume BRANCH`; "The CLI restores the session log if needed and prints the Claude Code resume command." (The docs do not print the literal command string for Claude Code, unlike Codex/Copilot/OpenCode/Pi/Factory where they do.)
**Explain** — `entire checkpoint explain`.

&gt; **Note:** The `--generate` flag on `entire checkpoint explain` requires the configured provider CLI, such as Claude CLI, to be installed and authenticated.

### Working-directory / restart requirements

The Claude Code troubleshooting section states:

&gt; 1. Verify Entire is enabled: `entire status`
&gt; 2. Check that Git hooks are installed: `ls .git/hooks`
&gt; 3. Ensure Claude Code is running from within the repository

And `/guides/configuration/agent-hooks.md` adds the general rule (applies to all agents):

&gt; Then start a fresh session with the configured agent from inside the repository. If the agent was already running before hooks were installed, restart it.

So: the session must be launched **from inside the repo**, and an agent already running when hooks were installed must be **restarted**. The docs do not state anything more specific about *when* Claude Code reads its hook config.

### Documented example workflow

```bash
# 1. Enable Entire
cd my-project
entire enable

# 2. Start Claude Code
claude

# 3. Work with Claude (in Claude Code)
&gt; Add user authentication with JWT tokens

# 4. Check status (in another terminal)
entire status

# 5. Made a mistake? Rewind
entire checkpoint rewind

# 6. Commit when ready
git add .
git commit -m "Add JWT authentication"

# 7. Push to see on entire.io
git push
```

### Caveats

The Claude Code page lists **no** "Caveats and Limitations" section — it is the only built-in agent page without one. Its stated troubleshooting cases are "Session Not Being Captured" (above) and "Rewind Not Working":

&gt; Rewind requires an active session with checkpoints. […] If no session is active, checkpoints may only exist on the sessions branch.

---

## 3. Multi-agent setups

**Yes, explicitly supported.** From `/guides/configuration/agent-hooks.md`:

&gt; You can enable multiple agents at the same time. Each agent's hooks are independent.

From `/cli-reference/configure.md`:

&gt; To manage agent hooks, use `entire agent`; on first run, it installs agent hooks, and on subsequent runs, it lets you enable more agents.

Exact commands (`/cli-reference/agent.md`):

```bash
entire agent SUBCOMMAND
```

```bash
entire agent list          # List installed and available agents.
entire agent add AGENT_NAME [flags]   # Install hooks for an agent.
entire agent remove AGENT_NAME        # Uninstall hooks for an agent.
```

`agent add` flags:

| Flag | Description |
|---|---|
| `--force` | Reinstall hooks even if already present |
| `--local-dev` | Install hooks in local-dev mode |

Reinstall a stale/hand-edited hook:

```bash
entire agent add AGENT_NAME --force
```

Verify:

```bash
entire agent list
entire status --detailed
```

Note on `entire enable`: "If you also pass `--agent NAME`, that explicit agent selection takes precedence over the default multi-agent behavior." — implying `entire enable` without `--agent` has a default multi-agent path. The docs do not describe what that default set is.

---

## 4. Other agents — setup and caveats

### Codex — `codex`

```bash
entire agent add codex
codex
```

&gt; `entire agent add codex` installs hooks into `.codex/hooks.json` and enables Codex's required `codex_hooks = true` feature flag in `.codex/config.toml` automatically — no manual config needed.

Hook events (the only agent whose events the docs name): `SessionStart` → new session; `UserPromptSubmit` → each prompt; `Stop` → finalizes each turn and advances checkpoint state. Transcript read from Codex JSONL rollout files in `~/.codex/` or `CODEX_HOME`. Resume prints `codex resume SESSION_ID`.

Caveats, verbatim:
1. Subagent capture is not available — Codex does not expose subagent lifecycle hooks.
2. Session completion is turn-based — no distinct session-end hook; relies on per-turn `Stop`.
3. Transcript paths can be missing — in ephemeral Codex runs the path may be null.
4. `PreToolUse` is currently shell-only and is not used for lifecycle tracking.
5. Transcript capture depends on Codex writing rollout JSONL files.

### Copilot CLI — `copilot-cli`

```bash
entire agent add copilot-cli
copilot
```

&gt; creates `.github/hooks/entire.json`, which Copilot CLI automatically discovers and executes at key lifecycle events.

Tip from docs: you can commit `.github/hooks/entire.json` so your team gets Entire integration automatically. Transcripts read from `~/.copilot/session-state/` (`events.jsonl`). Resume prints `copilot --resume SESSION_ID`.

Caveats, verbatim:
- Token usage is only available in aggregate after the session ends, not mid-session.
- No subagent start hook — only subagent stop events are captured.
- The `userPromptSubmitted` hook fires before `sessionStart` on the first prompt (handled automatically).
- Works best when you commit at logical stopping points, or tell the agent to handle committing.

### Cursor — `cursor`

```bash
entire agent add cursor
cursor   # or: agent  (for Cursor CLI)
```

Hooks in `.cursor/hooks.json`. Named hooks: `before-submit-prompt`, `subagent-start`, `subagent-stop`, `pre-compact`, plus session start/end.

&gt; **Warning: Rewind and resume are not available** for Cursor sessions. You can use other Entire commands (`status`, `doctor`, `checkpoint explain`, `disable`), but cannot restore to previous checkpoints.

Other caveats: no token accounting; **file detection via `git status`** (Cursor transcripts lack tool-invocation records, so files must be staged or committed to be tracked); no detailed tool records; `@` context-attachment syntax differs between IDE and CLI; preview integration.

### Gemini CLI — `gemini`

```bash
entire agent add gemini
gemini
```

Hook file `.gemini/settings.json`. The weakest integration:

- Conversation transcripts may not be captured depending on Gemini CLI version.
- Session detection relies on process monitoring rather than dedicated lifecycle hooks.
- Token usage is not tracked.
- Works best when you commit at logical stopping points.

Captured: file changes, timestamps, session boundaries, checkpoints — the "What Gets Captured" table has **no Conversation and no Token usage rows**.

### OpenCode — `opencode`

```bash
entire agent add opencode
opencode
```

&gt; installs a TypeScript plugin that hooks into OpenCode's event system.

Plugin at `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts`. Events: `session.created`, `turn-start`, `turn-end`; on `turn-end` Entire calls `opencode export` for the transcript. Supports **mid-turn commits** — a commit made during a turn gets its own checkpoint, transcript fetched on demand. Rewind reimports the checkpoint-state transcript into OpenCode's database. Resume prints `opencode -s SESSION_ID`. Manual hook debug: `entire hooks opencode turn-end`.

Caveats: preview; **plugin requires Bun runtime**; session detection relies on OpenCode's event system; works best with the manual-commit strategy.

### Factory — `factoryai-droid`

```bash
entire agent add factoryai-droid
droid
```

&gt; installs hooks into `.factory/settings.json` automatically.

Hooks: `user-prompt-submit` for turns; `pre-tool-use` and `post-tool-use` capture Task tool invocations for **nested subagent sessions**. Transcripts under `~/.factory/sessions/&lt;repo&gt;/`. Resume prints `droid --session-id ID`. Full token capture (input, output, reasoning, cache).

Caveats: preview; session detection relies on hook-based process detection; transcript capture depends on Factory writing JSONL session logs.

### Pi — `pi` (Preview)

```bash
entire agent add pi
pi
```

&gt; installs a TypeScript extension at `.pi/extensions/entire/index.ts`. Pi loads the extension from the project and forwards lifecycle events to Entire.

Extension events: `session_start`, `before_agent_start` (captures prompt + active transcript path), `agent_end` (finalizes turn, stable transcript snapshot), `session_shutdown`. Transcripts from `~/.pi/agent/sessions/` or `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`; snapshots cached under `.entire/tmp/pi/`. Resume prints `pi --session SESSION_ID`. Reinstall: `entire agent add pi --force`.

Caveats, verbatim: preview; hooks are extension-based (TS, not JSON hook config); transcript capture depends on Pi writing JSONL session files; **subagent capture is not available**; shell-written files are less granular (paths extracted from Pi's `write` and `edit` tools; shell-modified files show up only via Git diffs at checkpoint time).

---

## 5. External agent plugin system

### Discovery and architecture

&gt; 1. You create an executable named `entire-agent-&lt;name&gt;`
&gt; 2. Place it anywhere on your `$PATH`
&gt; 3. The CLI discovers it at startup and registers it as a new agent

Communication is via **subcommands reading/writing JSON over stdin/stdout. Each invocation is stateless.**

Every invocation receives:

| Variable | Description |
|---|---|
| `ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT` | Absolute path to the git repository root |
| `ENTIRE_PROTOCOL_VERSION` | Protocol version (currently `1`) |

&gt; The working directory is always set to the repository root.

Conventions: JSON on stdout (unless noted); errors = non-zero exit + stderr message; stateless, no persistent connection.

Enablement setting — selecting an external agent during `entire agent add` or `entire enable` auto-enables `external_agents`. To enable discovery manually:

```json .entire/settings.json
{
  "external_agents": true
}
```

&gt; **Warning:** Plugins are arbitrary executables on your `$PATH` that receive repository paths and session data. Only install plugins you trust, as they have the same filesystem access as the CLI itself.

### Lifecycle — how capture actually works

**Phase 1 — Discovery (every CLI startup):** scans `$PATH` for `entire-agent-&lt;name&gt;`; calls `info` once per binary; validates protocol version; registers. If it fails, **the agent is silently skipped**.

**Phase 2 — Enable (`entire enable`):** `detect` → then `install-hooks [--local-dev] [--force]` (only if the `hooks` capability is declared).

**Phase 3 — Agent session.** Requires the `hooks` capability. Every hook invocation:

```
Hook fires → parse-hook → Event dispatched by type
```

`parse-hook --hook &lt;name&gt;` receives the raw payload on stdin and returns a normalized Event, or `null` if the hook has no lifecycle significance (CLI does nothing).

Routing by event type:

- **SessionStart (1):** `get-session-id` → `get-session-dir --repo-path &lt;path&gt;` → `resolve-session-file --session-dir &lt;dir&gt; --session-id &lt;id&gt;` → `read-session` → `write-session` → optionally `write-hook-response --message &lt;msg&gt;` (if `hook_response_writer`).
- **TurnStart (2):** `extract-modified-files --path &lt;path&gt; --offset &lt;n&gt;` (if `transcript_analyzer`), then **the CLI creates a git checkpoint of the current state**.
- **TurnEnd (3):** `prepare-transcript --session-ref &lt;path&gt;` (if `transcript_preparer`) → `read-transcript --session-ref &lt;path&gt;` → `chunk-transcript --max-size &lt;n&gt;` → `get-transcript-position --path &lt;path&gt;` (if `transcript_analyzer`) → `extract-modified-files --path &lt;path&gt; --offset &lt;n&gt;` (if `transcript_analyzer`) → `calculate-tokens --offset &lt;n&gt;` (if `token_calculator`) → **the CLI stores a checkpoint in git history**.
- **Compaction (4):** same flow as TurnEnd (save + reset offset).
- **SessionEnd (5):** CLI creates a final checkpoint and cleans up session state.
- **SubagentStart / SubagentEnd (6–7):** if `subagent_aware_extractor`, CLI calls `extract-all-modified-files --offset &lt;n&gt; --subagents-dir &lt;dir&gt;` and `calculate-total-tokens --offset &lt;n&gt; --subagents-dir &lt;dir&gt;`.

**Phase 4 — User commands:**

| Command | Subcommands called |
|---|---|
| `entire checkpoint rewind` | `read-transcript`, `reassemble-transcript`, `extract-modified-files` |
| `entire status` | `get-transcript-position` |
| `entire session resume` | `format-resume-command` |

**Phase 5 — Disable (`entire disable --uninstall`):** `uninstall-hooks` (only if `hooks` capability).

**Error handling:** 30-second default timeout per subcommand; stdout and stderr each capped at **10 MB** (matters for `read-transcript` on large sessions); **no retries** — non-zero exit + stderr message is a failure; graceful degradation — `parse-hook` returning `null` means no action, and if the repo has Entire disabled, hooks exit silently.

### Required commands

`info`:

```bash
entire-agent-myagent info
```

```json
{
  "protocol_version": 1,
  "name": "myagent",
  "type": "MyAgent",
  "description": "MyAgent - AI-powered code editor",
  "is_preview": true,
  "protected_dirs": [".myagent"],
  "hook_names": ["session-start", "session-end", "stop"],
  "capabilities": {
    "hooks": true,
    "transcript_analyzer": true,
    "transcript_preparer": false,
    "token_calculator": false,
    "text_generator": false,
    "hook_response_writer": false,
    "subagent_aware_extractor": false
  }
}
```

`name` must match the `&lt;name&gt;` in the binary name. `protected_dirs` are "Directories the CLI should not modify (excluded from checkpoints and diffs)".

Other required commands: `detect` → `{"present": true}` (return `{"present": false}` and the CLI skips enabling); `get-session-id` (stdin HookInput) → `{"session_id": "abc123"}`; `get-session-dir --repo-path &lt;path&gt;` → `{"session_dir": "/path/to/sessions"}`; `resolve-session-file --session-dir &lt;dir&gt; --session-id &lt;id&gt;` → `{"session_file": "/path/to/session/file.jsonl"}`; `read-session` (stdin HookInput → stdout AgentSession); `write-session` (stdin AgentSession, exit 0); `read-transcript --session-ref &lt;path&gt;` (raw bytes on stdout); `chunk-transcript --max-size &lt;n&gt;` → `{"chunks": ["&lt;base64-encoded-chunk&gt;", "..."]}`; `reassemble-transcript` (stdin chunks → stdout raw bytes, used by rewind); `format-resume-command --session-id &lt;id&gt;` → `{"command": "myagent --resume abc123"}`.

### Optional capabilities

- **`hooks`** — `parse-hook --hook &lt;name&gt;`; `install-hooks [--local-dev] [--force]` returning `{"hooks_installed": 3}`; `uninstall-hooks`; `are-hooks-installed` returning `{"installed": true}`.
- **`transcript_analyzer`** — `get-transcript-position --path &lt;path&gt;` → `{"position": 12345}`; `extract-modified-files --path &lt;path&gt; --offset &lt;n&gt;` → `{"files": [...], "current_position": 12345}`; `extract-prompts --session-ref &lt;path&gt; --offset &lt;n&gt;` → `{"prompts": [...]}`; `extract-summary --session-ref &lt;path&gt;` → `{"summary": "...", "has_summary": true}`.
- **`transcript_preparer`** — `prepare-transcript --session-ref &lt;path&gt;`.
- **`token_calculator`** — `calculate-tokens --offset &lt;n&gt;`:

```json
{
  "input_tokens": 1500,
  "output_tokens": 500,
  "cache_creation_tokens": 0,
  "cache_read_tokens": 200,
  "api_call_count": 3
}
```

  Only `input_tokens` and `output_tokens` are required; others default to `0`.
- **`text_generator`** — `generate-text --model &lt;model&gt;` → `{"text": "..."}`.
- **`hook_response_writer`** — `write-hook-response --message &lt;msg&gt;`.
- **`subagent_aware_extractor`** — `extract-all-modified-files --offset &lt;n&gt; --subagents-dir &lt;dir&gt;` → `{"files": [...]}`; `calculate-total-tokens --offset &lt;n&gt; --subagents-dir &lt;dir&gt;` (same format as `calculate-tokens`, plus an optional nested `subagent_tokens` object).

&gt; The CLI will never call subcommands for capabilities you don't declare.

### Data model

**HookInput** (stdin to `get-session-id` and `read-session`) — required: `hook_type`, `session_id`, `session_ref`, `timestamp` (RFC 3339). Optional: `user_prompt`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `tool_input`, `raw_data`. `hook_type` is one of `session_start`, `session_end`, `user_prompt_submit`, `stop`, `pre_tool_use`, `post_tool_use`.

**AgentSession** (input to `write-session`, output from `read-session`): `session_id`, `agent_name`, `repo_path`, `session_ref`, `start_time`, `native_data` (bytes/null, opaque native-format content), `modified_files[]`, `new_files[]`, `deleted_files[]`.

**Event** (returned by `parse-hook`) — only `type` and `session_id` are required:

| Value | Name | Description |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | SessionStart | Agent session has begun |
| 2 | TurnStart | User submitted a prompt |
| 3 | TurnEnd | Agent finished responding |
| 4 | Compaction | Context window compression (triggers save + offset reset) |
| 5 | SessionEnd | Session terminated |
| 6 | SubagentStart | A subagent was spawned |
| 7 | SubagentEnd | A subagent completed |

Optional Event fields: `previous_session_id`, `session_ref`, `prompt`, `model`, `timestamp`, `tool_use_id`, `subagent_id`, `tool_input`, `subagent_type`, `task_description`, `response_message`, `metadata`.

### Building a plugin

Open an issue in `entireio/external-agents` first ("Adding support for a new agent creates a long-term support commitment"). Private/internal plugins skip the issue and PR steps.

```bash
git clone https://github.com/entireio/external-agents.git
cd external-agents
mise trust
mise install
```

New integrations live under `agents/entire-agent-&lt;slug&gt;`. The repo ships an agent skill named `entire-external-agent`:

```text
/entire-external-agent
```

Single phases: `/entire-external-agent research`, `/entire-external-agent write-tests`, `/entire-external-agent implement`. Phases are Research → Scaffold → Implement.

Verify locally:

```bash
cd agents/entire-agent-&lt;slug&gt;
mise run build
export PATH="$PWD:$PATH"

cd /path/to/test-repo
entire enable --agent SLUG
```

```bash
entire status
entire checkpoint list
entire checkpoint explain CHECKPOINT_ID --short
```

Reference plugins: `entire-agent-amp` (Amp), `entire-agent-goose` (Goose, SQLite-backed storage), `entire-agent-kiro` (Kiro); minimal reference `entireio/roger-roger`.

&gt; Treat captured hook payloads and transcript fixtures as ground truth. Documentation can drift.

---

## Cross-cutting facts

- All agents store captured data on the **`entire/checkpoints/v1`** branch — no extra commits on your working branch.
- Commits include **`Entire-Checkpoint`** trailers, clickable from PRs.
- Every agent's troubleshooting flow starts with `entire status`, then checking that agent's hook file, then confirming the agent runs from inside the repo. Several pages point at `.entire/settings.json` for strategy settings and at `entire doctor` for hook errors.

## What the docs do NOT answer

1. **Claude Code hook event names** registered by Entire — absent from all 109 pages.
2. **The literal hook command lines** written into `.claude/settings.json` (or any built-in agent's hook file) — no page shows the file contents for any built-in agent.
3. The exact **Claude Code resume command** string printed by `entire session resume` (given verbatim for Codex, Copilot, Factory, OpenCode, Pi, but not Claude Code).
4. Any Claude Code **caveats/limitations** section — it is the only built-in agent page without one.
5. What the **default multi-agent behavior** of `entire enable` (without `--agent`) actually selects.
6. Whether `entire agent remove` accepts `--force` or any flags — none are listed.</result>
