# Entire CLI — Verified Reference (Search, Dispatches, Skills, Learn)

Sources: `docs.entire.io/guides/search/*`, `/guides/dispatches/*`, `/guides/skills/overview`, `/learn/*`, and the `cli-reference` pages those guides link to (`search`, `dispatch`, `recap`, `why`, `blame`, `investigate`, `review`, `agent-help`, `checkpoint`, `global-flags`, `guides/configuration/review-profiles`).

Every command, flag, and quoted string below appears in the docs. Anything the docs do not state is called out as **NOT DOCUMENTED**.

---

## 1. `entire search`

### Syntax

```bash
entire search [QUERY] [flags]
```

`entire checkpoint search` is an equivalent alias, and the troubleshooting page adds: it "is an equivalent alias in every version."

Run without arguments to open interactive search. Use `--json` for machine-readable output.

```shell
entire login          # required first
entire search         # interactive, current repo
```

### Two search modes

The overview page states search supports two kinds of results:

- **Semantic search** finds related commits and sessions based on meaning and keywords.
- **Code search** finds literal text in your indexed repositories, such as function names, error messages, and configuration keys.

| Surface | Semantic search | Code search |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Entire.io | Use the **Commits** and **Sessions** tabs. | Use the **Code** tab. |
| Entire CLI | Run `entire search "QUERY"`. | Run `entire search --code "TEXT"`. |

The `cli-reference/search` page describes the non-`--code` mode as "hybrid semantic and keyword matching" over "checkpoints, commits, and sessions."

### Full flag list (from `/cli-reference/search`)

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--all-repos` | Search all accessible repositories instead of only the current repository. |
| `--author NAME` | Filter by author name. |
| `--branch NAME` | Filter by branch name. |
| `--case-sensitive` | Reserved for future case-sensitive Code Search. Only valid with `--code`; currently does not change backend matching. |
| `--code` | Search code content across repositories instead of checkpoints, commits, and sessions. |
| `--date PERIOD` | Filter by time period. Use `week` or `month`. |
| `--json` | Output as JSON. |
| `--limit N` | Maximum number of results per page, or total results with `--code`. Default is `10`. |
| `--page N` | Page number, 1-based. Default is `1`. |
| `--repo REPOSITORY` | Filter by repository. Accepts `gh/owner/repo`, `et/project/repo`, `owner/repo`, a repository ULID, or `*`; repeatable and comma-separated. |

### Inline filters

```bash
entire search "retry logic author:alex date:month branch:main repo:entireio/cli"
```

Supported inline filters:

- `author:NAME`
- `date:week|month`
- `branch:NAME`
- `repo:OWNER/NAME`
- `repo:*`

### Documented examples

```bash
entire search "auth token refresh"
entire search "auth token refresh" --repo entireio/cli
entire search "repo:* auth token refresh"
entire search "retry logic" author:alex branch:main date:month
entire search "auth token refresh" --json
```

```shell
entire search "history import when enabling Entire"
entire search "how did we decide to make history import explicit when enabling Entire?"
entire search "history import when enabling Entire" branch:cli-onboard
entire search "history import when enabling Entire" --repo entireio/cli
entire search "history import when enabling Entire" --repo entireio/cli --json
entire search --code "import-history"
```

From the tutorial:

```shell
entire search "how did we decide whether a mission is ready to launch?"
entire search --code "getMissionReadinessStatus"
```

### Code search specifics

```bash
entire search --code "refreshAccessToken"
entire search --code "refreshAccessToken" --repo entireio/cli
entire search --code "repo:* refreshAccessToken"
entire search --code "refreshAccessToken" --json
```

Documented constraints, verbatim in substance:

- Results are grouped under a `repo:path` heading with line-numbered matches beneath it.
- Code Search matches exact text and is **currently case-insensitive**. The CLI accepts `--case-sensitive`, but the search backend does not yet apply it.
- In `--code` mode, `repo:` is the **only** supported inline filter. `author:`, `branch:`, and `date:` tokens are treated as **literal query text**, and the `--author`, `--branch`, `--date`, and `--page` flags **do not apply**.
- Code Search matches literal text, not a description of behavior — search for a function or variable name, error message, config key, or unique string. If you remember the work but not the source text, use semantic/keyword search without `--code`.

### Cross-repo search

- CLI search is **scoped to the current repository by default**. Entire.io searches **all accessible indexed repositories by default**.
- Change CLI scope with `--repo`, `--all-repos`, or `repo:*`.

```shell
entire search "retry logic" --repo OWNER/REPO
entire search "retry logic" --all-repos
```

- Entire.io: "You do not need a local clone to search a repository in Entire.io. The repository must be available to your Entire account and indexed. If it does not appear in the filter, you cannot search it from Entire.io yet."
- The `search` Skill "can also search across repositories you can access in Entire, even if they are not cloned to your computer."

### Interactive result tabs

In interactive search, enter a query and press Enter; number keys move between result types:

- `1` — Checkpoints
- `2` — Sessions
- `3` — Commits
- `4` — Code

"The same query searches past work and code. Code results are grouped by file."

In Entire.io the tabs are: **Commits** (related Git commits), **Sessions** (related agent conversations), **Code** (lines containing literal matches from indexed source files).

### What is indexed and when

What the docs actually state:

- Semantic side indexes **checkpoints, commits, and sessions**; `--code` indexes **code content / indexed source files**.
- "Confirm the work has been committed and pushed, then give Entire a couple minutes to index it." (troubleshooting)
- "Give Entire a couple of minutes to index all three changes." (tutorial)
- `search` "uses indexed checkpoint history, so give Entire a moment to index your pushed checkpoints before trying these queries."
- The repo must be Entire-enabled — check with `entire status`.
- Prerequisite for the search tutorial: the repo is mirrored to Entire, has checkpoint capture set up, and can be committed and pushed.
- You must be **logged in** to use Search, "including when searching public repositories."

**NOT DOCUMENTED:** the exact indexing latency SLA, the indexer's file-type/size limits, which branches get code-indexed, or whether unpushed local work is ever semantically searchable.

### Query-writing guidance (Entire.io page)

"Semantic search matches the content of past work, so describe what you are looking for rather than giving an instruction. A query like `git optimization` works better than `show me all of the latest git optimizations`: words such as 'show me all of the' do not appear in the work itself and dilute the match. To find recent work, use the date filter instead of words like 'latest'."

Opening Search in Entire.io: sidebar, `/`, or `Command-K` (macOS) / `Ctrl-K` elsewhere then choose the Search action. Empty query shows recent searches. Past-work results filter by repository, date, or branch and sort by relevance, date, author, or title; code results filter by repository only.

### Search troubleshooting (all documented cases)

- **Not authenticated** → `entire login`. "Search only returns repositories, history, and code your account can access."
- **`entire search` missing from `entire --help`** → update the CLI; check `entire version`. "In versions 0.9.0 and earlier, `entire search` was hidden from help output but still runnable."
- **Interactive view when you wanted plain output** → use `--json`. "Running a history search in a terminal opens the interactive results view. Redirected or piped output is JSON automatically."
- **Results too broad** → add a feature name, error message, file, decision, or filter: `entire search "duplicate validation error banner" branch:main date:month`
- **Search Skill returns nothing useful** → confirm the Skills workflow is installed and the agent can run `entire search --json`; make the prompt specific, e.g. `search past work for duplicate validation in the checkout form`.

---

## 2. `entire dispatch` and `entire recap`

### `entire dispatch`

```bash
entire dispatch [flags]
```

**What it produces:** "Dispatches turn recent agent work into a readable summary you can share with teammates, reviewers, product partners, or anyone who needs to understand what changed." Output is markdown.

**Two modes:**
- **Cloud mode** (default) — generates from Entire server data.
- **Local mode** (`--local`) — generates from local repository data using your locally installed agent CLI.

**Interactivity:** "If you run `entire dispatch` with no flags in an interactive terminal, Entire launches a guided wizard. In non-interactive contexts, or whenever you provide flags, it prints markdown output directly."

#### Flags (from `/cli-reference/dispatch`)

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--local` | Generate via the locally installed agent CLI instead of the Entire server |
| `--since TIME` | Start of the time window. Accepts Go durations, relative times, or ISO dates. Defaults to `7d`. |
| `--until TIME` | End of the time window. Defaults to now. |
| `--all-branches` | Include every existing local branch. Only valid with `--local`. |
| `--repos OWNER/REPO` | Limit cloud dispatch to one or more repos. Accepts comma-separated GitHub repo slugs. |
| `--voice PRESET_OR_TEXT` | Customize the dispatch tone with a preset such as `neutral` or `marvin`, or provide your own text. |

Constraints, verbatim: "`--repos` cannot be combined with `--local`, and `--all-branches` only applies to `--local`." / "Cloud dispatch supports up to 5 repos per request, and each repo must use `owner/repo` format." / "`--all-branches` only includes existing local branches in local mode. Renamed or deleted branches are skipped."

#### Documented examples

```bash
entire dispatch
entire dispatch --repos entireio/cli,entireio/entire.io
entire dispatch --since 7d
entire dispatch --local
entire dispatch --local --all-branches
entire dispatch --voice neutral
entire dispatch --voice "Audience: product team. Focus on user-visible changes."
entire dispatch --voice "Audience: leadership. Focus on impact and risks."
```

#### What dispatches summarize

- checkpoints created from agent-assisted commits
- sessions, prompts, transcripts, and tool activity
- commits connected to captured agent work
- one repository, multiple repositories, or a narrower branch and time window

**When to use:** "Use a dispatch when you want the story of recent work without opening every checkpoint, session, commit, and branch by hand." / "A dispatch is a summary. Use it to get oriented, write status updates, or prepare a handoff. Use Checkpoints when you need to inspect the exact prompt, transcript, files, and diff behind a change."

Common uses listed: weekly engineering update; progress across several repos; focused recap for one repo and branch; non-technical brief from technical work; catching up before reviewing a branch.

#### Dispatches in Entire.io

Open from sidebar, command palette, or the `g d` shortcut. Scope rules:

- Select up to 5 repositories per dispatch
- If you select one repository, choose up to 5 branches
- If you select multiple repositories, Dispatches use each repository's default branch
- Time windows: 1 day, 7 days, 14 days, or 30 days

Voices: `neutral` (straightforward product/engineering update prose), `marvin` (drier, more opinionated), `custom` (name the audience, purpose, or framing yourself — e.g. `Audience: product team. Focus on user-visible changes only.`). Click **Generate dispatch**; the preview includes the summary plus scope metadata (repositories, branches, date window).

**Caveat:** "Dispatches may be enabled gradually for Entire.io accounts." If not visible, confirm the account and workspace access, and use `entire dispatch` from the CLI meanwhile.

### `entire recap`

```bash
entire recap [flags]
```

**What it produces:** "a time-based summary of agent and checkpoint activity. By default, it opens the interactive recap view for today." The tutorial adds it "should show recent activity for this repository, including sessions, checkpoints, agent activity, and token usage when available."

#### Flags (from `/cli-reference/recap`)

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--90` | Show the rolling 90-day window. |
| `--agent ID` | Show activity for one agent, or use `all`. Defaults to `all`. |
| `--color MODE` | Set color output to `auto`, `always`, or `never`. Defaults to `auto`. |
| `--day` | Show today only. This is the default time window. |
| `--insecure-http-auth` | Allow plain HTTP auth. Local development only. |
| `--month` | Show this calendar month. |
| `--static` | Print static output instead of opening the interactive recap. |
| `--view VIEW` | Choose which columns to show: `you`, `team`, or `both`. Defaults to `both`. |
| `--week` | Show the last 7 days. |
| `-h, --help` | Show help for `entire recap`. |

#### Documented examples

```bash
entire recap
entire recap --static
entire recap --day
entire recap --week
entire recap --month
entire recap --90
entire recap --agent codex
entire recap --view you
entire recap --view team
entire recap --view both
entire recap --week --static --color never
entire recap --agent codex --static
```

### dispatch vs recap — when to use each

Stated distinction: `recap` is a **time-based summary of your/team checkpoint and agent activity** (an interactive terminal view by default, `--static` for scripts); `dispatch` produces **shareable markdown prose** for teammates and stakeholders, with voice control and multi-repo cloud scope. In the review tutorial, recap is step 3 of the loop ("Use `entire recap` to understand what happened").

**If `entire recap` looks empty:** ensure agent work ran in an Entire-enabled repo, commits were linked to session context, the branch was pushed, and hosted analysis had a few minutes. Also check `entire checkpoint list`.

**NOT DOCUMENTED:** any `--json` flag on `recap` or `dispatch`; any way to write a dispatch directly to a file or post it to Slack/GitHub; the full list of `--voice` presets beyond `neutral` and `marvin` (`custom` is named only on the web page).

---

## 3. `entire why` / `entire blame` / `entire investigate`

These require **Entire 0.7.7 or higher** (per the tutorial prerequisites; "Unknown command 'blame' or 'why'" → update).

### `entire blame`

```bash
entire blame FILE [flags]
```

**Answers:** "the attribution status of the commit that introduced each line." Tags are **commit-level** signals:

- `AI`: the commit was attributed to an AI agent session
- `MX`: the commit mixed AI-attributed and human-attributed work
- `HU`: the commit was made without AI-attributed checkpoint data

"When available, the output also links lines to their Entire checkpoint metadata."

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--line LINE_OR_RANGE` | Only show a line or range, for example `12` or `12-20` |
| `--long` | Show the full attribution table with agent, model, author, and session columns |
| `--json` | Output attribution as JSON |

```bash
entire blame overview.mdx
entire blame overview.mdx --line 12-20
entire blame overview.mdx --line 12-20 --long
entire blame overview.mdx --json
entire blame src/main.js --line 279
```

Documented sample output:

```text
Line  Tag   Agent  Author          Checkpoint    Content
279   [MX]  Codex  blackgirlbytes  bfc2c1df9e4b  document.body.classList.add("leaderboard-route");
```

**If blame shows no checkpoint:** "Make sure you cloned the sample repo with its checkpoint metadata branch. Run `git branch -r | grep 'entire/checkpoints/v1'` to confirm the remote checkpoint branch is present."

### `entire why`

```bash
entire why FILE[:LINE] [flags]
```

**Answers:** "Explain the commit, checkpoint, prompt, and session behind a file or line." / "Use `entire why` when you want to jump from a file or specific line to the commit, checkpoint, prompt, and session behind it."

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--line LINE` | Explain a specific line, for example `12`. This is the same as `FILE:12`. |
| `--json` | Output explanation as JSON |

```bash
entire why overview.mdx
entire why overview.mdx:12
entire why overview.mdx --line 12
entire why overview.mdx:12 --json
entire why src/main.js:279
```

Documented sample output:

```text
Line 279 in src/main.js
    document.body.classList.add("leaderboard-route");

[MX] by Codex - gpt-5.5 - checkpoint bfc2c1df9e4b - session 019edf9f - commit 35cdcfb6
Prompt: "we want to be able to go the route /leaderboard and it renders the leaderboard there"

Full context: entire checkpoint explain bfc2c1df9e4b
```

**Caveat:** "`entire why` says no checkpoint is linked — That can be a valid result. Some lines come from commits that were not linked to Entire session context."

Follow-up documented in the tutorial:

```bash
entire checkpoint explain bfc2c1df9e4b --generate
```

`entire checkpoint explain` full syntax and flags:

```bash
entire checkpoint explain [CHECKPOINT_ID | COMMIT_SHA] [flags]
```

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `-c, --checkpoint ID` | Explain a specific checkpoint (ID or prefix) |
| `--commit SHA` | Explain a specific commit (SHA or ref, "commit-ish") |
| `--force` | Regenerate summary even if one already exists (requires `--generate`) |
| `--full` | Show full parsed transcript (all prompts/responses) |
| `--generate` | Generate an AI summary for the checkpoint |
| `--no-pager` | Disable pager output |
| `--raw-transcript` | Show raw transcript file (JSONL format) |
| `--search-all` | Search all commits (no branch/depth limit, may be slow) |
| `--session ID` | Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix) |
| `-s, --short` | Show summary only (omit prompts and files) |

Note: "`--generate` requires the configured provider CLI, such as Claude CLI, to be installed and authenticated." Output is structured as `## Intent` and `## Outcome` sections.

### `entire investigate`

```bash
entire investigate [SEED_DOC] [flags]
entire investigate SUBCOMMAND
```

**Answers / what it does:** "Run a multi-agent investigation. Agents take turns appending findings, evidence, and analysis to a shared findings document until quorum is reached."

**Status:** "`entire investigate` is experimental and appears under labs while the workflow is still being refined."

**Inputs:**

| Input | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `SEED_DOC` | Path to a starting findings file |
| `--issue-link URL` | GitHub issue or pull request URL resolved through `gh` |

"When neither input is supplied and the multi-agent picker opens, the picker collects an investigation prompt that becomes the topic for the run."

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--agents AGENTS` | Override configured agents with a comma-separated list |
| `--max-turns N` | Per-agent turn budget. Defaults to `2`. |
| `--quorum N` | Approvals needed to terminate. `0` means all agents. |
| `--continue RUN_ID` | Resume an existing run |
| `--edit` | Re-open the investigate config picker |
| `--findings` | Browse local investigation manifests |
| `--allow-untrusted-seed` | Required for non-interactive `--issue-link` investigations because the seed is attacker-influenced GitHub content |

**Subcommands:**

| Subcommand | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `show RUN_ID` | Print a saved investigation's summary and findings |
| `fix RUN_ID` | Launch a coding agent with the run's findings as grounded context |
| `clean RUN_ID` | Delete a saved investigation |
| `clean --all` | Delete all saved investigation artifacts |

```bash
entire investigate leaderboard-route-investigation.md --max-turns 1
entire investigate --findings
entire investigate show RUN_ID
entire investigate fix RUN_ID
entire investigate clean RUN_ID
```

"On first run, Entire may ask which agents should participate. Pick the agents configured for your Entire setup." — re-open the picker later with `entire investigate --edit`.

### The documented investigation workflow

The `learn/investigate-why-code-exists` tutorial chains them in this order: `entire blame FILE --line N` (who/what attribution) → `entire why FILE:N` (checkpoint, session, commit, original prompt) → `entire checkpoint explain ID --generate` (intent + outcome) → write a seed doc → `entire investigate SEED.md --max-turns 1` → `entire investigate --findings` → `entire investigate show RUN_ID`.

---

## 4. Entire Skills

### What they are

"[Skills](https://github.com/entireio/skills) are reusable agent workflows built around the Entire CLI." / "Skills tell your agent when and how to run Entire CLI commands, interpret the results, and turn them into useful answers or follow-up actions."

With Skills you can: run repeatable Entire CLI workflows through natural language; search Checkpoints and transcripts without manually building CLI commands; expand session context and summarize the parts that matter; trace code back to the session or commit that produced it; hand off work between agents.

"Skills do not capture data by themselves. They wrap the Entire CLI and use the history it records. For the best experience, start with a repository where Entire has already captured at least one agent session and Checkpoint."

### Install commands (exact, from `/learn/skills`)

**Claude Code:**

```text
/plugin marketplace add entireio/skills
/plugin install entire
```

**Codex:**

```bash
mkdir -p ~/.agents/skills
cd ~/.agents/skills
git clone https://github.com/entireio/skills.git entire
```

**Agent Skills:**

```bash
npx skills add https://github.com/entireio/skills --all
```

**Gemini CLI:**

```bash
gemini extensions install https://github.com/entireio/skills
```

Prerequisites: Git installed; a supported AI agent (Claude Code, Codex, Copilot CLI, Cursor, Factory, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, or Pi); the Entire CLI installed. Also be logged in (`entire login`) **in the environment where your agent runs**.

### The 11 documented Skills (from `/guides/skills/overview`)

| Skill | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `explain` | Trace a function, file, or line back to the session context that produced it using Git history and `entire checkpoint explain`. |
| `recall` | Find similar past work and turn the closest prior session into a task playbook. |
| `replay` | Step through a feature's Checkpoints chronologically so you can understand how it was built. |
| `review` | Review code changes with checkpoint transcript context before auditing the diff. |
| `search` | Find prior work, earlier prompts, Checkpoints, and similar implementations across recorded sessions. |
| `session-crosslink` | Attach a session to another Entire-enabled repo when the agent started outside the repo whose commits should record it. |
| `session-handoff` | Package recent session context so another agent can continue from prior work instead of starting from scratch. |
| `session-to-skill` | Turn Entire-tracked sessions, Checkpoints, or repeated agent workflows into reusable Skills. |
| `teach` | Build a topic-focused lesson from canonical Checkpoints instead of a broad repository overview. |
| `using-entire` | Route general codebase exploration through the right Entire Skill so answers are grounded in recorded history. |
| `what-happened` | Investigate why a specific code block changed by combining Git provenance with Entire session context. |

Each links to `https://github.com/entireio/skills/tree/main/skills/&lt;name&gt;`.

### Documented invocation prompts

`session-handoff`:
```text
hand off this session
hand off the codex session
prepare a handoff for another agent
```

`explain`:
```text
/explain src/main.ts
/explain parseConfig
Explain why auth.ts exists
```

`what-happened`:
```text
what happened here: src/app.ts:15-32
what happened at src/app.ts:21
tell me why this code is like this
```

`search`:
```text
search past work for rate limiting
find checkpoints about auth
have we done this before?
```

Slash-command note: "In clients that expose installed Skills as slash commands, you can also invoke the Skill directly with `/search`, followed by what you want to find."

### The `search` Skill contract

"Describe what you are looking for, and the Skill chooses the appropriate search, reviews the results, and summarizes the most relevant matches." / "Unlike a query you type into Search directly, requests to your agent can be phrased as instructions — the Skill translates them into effective search queries for you." / "The Skill summarizes the most relevant matches. For past work, it can inspect a checkpoint transcript when more context is needed. For source code, it returns file paths and matching snippets."

Example instructions:
```text
how did we decide to make history import explicit when enabling Entire?
find where the --import-history option is implemented
```

### Two-step cross-agent handoff (documented pattern)

Step 1, in the sending agent:
```text
Prepare a handoff for Claude Code. The next task is to add edit and delete actions for bug notes. Preserve the current vanilla JS structure and localStorage behavior.
```

Step 2, in the receiving agent:
```text
Continue this project from the handoff below.

Your next task:
- add edit and delete actions for bug notes
- keep the app in plain vanilla JS
- preserve the existing localStorage behavior
- keep the code simple and readable
- make one commit when the feature is complete

Handoff:
[paste the handoff summary here]
```

### Skills troubleshooting

- **Installed but not triggering** → check the repo is in the right discovery path for your tool, the tool supports Skills or plugins, and the prompts clearly invoke the skill.
- **`search`/`explain` returns no transcript** → code created outside an Entire-tracked session; not committed, or not pushed (required for `search`); Entire not enabled in the repo; incomplete auth or checkpoint config.
- **`search` feels underwhelming** → query too broad (`persistence`, `auth`); not enough prior sessions; earlier sessions lacked distinctive feature language. Fix by using specific queries like `duplicate bug detection` or `severity badge filter`, building 2–3 distinct features first, and committing/pushing after each.

**NOT DOCUMENTED on these pages:** the per-Skill internal prompt text, uninstall commands, or version pinning for the Skills repo.

---

## 5. `entire review` and cross-agent reviews

### Syntax

```bash
entire review [PROFILE] [flags]
```

"`entire review` starts reviewer agents in parallel, then uses a judge agent to consolidate their reports into a final verdict." / "Reviews are saved as named profiles in Entire settings. On first run, guided setup writes a profile and asks before starting agents."

### Full flag list (from `/cli-reference/review`)

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--configure` | Set up a review profile. Shows available agents and profiles unless paired with `--set-*` flags. |
| `--set-agents AGENTS` | With `--configure`, set comma-separated reviewer agents for the profile. |
| `--set-judge AGENT[=MODEL]` | With `--configure`, set the consolidating judge. |
| `--set-output local` | With `--configure`, deliver the verdict locally. Defaults to `local`. |
| `--local` | With `--configure`, save to `.entire/settings.local.json` instead of shared `.entire/settings.json`. |
| `--set-task TEXT` | With `--configure`, set the profile's canonical task text. |
| `--set-model AGENT=MODEL` | With `--configure`, set a model for a reviewer agent. Repeatable. |
| `--set-slot AGENT[=MODEL]` | With `--configure`, set one reviewer slot. Repeatable; the same agent or model can appear more than once. |
| `--edit` | Reopen the advanced review profile skill picker. |
| `--findings` | Browse local review findings. |
| `--agent NAME` | Run one reviewer from the selected profile. |
| `--agents` | List reviewer agents available for the selected profile. |
| `--model NAME` | Override the model for the selected `--agent` reviewer. Requires `--agent`. |
| `--models` | List the models each review agent advertises. Optionally combine with `--agent NAME`. |
| `--profile NAME` | Select a review profile. You can also pass the profile name as the positional argument. |
| `--prompt TEXT` | Add one-off instructions for this review run. |
| `--timeout DURATION` | Maximum time each reviewer may run before being marked failed. Defaults to `10m0s`; `0` disables the timeout. |
| `--base REF` | Scope the review against a Git ref instead of the default mainline ref. Defaults to the first existing ref from `origin/HEAD`, `origin/main`, `origin/master`, `main`, or `master`. |
| `--list` | List configured review profiles, reviewers, judges, and output modes. |
| `-h, --help` | Show help for `entire review`. |

Note: "`entire review --findings` only browses local findings. It does not apply fixes."

### Documented examples

```bash
entire review --configure
entire review --configure --set-agents codex,claude-code --set-judge codex --set-output local
entire review --configure --local
entire review --configure --local --set-agents codex --set-judge codex --set-output local
entire review general
entire review --profile general
entire review general --base origin/main
entire review general --base origin/my-parent-branch
entire review general --prompt "Focus on auth, error handling, and missing tests."
entire review general --prompt "Focus on risky state transitions and missing tests."
entire review general --agent codex
entire review --findings
entire review --list
entire review --agents
entire review --edit
```

"This creates the default `general` profile unless you choose another profile name in the interactive setup."

### Attaching an existing session as a review

```bash
entire session attach SESSION_ID --review
```

### Review profiles config

- Shared: `entire review --configure` saves to `.entire/settings.json` by default. "Commit `.entire/settings.json` when the review profile should apply to everyone working in the repository."
- Local: `--local` saves to `.entire/settings.local.json`. "Local settings have higher priority than shared project settings."

Setting paths managed by `--configure`:

| Setting path | Type | Use for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `review_default_profile` | string | The default review profile name |
| `review_profiles` | object | Named review profiles and their reviewer, judge, output, task, model, and slot settings |

"Use `entire review --configure` or `entire review --edit` to update review profiles when possible. The profile JSON can contain nested reviewer and model settings, so the CLI is the safest way to keep the shape valid." If a review uses the wrong agents or output mode, check whether a local profile is overriding the shared one.

### Setting up a second review agent (from the tutorial)

```bash
entire enable -y --agent codex
entire agent add claude-code
entire agent list
entire agent add AGENT_NAME
```

### The documented review loop

1. Build with an agent
2. Commit and push useful chunks of work
3. Use `entire recap` to understand what happened
4. Use `entire review` to review the branch before merging
5. Fix the findings that matter

Reading the verdict: "Entire shows each reviewer's result and then consolidates the reports into a final verdict." Compare which issues multiple agents found, which were unique to one reviewer, which are blockers before merge, and which are follow-up work.

Commit-linking prompt seen during this flow:
```text
Link this commit to session context?
  [Y]es / [n]o / [a]lways (remember my choice): a
```

**Note the name collision:** there is both a `review` **Skill** (agent workflow: "Review code changes with checkpoint transcript context before auditing the diff") and the `entire review` **CLI command** (multi-agent reviewers + judge). The docs do not state how, or whether, they interact.

---

## 6. How an agent calls Entire — the `entire agent-help` contract

### Purpose

"Show coding agents which Entire command to use and retrieve exact usage for the installed CLI." / "Unlike static command documentation, `agent-help` generates its output from the installed Entire binary. This helps agents avoid guessing commands or flags that may differ between CLI versions."

**The authority rule, verbatim:** "`entire agent-help` describes the CLI installed on your machine. Use this command as the source of truth when its output differs from static documentation."

### Syntax

```bash
entire agent-help [COMMAND...] [flags]
```

With no command:

```bash
entire agent-help
```

"prints a high-level map of available commands and when to use them. The output also identifies the repository detected from the Git remote. You do not need to provide the repository name when working in the current repository."

### Per-command help

```bash
entire agent-help status
entire agent-help checkpoint explain
entire agent-help session resume
```

"Pass a command or nested command path to see its description, subcommands, and currently installed flags. The command map only includes commands available in the current installation and repository. Commands that are deprecated, internal, or unavailable for the repository are omitted."

### Structured output

```bash
entire agent-help --json
entire agent-help checkpoint explain --json
```

"The JSON response includes the command name, short and long descriptions, detected repository, subcommands, and flags when applicable."

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--json` | Emit structured JSON instead of text |
| `--help`, `-h` | Show help for `agent-help` |

### The practical agent contract, as assembled from the docs

1. Be logged in in the environment where the agent runs (`entire login`).
2. Discover commands and flags for the *installed* binary with `entire agent-help` / `entire agent-help --json`, rather than relying on static docs — repo detection comes from the Git remote, so no repo argument is needed in-repo.
3. Drill into a specific command with `entire agent-help COMMAND [SUBCOMMAND] [--json]`.
4. Run the command with `--json` where supported (`search`, `why`, `blame`, `checkpoint search`) — the troubleshooting page specifically says the search Skill requires the agent to be able to run `entire search --json`, and "Redirected or piped output is JSON automatically" for history search.
5. Install the Skills repo so the agent has the wrapped workflows (`/plugin marketplace add entireio/skills` + `/plugin install entire` for Claude Code).

**NOT DOCUMENTED:** any MCP server, HTTP API, or non-CLI programmatic interface for agents; the literal text/JSON schema of `agent-help` output (only the fields it "includes" are listed); whether `agent-help` output is stable across versions.

---

## Global flags

| Flag | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--help`, `-h` | Show help for entire or any subcommand |
| `--version`, `-v` | Show build information (same as `entire version`) |

---

## Version and capability notes worth flagging to the team

- `entire search` was **hidden from `entire --help` in 0.9.0 and earlier** but still runnable; `entire checkpoint search` works in every version.
- `entire blame` / `entire why` require **0.7.7+**.
- `entire investigate` is **experimental / labs**.
- `entire checkpoint rewind` is **deprecated and will be removed in a future release**; `--reset` is destructive.
- `--case-sensitive` on search is **accepted but not applied by the backend**.
- Dispatches in Entire.io may be **gradually enabled** per account.

---

## Explicit gaps — things the requested pages do NOT answer

- **Index freshness**: only "a couple minutes" after commit+push; no guaranteed latency, no index-status command documented.
- **Code-search corpus rules**: which branches, file types, or size limits are indexed is never stated.
- **`recap`/`dispatch` machine output**: no `--json` documented for either.
- **`--voice` preset list**: only `neutral` and `marvin` are named in the CLI docs (`custom` is a web-UI option).
- **Skill internals**: no per-Skill flag/prompt spec, no uninstall or update commands on these pages.
- **`entire review` output location**: `--set-output local` is documented and "Defaults to `local`", but no other output mode is named, and the on-disk path of saved findings is not given.
- **Relationship between the `review` Skill and `entire review`**: not described anywhere in the fetched pages.
- **`agent-help` output schema**: fields are listed but no example payload is shown.</result>
<usage><subagent_tokens>67790</subagent_tokens><tool_uses>14</tool_uses><duration_ms>244634</duration_ms></usage>
</task-notification>

# Entire CLI Reference

**Verified against:** `Entire CLI 0.10.2` (go1.26.6, darwin/arm64), `/opt/homebrew/bin/entire`
**Docs source:** `https://docs.entire.io/cli-reference/*.md`

Where the published docs and the installed binary disagree, **the binary is authoritative** and the difference is called out in a `⚠️ Docs vs 0.10.2` block.

---

## Global Flags

Available on the root command and inherited by every subcommand.

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for entire |
| `-v`, `--version` | — | — | version for entire |
| `--context` | `name` | — | Act as this saved login name for this command only, instead of the active context (`entire auth contexts` lists them) |

Root usage:

```bash
entire [flags]
entire [command]
```

**Environment variables** (from root help):

| Variable | Description |
|---|---|
| `ACCESSIBLE` | Set to any value (e.g. `ACCESSIBLE=1`) to enable accessibility mode. Uses simpler text prompts instead of interactive TUI elements, which works better with screen readers. |

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — `global-flags.md` documents only `--help` and `--version`. The installed binary also exposes a third global flag, **`--context name`**, which appears under "Global Flags" on every subcommand. It is entirely absent from the docs page.

---

## `entire activity`

Display your activity overview, repository breakdown, and recent sessions from entire.io.

```bash
entire activity [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--commits` | — | off | Show recent commits instead of recent sessions |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for activity |

The recent list shows your **sessions** by default, matching the entire.io Overview page. Pass `--commits` to show recent commits instead.

Examples (from the docs page):

```bash
entire login
entire activity
ACCESSIBLE=1 entire activity
```

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — two differences:
&gt; 1. `activity.md` lists **no flags at all**. The binary has `--commits`.
&gt; 2. The docs describe the output as "recent commits from the last month, grouped by day", i.e. commits are the default. In 0.10.2 the default is **recent sessions**; commits require `--commits`. The docs describe the pre-`--commits` behavior.

---

## `entire agent`

Manage agent integrations in this repository.

```bash
entire agent [flags]
entire agent [command]
```

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `add` | Install hooks for an agent |
| `list` | List installed and available agents |
| `remove` | Uninstall hooks for an agent |

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for agent |

Examples:

```bash
entire agent
entire agent list
entire agent add claude-code
entire agent remove claude-code
```

### `entire agent add`

```bash
entire agent add &lt;agent-name&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--agent-help-skill` | — | off | Install the stable Entire agent-help skill (points agents at `entire agent-help`) |
| `--force` | — | off | Reinstall hooks even if already present |
| `--search-skill` | — | off | Install the optional Entire search skill |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for add |

```bash
entire agent add claude-code
entire agent add gemini
```

### `entire agent list`

```bash
entire agent list [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for list |

### `entire agent remove`

```bash
entire agent remove &lt;agent-name&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |

```bash
entire agent remove claude-code
```

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — `agent.md` documents `agent add` as having `--force` and **`--local-dev`**. There is **no `--local-dev` flag in 0.10.2**. The binary instead has `--agent-help-skill` and `--search-skill`, neither of which appears in the docs.

## `entire agent-help`

Prints agent-facing usage for the Entire CLI, generated live from the installed command tree so it always matches this binary. With no arguments it prints a high-level map of when to use `entire` and which subcommand; pass a command path (e.g. `agent-help checkpoint`) to see that command's exact, current flags.

```bash
entire agent-help [command...] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | off | Emit structured JSON instead of text |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for agent-help |

Examples (from the docs page):

```bash
entire agent-help status
entire agent-help checkpoint explain
entire agent-help session resume
entire agent-help --json
entire agent-help checkpoint explain --json
```

**Note (docs):** Use this command as the source of truth when its output differs from static documentation.

&gt; ✅ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — no discrepancy. Flags and usage match. This is the recommended way to resolve any doubt about the other commands below, since it is generated from the installed binary.

## `entire session attach`

&gt; ⚠️ **Path discrepancy — read first.** The docs page is served at `cli-reference/attach.md`, but there is **no top-level `entire attach` command**. `entire attach --help` fails with `Error: Invalid usage: unknown command "attach" for "entire"`. The real command is **`entire session attach`**, which is what the page body actually documents.

Attach an existing agent session that wasn't captured by hooks. This creates a checkpoint from the session's transcript and links it to the last commit. Use this when hooks failed to fire or weren't installed when the session started, or to attach a research session.

If the last commit already has a checkpoint, the session is added to it. Otherwise a new checkpoint is created.

Use `--review` to tag the attached session as an agent review. The first user prompt in the transcript is recorded as the review prompt. Pass `--skills` to declare which skills were actually run; omit to attach a review without a declared skills list.

Works with any registered agent, including external agents enabled via `external_agents` in settings. Run `entire agent list` to see the full list. If `--agent` doesn't locate a transcript, Entire auto-detects the agent from the transcript and prints the detected agent name.

```bash
entire session attach &lt;session-id&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-a`, `--agent` | `string` | `"claude-code"` | Agent that created the session (see `entire agent list` for registered agents, including external) |
| `-f`, `--force` | — | off | Skip confirmation and amend the last commit with the checkpoint trailer (best-effort; if the amend fails the checkpoint is still created and the trailer is printed for manual paste) |
| `--review` | — | off | Tag the attached session as an agent review |
| `--skills` | `strings` | — | Optional: declare which review skills were run in this session. Only used with `--review` |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for attach |

Examples (from the docs page):

```bash
# Attach a Codex session and amend HEAD automatically
entire session attach 019d6c43-1537-7343-9691-1f8cee04fe59 -a codex -f

# Attach a Claude Code session using the default agent
entire session attach 9b1deb4d-3b7d-4bad-9bdd-2b0d7b3dcbfe

# Attach a Codex session as a review
entire session attach 019d6c43-1537-7343-9691-1f8cee04fe59 -a codex --review
```

**Note (docs):** `entire session attach` requires the repository to already have at least one commit. If Entire cannot find the transcript for the agent you specify, it will try to auto-detect the correct agent from known transcript locations.

&gt; ✅ **Flags match.** The only discrepancy is the page's URL/command path (`attach.md` → `entire session attach`). Every documented flag exists with the documented spelling and default.

---

## `entire auth`

Authentication subcommands. Includes login, logout, status, and login-context management (contexts, use).

```bash
entire auth [flags]
entire auth [command]
```

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `contexts` | List stored login contexts |
| `login` | Log in to Entire |
| `logout` | Log out of Entire |
| `status` | Show authentication status |
| `token` | Print an Entire bearer token — a live credential, treat as a secret |
| `use` | Switch the active login context |

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for auth |

### `entire auth login`

```bash
entire auth login [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--device` | — | off | Use the device-code flow (enter a code in your browser) instead of the default browser redirect |
| `--server` | `string` | `"https://auth.entire.io"` | login server to authenticate against (rarely needed; the default serves all standard accounts) |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for login |

### `entire auth logout`

By default this ends the active session only (server-side) and removes the active login from this machine. Other saved logins (contexts) remain and can still authenticate `git clone entire://…` against clusters fronted by their login server. Without `--all-contexts`, logging out promotes the next saved login (if any) to active, so running `entire logout` repeatedly drains every saved login in turn.

```bash
entire auth logout [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all-contexts` | — | off | Log out of every saved login (context), not just the active one |
| `--everywhere` | — | off | Revoke every session server-side, not just the current one |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for logout |

### `entire auth status`

```bash
entire auth status [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for status |

### `entire auth contexts`

```bash
entire auth contexts [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for contexts |

### `entire auth use`

Switch the active login context. The active context is the identity for every authenticated operation: `git clone entire://…`, the control-plane commands (`auth status`, `org`/`project`/`repo`/`grant`), and the data-API commands (`activity`, `search`, `trail`, `dispatch`). The switch takes effect on the next operation.

This is persistent and machine-wide — it changes the identity for every shell, worktree, and background git hook until you switch back. To act as another login for a single command instead, pass `--context`, or set `ENTIRE_CONTEXT` for one shell or one git operation.

```bash
entire auth use &lt;context&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for use |

```bash
entire --context staging activity
ENTIRE_CONTEXT=staging git push
```

Activity/search/dispatch take their host from `ENTIRE_API_BASE_URL`; trail commands route to the repository's owning cell. The selected context supplies the identity.

### `entire auth token`

Print an Entire bearer token to stdout so scripts and ad-hoc curl can authenticate without plumbing auth themselves. By default it prints the control-plane bearer (`ENTIRE_TOKEN` verbatim when set, otherwise the active context's login JWT, refreshed if near expiry). With `--jurisdiction &lt;slug&gt;` it instead mints a jurisdictional identity token for that jurisdiction's entire-api cells, which reject the control-plane bearer.

**The output is a live credential — treat it as a secret.** Only the token is printed to stdout; errors and the not-logged-in hint go to stderr so command substitution stays clean.

```bash
entire auth token [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-j`, `--jurisdiction` | `string` | — | mint a jurisdictional identity token for this jurisdiction slug (e.g. `us`, `eu`) for use against that jurisdiction's entire-api cells |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for token |

```bash
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $(entire auth token)" "https://us.console.entire.io/api/v1/clusters"
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $(entire auth token --jurisdiction us)" "https://aws-us-east-2.api.entire.io/api/v1/me/activity"
```

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2 — this is the worst-drifted page.**
&gt; - `auth.md` documents **`entire auth list`** ("List active API tokens") and **`entire auth revoke [TOKEN_ID] --current`**. **Neither exists in 0.10.2.** Both invocations silently fall through to the parent `entire auth` help. Do not use them; the `--current` flag does not exist.
&gt; - Three subcommands present in the binary are **missing from the docs entirely**: `contexts`, `use`, and `token` (including `-j/--jurisdiction`).
&gt; - `auth login` in the binary has `--device` and `--server`; the docs list no flags for it.
&gt; - `auth logout` in the binary has `--everywhere` and `--all-contexts`; the docs list no flags for it.

## `entire blame`

Show git-blame-style line attribution enriched with Entire checkpoint metadata. Limit to a line or range with `&lt;file&gt;:12`, `&lt;file&gt;:12-20`, or the `--line` flag.

```bash
entire blame &lt;file&gt;[:line[-line]] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | off | Output attribution as JSON |
| `--line` | `string` | — | Only show a line or range, for example `12` or `12-20` |
| `--long` | — | off | Show the full attribution table with agent, model, author, and session columns |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for blame |

Examples (installed binary):

```bash
entire blame src/auth.go
entire blame src/auth.go:10-40 --json
```

Examples (docs page):

```bash
entire blame overview.mdx
entire blame overview.mdx --line 12-20
entire blame overview.mdx --line 12-20 --long
entire blame overview.mdx --json
```

**Attribution tags** (docs page only — not repeated in `--help`):

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `AI` | the commit was attributed to an AI agent session |
| `MX` | the commit mixed AI-attributed and human-attributed work |
| `HU` | the commit was made without AI-attributed checkpoint data |

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2 (minor)** — flags match exactly. The docs give the usage line as `entire blame FILE [flags]` and omit the inline `&lt;file&gt;:line[-line]` form that the binary documents and supports. The `AI`/`MX`/`HU` tag legend appears only in the docs, not in `--help`.

## `entire checkpoint`

Operations on checkpoints — the persistent records of agent work tied to commits.

```bash
entire checkpoint [command]
```

**Aliases:** `checkpoint`, `cp`, `checkpoints`

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `explain` | Explain a session, commit, or checkpoint |
| `list` | List checkpoints on the current branch |
| `search` | Search checkpoints, commits, and sessions using semantic and keyword matching |
| `tokens` | Show token usage and optimization recommendations for a checkpoint |

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for checkpoint |

Examples:

```bash
entire checkpoint list
entire checkpoint explain &lt;id|sha&gt;
entire checkpoint tokens &lt;id&gt;
entire checkpoint search "fix login"
```

### `entire checkpoint list`

By default shows condensed checkpoints from the checkpoints branch for the current branch. Use `--pending` to list the live session's shadow-branch rewind points instead (task checkpoints, logs-only points, condensation IDs).

```bash
entire checkpoint list [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | off | Output as JSON instead of the human view |
| `--no-pager` | — | off | Disable pager output |
| `--pending` | — | off | List the live session's shadow-branch rewind points instead of condensed checkpoints |
| `--session` | `string` | — | Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix) |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for list |

Output modes: `--json` (machine-readable), `--pending` (live shadow-branch rewind points), `--pending --json` (rewind points as JSON — replaces the deprecated `rewind --list`). `--session` is not applicable with `--pending`.

### `entire checkpoint explain`

```bash
entire checkpoint explain [checkpoint-id | commit-sha] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-c`, `--checkpoint` | `string` | — | Explain a specific checkpoint (ID or prefix) |
| `--commit` | `string` | — | Explain a specific commit (SHA or ref, "commit-ish") |
| `--force` | — | off | Regenerate summary even if one already exists (requires `--generate`) |
| `--full` | — | off | Show full parsed transcript (all prompts/responses) |
| `--generate` | — | off | Generate an AI summary for the checkpoint |
| `--insecure-http-auth` | — | off | Allow plain-HTTP auth for `--repo` (local dev only) |
| `--json` | — | off | Output metadata as JSON (no transcript bytes) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `100` | Cap the list view at N checkpoints. Only meaningful with `--json`. |
| `--no-pager` | — | off | Disable pager output |
| `--raw-transcript` | — | off | Show raw transcript file (JSONL format) |
| `--repo` | `string` | — | Explain a checkpoint owned by another repo (`owner/name` or `gh/owner/name`), read from that repo's Entire API |
| `--search-all` | — | off | Search all commits (no branch/depth limit, may be slow) |
| `--session` | `string` | — | Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix) |
| `--session-index` | `int` | `-1` | Session index within a multi-session checkpoint (0-based, defaults to latest) |
| `-s`, `--short` | — | off | Show summary only (omit prompts and files) |
| `--summary-timeout-seconds` | `int` | `0` | Hard deadline in seconds for `--generate` summary generation; overrides the `summary_timeout_seconds` setting. `0` = use setting; if setting is also unset or 0, no automatic deadline applies. |
| `--transcript` | — | off | Stream stored checkpoint transcript bytes to stdout |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for explain |

```bash
entire checkpoint explain &lt;id-or-sha&gt;
entire checkpoint explain --checkpoint &lt;id&gt;
entire checkpoint explain --commit &lt;ref&gt;
entire checkpoint explain &lt;id&gt; --repo owner/name
```

**Note:** `--session` filters the list view; the positional arg, `--commit`, and `--checkpoint` are mutually exclusive. `--repo` requires a full checkpoint ID (positional or `--checkpoint`) and a checkpoint that has been pushed; nothing is written to the local repo.

### `entire checkpoint search`

Search checkpoints, commits, and sessions using hybrid search (semantic + keyword), powered by the Entire search service. Requires authentication via `entire login`. By default, results are scoped to the current repository.

```bash
entire checkpoint search [query] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all-repos` | — | off | Search all accessible repos instead of just the current one |
| `--author` | `string` | — | Filter by author name |
| `--branch` | `string` | — | Filter by branch name |
| `--case-sensitive` | — | off | Case-sensitive code search (only with `--code`) |
| `--code` | — | off | Search code content across repositories |
| `--compact` | — | off | Trimmed JSON output for agents: id, repo, files touched, score, match snippet, and a truncated title instead of the full prompt (implies `--json`) |
| `--date` | `string` | — | Filter by time period (`week` or `month`) |
| `--json` | — | off | Output as JSON |
| `--limit` | `int` | `10` | Maximum number of results (per page for checkpoint search, total for `--code`) |
| `--page` | `int` | `1` | Page number (1-based) |
| `--repo` | `strings` | — | Filter by repository (`gh/owner/repo`, `et/proj/repo`, `owner/repo`, ULID, or `*`); repeatable and comma-separated for multiple repos |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for search |

```bash
entire checkpoint search "retry backoff" --json
entire checkpoint search "retry backoff" --json --compact
entire checkpoint search "auth timeout author:alice date:week"
entire checkpoint search --code "parseToken"
```

CLI queries also support inline filters like `author:&lt;name&gt;`, `date:&lt;week|month&gt;`, `branch:&lt;name&gt;`, `repo:&lt;owner/name&gt;`, and `repo:*` to search all accessible repos.

### `entire checkpoint tokens`

Show token usage and optimization recommendations for a checkpoint. Checkpoint IDs may be abbreviated as long as the prefix is unambiguous; positional targets may also resolve from a commit ref with an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.

```bash
entire checkpoint tokens &lt;checkpoint-id&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--agent-brief` | — | off | Output compact next-step guidance for agents |
| `--compare` | `string` | — | Compare against a baseline checkpoint ID |
| `--json` | — | off | Output as JSON |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for tokens |

```bash
entire checkpoint tokens a1b2
entire checkpoint tokens a1b2 --compare c3d4
entire checkpoint tokens a1b2 --json
```

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2 — heavy drift.**
&gt; - **`entire checkpoint rewind` is gone.** `checkpoint.md` documents it (marked deprecated) with `--to`, `--list`, `--logs-only`, `--reset`. In 0.10.2 the subcommand and all four flags **no longer exist** — `entire checkpoint rewind --help` falls through to the parent help. `checkpoint list --pending --json` is the documented replacement for `rewind --list`.
&gt; - **`entire checkpoint tokens` is undocumented.** It exists in the binary with `--agent-brief`, `--compare`, `--json`.
&gt; - `checkpoint list` gained `--json` and `--pending` in the binary; the docs list only `--no-pager` and `--session`.
&gt; - `checkpoint explain` in the binary has 9 flags the docs omit: `--insecure-http-auth`, `--json`, `--limit`, `--repo`, `--session-index`, `--summary-timeout-seconds`, `--transcript` (plus the `--repo` cross-repo workflow).
&gt; - `checkpoint search`: the docs omit `--all-repos`, `--code`, `--case-sensitive`, and `--compact`; give no defaults for `--limit` (binary: `10`) or `--page` (binary: `1`); and describe `--repo` as a single `OWNER/REPO` value when the binary accepts a repeatable/comma-separated `strings` list with more ID forms.
&gt; - Aliases `cp` and `checkpoints` are undocumented.

## `entire clean`

Clean up Entire session data for the current HEAD commit.

By default, cleans session state and shadow branches for the current HEAD:
- Session state files (`.git/entire-sessions/&lt;session-id&gt;.json`)
- Shadow branch (`entire/&lt;commit-hash&gt;-&lt;worktree-hash&gt;`)

Use `--all` to clean all Entire session data across the repository:
- All session state files (`.git/entire-sessions/`)
- All shadow branches
- Temporary files (`.entire/tmp/`)

Without `--force`, prompts for confirmation before deleting. Use `--dry-run` to preview what would be deleted without prompting.

```bash
entire clean [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-a`, `--all` | — | off | Clean all session data across the repository |
| `-d`, `--dry-run` | — | off | Preview what would be deleted without deleting |
| `-f`, `--force` | — | off | Skip confirmation prompt and override active session guard |
| `--session` | `string` | current HEAD | Clean a specific session by ID |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for clean |

Examples (from the docs page):

```bash
entire clean --dry-run
entire clean --session 2026-02-02-test123 --force
entire clean --all --force
```

**Notes (docs):** `--session ID` removes the session state and deletes its shadow branch if no other session needs it; file changes remain in the working directory. `--all` preserves the permanent `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch. `entire reset` is deprecated — use `entire clean`, or `entire clean --all` for repository-wide cleanup.

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2 (minor)** — the docs list `--dry-run` with no short form. The binary also accepts **`-d`**. All other flags match.

---

## `entire configure`

Update non-agent Entire settings in the current repository. Manages telemetry, git-hook installation mode, strategy options, and summary provider configuration. Agent installation is handled by `entire agent`.

```bash
entire configure [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--absolute-git-hook-path` | — | off | Embed full binary path in git hooks (for GUI git clients that don't source shell profiles) |
| `--checkpoint-backend` | `string` | — | Checkpoint storage backend: `refs` (one git ref per checkpoint; recommended) or `branch` (shared `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch) |
| `--checkpoint-remote` | `string` | — | Checkpoint remote in `provider:owner/repo` format (e.g. `github:org/checkpoints-repo`) |
| `-f`, `--force` | — | off | Reinstall the Entire git hook |
| `--local` | — | off | Write settings to `.entire/settings.local.json` instead of `.entire/settings.json` |
| `--project` | — | off | Write settings to `.entire/settings.json` even if it already exists |
| `--skip-push-sessions` | — | off | Disable automatic pushing of session logs on git push |
| `--summarize-model` | `string` | — | Set the model hint used by `explain --generate` |
| `--summarize-provider` | `string` | — | Set the provider used by `explain --generate` (e.g. `claude-code`, `codex`, `gemini`, `pi`, `cursor`, `copilot-cli`) |
| `--summarize-timeout-seconds` | `int` | — | Set the hard deadline (seconds) for `explain --generate` summary generation. `0` clears (falls back to 5m default). |
| `--telemetry` | — | `true` | Enable anonymous usage analytics |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for configure |

Examples:

```bash
entire configure                                # Show this help
entire configure --telemetry=false              # Opt out of telemetry
entire configure --absolute-git-hook-path       # Reinstall git hook with absolute path
entire configure --force                        # Reinstall git hook
entire configure --checkpoint-remote github:org/checkpoints
entire configure --checkpoint-backend refs      # Store each checkpoint as its own git ref
entire configure --summarize-provider claude-code
entire configure --summarize-timeout-seconds 300   # 5m deadline for explain --generate
```

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — two differences:
&gt; 1. **`--summarize-timeout-seconds` is missing from the docs.** It exists in 0.10.2 and takes an `int`.
&gt; 2. The docs state `--checkpoint-backend` defaults to **`branch`**. The binary's help text states no default and explicitly marks **`refs` as recommended**. Do not rely on the docs' claimed default — set it explicitly.

## `entire disable`

Disable Entire integrations in the current repository. By default, this command will disable Entire; hooks will exit silently and commands will show a disabled message.

To completely remove Entire integrations from this repository, use `--uninstall`, which removes:
- `.entire/` directory (settings, logs, metadata)
- Git hooks (`prepare-commit-msg`, `commit-msg`, `post-commit`, `pre-push`)
- Session state files (`.git/entire-sessions/`)
- Shadow branches (`entire/&lt;hash&gt;`)
- Agent hooks

```bash
entire disable [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--force` | — | off | Skip confirmation prompt (use with `--uninstall`) |
| `--local` | — | on (default target) | Update `.entire/settings.local.json` (the default) instead of `.entire/settings.json` |
| `--project` | — | off | Update `.entire/settings.json` instead of `.entire/settings.local.json` |
| `--uninstall` | — | off | Completely remove Entire from this repository |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for disable |

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — `disable.md` lists only `--force`, `--project`, `--uninstall`. The binary also has **`--local`**, and its help clarifies that `.entire/settings.local.json` is the **default** write target (the docs never say which file is written by default). The docs page provides no examples.

## `entire dispatch`

Generate a dispatch summarizing recent agent work.

```bash
entire dispatch [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--agent` | `string` | — | local text-generation agent (requires `--local`) |
| `--all-branches` | — | off | include every existing local branch (`--local` only; renamed or deleted branches are skipped) |
| `--local` | — | off | generate via the locally-installed agent CLI instead of the Entire server |
| `--repos` | `strings` | — | cloud repo slugs, up to 5 (for example `entireio/cli`) |
| `--since` | `string` | `"7d"` | time window (Go duration, relative time, or ISO date) |
| `--until` | `string` | now | window end time (defaults to now) |
| `--voice` | `string` | — | voice preset name or literal description |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for dispatch |

Examples (installed binary):

```bash
entire dispatch
entire dispatch --local --all-branches
entire dispatch --local --agent codex
entire dispatch --repos entireio/cli
entire dispatch --voice neutral
```

Examples (docs page):

```bash
entire dispatch
entire dispatch --repos entireio/cli,entireio/entire.io
entire dispatch --local
entire dispatch --local --all-branches
entire dispatch --voice neutral
```

**Constraints (docs):** `--repos` cannot be combined with `--local`. `--all-branches` only applies to `--local`. Cloud dispatch supports a maximum of 5 repos per request using `owner/repo` format.

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — `dispatch.md` omits **`--agent string`** (`local text-generation agent (requires --local)`), which exists in the binary and appears in its own examples (`entire dispatch --local --agent codex`). All other flags and the `--since` default of `7d` match.

## `entire doctor`

Scan for session issues and offer to fix them.

**Checks performed:**
1. **Disconnected metadata branches** — detects when local and remote `entire/checkpoints/v1` branches share no common ancestor (caused by a previous bug). Fixes by cherry-picking local checkpoints onto remote tip.
2. **Codex hook trust** (when Codex hooks are installed) — warns when hooks declared in `.codex/hooks.json` lack a `trusted_hash` entry in the user's Codex config.
3. **Hook config** (per installed agent reporting drift) — warns when installed hooks no longer match what this CLI writes. Fix by re-running `entire enable --force`.
4. **Stuck sessions** — sessions stuck in ACTIVE or ENDED phase that need cleanup.

A session is considered stuck if it is in ACTIVE phase with no interaction for over 1 hour, or in ENDED phase with uncondensed checkpoint data on a shadow branch. For each stuck session you can **Condense** (save to permanent storage), **Discard** (remove session state and shadow branch data), or **Skip**.

```bash
entire doctor [flags]
entire doctor [command]
```

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `bundle` | Produce a diagnostic bundle (zip) for bug reports — secrets are redacted by default |
| `logs` | Show recent operational logs |
| `migrate-checkpoints` | Convert git-branch checkpoints into per-checkpoint git refs (git-refs store) |
| `trace` | Show hook performance traces |

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-f`, `--force` | — | off | Auto-fix all issues without prompting |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for doctor |

`--force` condenses all fixable sessions without prompting; sessions that can't be condensed will be discarded.

### `entire doctor logs`

Print operational logs from `.entire/logs/entire.log`.

```bash
entire doctor logs [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-f`, `--follow` | — | off | Stream new log lines as they are written |
| `--tail` | `int` | `100` | Show the last N lines (use `0` for all) |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for logs |

### `entire doctor bundle`

Produce a zip archive containing logs, settings, and a git snapshot suitable for attaching to bug reports. The archive includes `logs/`, `settings/settings.json` and `settings/settings.local.json` (if present), `git-status.txt`, `git-log.txt`, `git-remote.txt`, and `version.txt` with CLI version, Go version, OS/Arch.

By default the bundle redacts known secrets (API keys, credentialed URIs, database connection strings, bounded `KEY=value` credentials) from log files, settings JSON, and git command output before zipping.

```bash
entire doctor bundle [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-o`, `--out` | `string` | OS temp dir | Path to write the bundle archive |
| `--raw` | — | off | Skip secret redaction. The archive will contain raw log lines, settings, and git output. Use only when support has asked for an unredacted bundle. |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for bundle |

### `entire doctor trace`

Show timing information for recent hook invocations. Traces are emitted at DEBUG log level. To enable them, set `ENTIRE_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` in your shell profile, or add `"log_level": "DEBUG"` to `.entire/settings.json`.

```bash
entire doctor trace [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--hook` | `string` | — | Filter by hook type (e.g. `post-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg`, `pre-push`) |
| `--last` | `int` | `1` | Show last N hook invocations |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for trace |

```bash
entire doctor trace                     # Show the most recent hook trace
entire doctor trace --last 5            # Show the last 5 hook traces
entire doctor trace --hook post-commit  # Show only post-commit hook traces
```

### `entire doctor migrate-checkpoints`

Convert the checkpoints stored on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch into per-checkpoint refs under `refs/entire/checkpoints/&lt;shard&gt;/&lt;id&gt;`, the layout the git-refs checkpoint store uses. The command is idempotent: checkpoints already converted are skipped, so it is safe to re-run.

New refs are queued for push. Run interactively, it asks whether to push them now; non-interactively it never pushes — the refs stay queued and flush on the next push once the git-refs store is the configured primary.

```bash
entire doctor migrate-checkpoints [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--dry-run` | — | off | Report what would be migrated without writing refs |
| `--remote` | `string` | the checkpoint sync remote | Remote to push migrated refs to |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for migrate-checkpoints |

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2** — `doctor.md` documents only `logs`, `bundle`, and `trace`. **`entire doctor migrate-checkpoints` is undocumented** (with `--dry-run` and `--remote`). The docs also describe `doctor` as scanning only for "stuck or problematic sessions"; 0.10.2 performs four distinct checks including disconnected metadata branches, Codex hook trust, and hook-config drift. All documented subcommand flags and defaults (`--tail 100`, `--last 1`, `-o/--out`, `--raw`, `--hook`, `-f/--follow`) match the binary.

---

## `entire enable`

Enable Entire with session tracking for your AI agent workflows. If Entire is not yet configured, this runs the full configuration flow. If Entire is already configured but disabled, this re-enables it. If the current directory is not a git repository, Entire can initialize one for you and optionally create a matching GitHub repository via the `gh` CLI.

```bash
entire enable [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--absolute-git-hook-path` | — | off | Embed full binary path in git hooks (for GUI git clients that don't source shell profiles) |
| `--agent` | `string` | — | Agent to set up hooks for (e.g. `claude-code`, `codex`, `copilot-cli`, `cursor`, `factoryai-droid`, `gemini`, `opencode`, `pi`; external agents on `$PATH` are also available). Enables non-interactive mode. |
| `--agent-help-skill` | — | off | Install the stable Entire agent-help skill (points agents at `entire agent-help`) for selected agent(s) |
| `--checkpoint-backend` | `string` | — | Checkpoint storage backend: `refs` (one git ref per checkpoint; recommended) or `branch` (shared `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch) |
| `--checkpoint-remote` | `string` | — | Checkpoint remote in `provider:owner/repo` format (e.g. `github:org/checkpoints-repo`) |
| `-f`, `--force` | — | off | Force reinstall hooks (removes existing Entire hooks first) |
| `--import-history` | — | off | During first-time setup, import the selected agents' existing session history (last 30 days) without prompting |
| `--init-repo` | — | off | If not a git repo, initialize one non-interactively |
| `--initial-commit-message` | `string` | — | Commit message for the initial commit when bootstrapping a new repo |
| `--local` | — | off | Write settings to `.entire/settings.local.json` instead of `.entire/settings.json` |
| `--no-github` | — | off | Initialize local git repo only; skip creating a GitHub remote |
| `--no-init-repo` | — | off | If not a git repo, exit instead of prompting to initialize one |
| `--project` | — | off | Write settings to `.entire/settings.json` even if it already exists |
| `--push` | — | off | When bootstrapping a new repo, push the initial commit to the created GitHub remote (implies creating the remote; without it the repo is created but not pushed) |
| `--repo-name` | `string` | — | GitHub repository name for the new repo (used when bootstrapping) |
| `--repo-owner` | `string` | — | GitHub user or organization login for the new repo |
| `--repo-visibility` | `string` | — | GitHub repository visibility: `public`, `private`, or `internal` |
| `--search-skill` | — | off | Install the optional Entire search skill for selected agent(s) |
| `--skip-initial-commit` | — | off | Don't create the initial commit when bootstrapping a new repo |
| `--skip-push-sessions` | — | off | Disable automatic pushing of session logs on git push |
| `--telemetry` | — | `true` | Enable anonymous usage analytics |
| `-y`, `--yes` | — | off | Accept all defaults without prompting (in a non-repo directory: init git, create private GitHub repo, commit, and push; then enable all agents and accept telemetry). Does not import existing agent history — see `--import-history` |
| `-h`, `--help` | — | — | help for enable |

Examples (from the docs page):

```bash
entire enable --yes --agent claude-code
entire enable --yes
```

**Notes (docs):** When both `--agent NAME` and `--yes` are passed, explicit agent selection takes precedence. `--telemetry=false` or `ENTIRE_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT` override the `--yes` default. The `--local` and `--project` flags control which Entire settings file is modified; agent project settings are always modified regardless of flags used.

&gt; ⚠️ **Docs vs 0.10.2 — most incomplete page.** `enable.md` documents 10 flags; the binary has 23. **Twelve flags are missing from the docs:** `--agent-help-skill`, `--import-history`, `--init-repo`, `--initial-commit-message`, `--no-github`, `--no-init-repo`, `--push`, `--repo-name`, `--repo-owner`, `--repo-visibility`, `--search-skill`, `--skip-initial-commit`. The entire non-git-repo bootstrap workflow (init repo + create GitHub repo via `gh`) is undocumented.
&gt; Additionally, the docs claim `--checkpoint-backend` defaults to **`branch`**; the binary states no default and marks **`refs` as recommended**. The docs' `-y/--yes` description ("Accept defaults without prompting") also omits the binary's important caveat that `--yes` in a non-repo directory will init git, create a **private GitHub repo**, commit, and **push** — and that it does **not** import existing agent history.

**Recommendation for the team:** treat `entire agent-help [command] --json` as the source of truth rather than `docs.entire.io/cli-reference/*`. It is generated from the installed command tree, and the docs page for `agent-help` says so explicitly. The docs appear to lag 0.10.2 by at least one significant release — three documented subcommands (`auth list`, `auth revoke`, `checkpoint rewind`) and one documented flag (`agent add --local-dev`) have been removed outright, so anything scripted against them will silently fall through to a parent help screen with **exit code 0** rather than failing loudly.</result>
