# Entire CLI — Repositories &amp; Mirrors Reference (verbatim digest)

Sources: the 10 assigned pages. Anything drawn from a *different* page is explicitly labeled `[outside the assigned 10]`. Nothing below is inferred — where the docs are silent, it says so.

---

## 1. How Entire identifies the current repo, and `--repo`

**The 10 assigned pages never describe repo auto-detection, and never mention a `--repo` flag.** Not once. All repository/mirror commands on those pages take the GitHub repo as a **positional argument**.

What the assigned pages *do* state about repo identifiers:

From `mirrors-in-cli.md`:
&gt; The repository can be written as `owner/repo`, an HTTPS GitHub URL, or an SSH GitHub URL. If you omit the cluster host, an interactive terminal prompts you to pick a region; non-interactive runs default to `aws-us-east-2.entire.io`. To mirror into more than one region from a script, run the command once per cluster host.

Forms used verbatim across the assigned pages:
- `github.com/OWNER/REPO` — `github-app-access.md`, `mirror-access-and-permissions.md`, `troubleshooting.md`
- `"github.com/$GH_OWNER/$GH_REPO"` — `mirrors-in-cli.md`
- Mirror clone URL: `entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;` and `entire://aws-eu-central-1.entire.io/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;`

So the **mirror URL identifier format** is:
```
entire://&lt;cluster-host&gt;/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;
```

`[outside the assigned 10]` The only statement in the entire docs corpus about detecting the current repo is in https://docs.entire.io/cli-reference/agent-help.md:
&gt; The output also identifies the repository detected from the Git remote. You do not need to provide the repository name when working in the current repository.

Note it says "the Git remote" — **the docs never say "origin" specifically** in this context.

`[outside the assigned 10]` `--repo` exists only on search/checkpoint commands, per https://docs.entire.io/cli-reference/search.md:
&gt; | `--repo REPOSITORY` | Filter by repository. Accepts `gh/owner/repo`, `et/project/repo`, `owner/repo`, a repository ULID, or `*`; repeatable and comma-separated. |

and https://docs.entire.io/cli-reference/checkpoint.md:
&gt; | `--repo OWNER/REPO` | Filter by repository. Use `*` to search all accessible repos. |

with https://docs.entire.io/guides/search/search-in-cli.md stating "By default, the CLI searches the current repository" and:
```bash
entire search "history import when enabling Entire" --repo entireio/cli
```

**Bottom line: `--repo` is a search-scoping flag, not a repository-targeting flag for mirror operations. The docs do not document when `--repo` is "needed" for repository/mirror management.**

---

## 2. The Entire GitHub App

Source: `github-app-access.md`.

### When you need it
&gt; Configure GitHub App access when you want to:
&gt; * make a GitHub repository appear in Entire
&gt; * create a repository mirror
&gt; * let Entire read repository and checkpoint activity
&gt; * let Entire verify GitHub collaborator access for mirror permissions
&gt;
&gt; GitHub App access controls which GitHub repositories Entire can see. It does not move, delete, or replace the GitHub repository.

### Install — Personal Repository
&gt; In Entire.io, open repository setup and choose **Configure GitHub App**.
&gt; GitHub opens the Entire GitHub App settings. From there, choose either:
&gt; * **All repositories**, if Entire should be able to access every repository in your account
&gt; * **Only select repositories**, if Entire should only access specific repositories
&gt;
&gt; Save the GitHub App configuration, then return to Entire.io.

### Install — Organization Repository
&gt; For organization repositories, a GitHub organization admin must install or update the Entire GitHub App.
&gt;
&gt; [same All/Only-select choice] …
&gt;
&gt; After the app is installed and allowed to access a repository, team members can use repositories they already have GitHub collaborator access to.

### Permissions requested (exact table)

**Repository permissions**

| Permission | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Administration: read | Read repository settings, teams, and collaborators. |
| Checks: read | Read check status for repository activity and review context. |
| Commit statuses: read | Read commit status information. |
| Contents: read/write | Read repository contents and support Git operations that require repository content access. |
| Pull requests: read/write | Read and support pull request workflows, comments, labels, assignees, and related metadata. |

**Organization permissions**

| Permission | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Members: read | Read organization members and teams. |

### Note on checkpoints (verbatim)
&gt; Checkpoint data is created locally by the Entire CLI and stored in your repository on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch. The GitHub App does not create local checkpoints for you.

### Verify access — all three must be true
&gt; * the Entire GitHub App must be installed for the account or organization
&gt; * the app must be allowed to access that repository
&gt; * your GitHub account must have access to that repository

### What breaks without it (verbatim)
&gt; If you uninstall the Entire GitHub App or remove a repository from its access list:
&gt; * the repository may stop appearing in Entire.io
&gt; * Entire cannot create or update mirrors for that repository
&gt; * Entire cannot sync new checkpoint activity from that repository
&gt; * existing checkpoint data already stored in Entire remains visible according to your current access settings
&gt; * local Git branches and local checkpoint data are not deleted

Command shown on that page:
```bash
entire repo mirror create github.com/OWNER/REPO
```

---

## 3. Mirrors

### Definition (`mirrors.md`)
&gt; A mirror is a synced, read-optimized copy of a GitHub repository, hosted on Entire's Git network in the region you choose. The repository stays on GitHub, and mirroring gives developers and agents a low-latency `entire://` remote they can clone, fetch, and push through without a hard cutover.

### How they work
&gt; When the mirror is created:
&gt; * Entire syncs the repository from GitHub
&gt; * the mirror is placed in the selected Entire region
&gt; * users get an `entire://` Git remote
&gt; * agents can clone and fetch from the regional mirror
&gt; * pushes through the mirror are authenticated with GitHub

### What changes / does not change
&gt; Creating a mirror gives you:
&gt; * a synced Entire copy of the GitHub repository
&gt; * an `entire://` remote for Git operations
&gt; * a regional read path for agents and automation
&gt; * an Entire mirror access list synced hourly with GitHub collaborators

&gt; Creating a mirror does not:
&gt; * move, delete, or replace the GitHub repository
&gt; * require every developer to change remotes
&gt; * rewrite Git history
&gt; * bypass GitHub authentication for pushes

### Why (from `overview.md`)
&gt; * **Regional control**: store and serve repository data from the Entire region your team chooses.
&gt; * **Consistency**: keep changing branch state coordinated as humans and agents push work.
&gt; * **Scale**: support many concurrent agent reads without overloading a single Git host.
&gt; * **Sovereignty**: keep sensitive code in the region your team chooses.
&gt; * **Traceability**: keep agent sessions and checkpoints connected to the repository they changed.

Also from `overview.md`: EntireDB is built around "repository data that never changes, such as Git objects" and "repository state that must stay consistent, such as branches and refs". "Supported regions are in the United States and the European Union."

### Exact CLI commands (`mirrors-in-cli.md`)

Prereqs: Entire installed; access to clone the GitHub repository; Entire GitHub App authorized for the repository.

```bash
entire login
```
```bash
entire auth status
```
```bash
entire repo mirror create
```

Interactive output verbatim:
```text
Select repos to mirror
Space to select, enter to confirm.

&gt; [•] &lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;

Select regions to mirror into
Each repo is mirrored into every selected region.

&gt; [•] us-east (us)
  [•] eu-west (eu)

REPO            REGION        STATUS  CLONE URL
&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;  us-east (us)  ready   entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;
&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;  eu-west (eu)  ready   entire://aws-eu-central-1.entire.io/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;
```
&gt; This registers the mirrors on Entire. It does not change any local Git checkout.

Scripted:
```bash
export GH_OWNER="&lt;owner&gt;"
export GH_REPO="&lt;repo&gt;"

entire repo mirror create "github.com/$GH_OWNER/$GH_REPO" aws-us-east-2.entire.io
```
```text
Registered mirror &lt;mirror-id&gt;
  entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;
  cloning. ready
```
&gt; Creating a mirror is idempotent for a given repository and region: rerunning the command returns the existing mirror. To return as soon as the mirror is registered instead of waiting for the initial clone, pass `--no-wait`.

Use with Git:
```bash
git clone "entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/$GH_OWNER/$GH_REPO"
```
```bash
cd "$GH_REPO"
git remote get-url origin
git remote set-url origin "entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/$GH_OWNER/$GH_REPO"
git fetch origin
```
```bash
cd "$GH_REPO"
git remote add entire "entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/$GH_OWNER/$GH_REPO"
git fetch entire
```
&gt; These commands only update your local `.git/config`. They do not create or delete the server-side mirror.

Push:
```bash
git push -u origin &lt;branch&gt;
```
&gt; Entire forwards the push through the mirror to the upstream GitHub repository. Your GitHub write access, branch protection rules, and required checks still apply.

Unmirrored branches:
```bash
git switch -c "entire/unmirrored/&lt;branch&gt;"
git push -u origin "entire/unmirrored/&lt;branch&gt;"
```
```bash
git push origin "entire/unmirrored/&lt;branch&gt;:&lt;branch&gt;"
```

Remove:
```bash
entire repo mirror remove "github.com/$GH_OWNER/$GH_REPO"
```
&gt; Removing a mirror does not delete or rewrite the GitHub repository. It also does not change any local Git remotes you configured in `.git/config`.

`[outside the assigned 10]` https://docs.entire.io/cli-reference/repo.md gives full syntax: `entire repo create NAME [flags]` (`--cluster-host HOST`, `--project PROJECT` required), `entire repo list PROJECT`, `entire repo get REPO`, `entire repo delete REPO` (`-f/--force`, `--project`, `-y/--yes`), `entire repo mirror create [GITHUB_URL] [CLUSTER_HOST]` (`--no-wait`, `--wait-timeout DURATION`, default `30m0s`), `entire repo mirror remove GITHUB_URL [CLUSTER_HOST]`, `entire repo mirror collaborators list GITHUB_URL [CLUSTER_HOST]`. Default cluster host `aws-us-east-2.entire.io`. Caller for `collaborators list` "must be a live GitHub admin of the upstream organization repository, or the owner of a user-owned repository."

### Unmirrored branches (`mirrors.md`)
&gt; Branches whose names start with `entire/unmirrored/` are never synced to GitHub. An unmirrored branch exists only in the Entire region you push it to: it does not appear on GitHub, and it is not available from mirrors in other regions.
&gt;
&gt; Because pushes to unmirrored branches stay in Entire instead of being forwarded to GitHub, they are not subject to GitHub's push rate limits. Use an unmirrored branch when you need:
&gt; * more push throughput than GitHub allows
&gt; * a branch you can keep pushing to while GitHub is unavailable
&gt; * a target for push benchmarks

Benchmarking tool referenced: ForgeMark, https://github.com/entireio/forgemark.

&gt; **Warning:** Entire does not currently back up unmirrored branches. Keep a copy of any work you cannot afford to lose on a mirrored branch or on GitHub.

### Access / permission model (`mirror-access-and-permissions.md`)
&gt; A mirror has two access requirements:
&gt; * **GitHub App access** lets Entire see and mirror the source repository.
&gt; * **GitHub collaborator access** controls who can clone, fetch, and push through the `entire://` remote.
&gt;
&gt; Entire syncs GitHub collaborators into mirror access hourly. There is no separate Entire-side permission list to grant or revoke manually.

&gt; A read-only GitHub collaborator can clone and fetch from the mirror. A GitHub collaborator with write access can also push through the mirror.
&gt;
&gt; GitHub access changes can take up to an hour to appear in Entire. If a GitHub collaborator has not signed in to Entire yet, automatic access can take up to an hour after they sign in.

Self-onboarding:
```bash
entire login
entire repo mirror create github.com/OWNER/REPO
```
&gt; If the mirror does not exist yet, Entire creates it. If the mirror already exists, the command checks the current user's GitHub access and returns the mirror URL.

Audit:
```bash
entire repo mirror collaborators list github.com/OWNER/REPO
```
&gt; This command is read-only. To change who can use the mirror, update collaborator access in GitHub and wait for the next hourly sync.

Offboarding warning (verbatim):
&gt; After GitHub access is removed, the user can no longer push through the mirror because pushes are authenticated with GitHub. Clone and fetch access may continue until the next hourly collaborator sync.

### Web flow (`mirrors-in-entire-io.md`)
Before you start: a GitHub account connected to Entire; access to the repo; an org admin available if the app isn't installed yet. Steps: **Configure GitHub App** → choose **All repositories** / **Only select repositories** → save → return to Entire.io → **Select Repositories** → **Choose Data Residency** ("Entire currently supports US and EU regions… You can choose a region per repository before continuing.") → repositories appear in the repository list.
&gt; If you are not a GitHub organization admin, you may not be able to add or remove repositories from the GitHub App configuration. You can still continue to Entire.io and use repositories that an admin has already made available.

### Troubleshooting (`troubleshooting.md`) — accordion titles and fixes
- **Unknown Command "repo"** → binary older than the mirror commands; update, then `entire repo mirror create github.com/OWNER/REPO`.
- **Repository missing from GitHub App access** → 1. Open https://github.com/settings/installations 2. Find **Entire** 3. Click **Configure** 4. Choose **All repositories** or select the repo 5. Save.
- **A user cannot create or join a mirror** → app can access the repo; user has GitHub access; user ran `entire login`; hourly sync may need to elapse.
- **Mirror creation takes a while** → `entire repo mirror create github.com/OWNER/REPO --no-wait`
- **Git cannot clone or fetch** → confirm the `entire://` URL; `entire login`; confirm collaborator access; wait for hourly sync; confirm network reach to the cluster host; `git remote -v`.
- **LFS clone fails during checkout** → see §4.
- **Pushes rejected** → confirm write access, branch protection/required checks, App access, then:
  ```bash
  entire auth logout
  entire auth login
  ```
- **A pushed branch does not appear on GitHub** → `entire/unmirrored/` prefix is intentional; republish with `git push origin "entire/unmirrored/&lt;branch&gt;:&lt;branch&gt;"`.
- **A removed user can still read from a mirror** → clone/fetch may continue until the next hourly sync.
- **Mirror removed but local checkout still points to it** → `git remote -v`, then `git remote set-url origin git@github.com:&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;.git`.
- **Still stuck?** → include `entire version`, GitHub repo URL, `entire://` mirror URL, App access status, phase (create/clone/fetch/push), full error. File at https://github.com/entireio/cli or ask in https://discord.gg/jZJs3Tue4S.

---

## 4. LIMITATIONS — and the fork question

### The entire documented limitations page contains exactly ONE limitation: Git LFS

`limitations.md` in full substance:
&gt; This page lists what repository mirrors do not support yet, with workarounds where they exist.
&gt;
&gt; ## Git LFS
&gt; Mirrors do not currently support [Git LFS](https://git-lfs.com/). Cloning a mirror of a repository that uses LFS fails during checkout, because Git LFS tries to download the large files through the `entire://` remote:

```text
Error downloading object: ... batch request: ssh: Could not resolve hostname entire
error: external filter 'git-lfs filter-process' failed
fatal: &lt;file&gt;: smudge filter lfs failed
```

Workaround, fresh clone:
```bash
GIT_LFS_SKIP_SMUDGE=1 git clone entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;
cd &lt;repo&gt;
git config lfs.url "https://github.com/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;.git/info/lfs"
git lfs pull
```
Existing checkout:
```bash
git config lfs.url "https://github.com/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;.git/info/lfs"
git lfs pull
```

Other limitation-shaped statements scattered across the assigned pages: unmirrored branches are not backed up, not on GitHub, not available from mirrors in other regions; offboarded users retain read access until the next hourly sync; collaborator changes take up to an hour.

### ⚠️ THE FORK QUESTION — THE DOCS ARE SILENT

**Explicit finding: the documentation nowhere addresses what happens to checkpoint data when a GitHub repository is forked, cloned fresh, or transferred.**

I grepped all 109 pages of the published docs corpus (every `.md` listed in https://docs.entire.io/llms.txt). Results:

**a) The string `refs/entire` appears ZERO times in the entire docs corpus.** So does the word "namespace". The premise that checkpoints live in `refs/entire/*` is **not something the docs state anywhere**. What the docs actually say:

- `github-app-access.md`: "Checkpoint data is created locally by the Entire CLI and stored in your repository on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch."
- `security.md`: "**The `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch** is the permanent home for redacted transcripts and checkpoint metadata. It's pushed alongside your code with normal `git push`."
- `[outside the assigned 10]` https://docs.entire.io/glossary.md: "**Checkpoints branch** | The permanent Git branch where Entire stores checkpoint and session metadata. The branch is named `entire/checkpoints/v1`."
- `[outside the assigned 10]` https://docs.entire.io/guides/checkpoints/store-checkpoints-with-your-code.md documents a **second, non-default backend** that *does* use bare refs:
  &gt; | **Branch-based (default)** | Stores all checkpoints on the shared `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch | 12-character hexadecimal ID, such as `a3b2c4d5e6f7` |
  &gt; | **Ref-based** | Stores each checkpoint in its own Git ref | 26-character, time-sortable ID, such as `01KVBJCWYA4YW6J5M9GP655HZN` |
  &gt;
  &gt; Ref-based storage lets Entire push and fetch checkpoints independently instead of updating one shared branch.
  ```bash
  entire enable --checkpoint-backend refs
  entire configure --checkpoint-backend refs
  entire configure --checkpoint-backend branch
  ```
  **The docs never state the ref path used by this backend, and never discuss its portability across clones or forks.**

**b) "fork" appears exactly 3 times in the whole corpus — all about the *checkpoint remote* same-owner rule, none about checkpoint data surviving a fork.** All three verbatim:

1. https://docs.entire.io/guides/configuration/checkpoint-storage.md:
   &gt; The different-owner case protects fork workflows, so contributors do not need write access to the upstream project's checkpoint repository.
2. https://docs.entire.io/guides/checkpoints/store-checkpoints-in-another-repo.md:
   &gt; The different-owner case is an intentional design to protect forks, keeping fork contributors from needing write access to the upstream project's checkpoint repository.
3. https://docs.entire.io/guides/checkpoints/troubleshooting.md, under "Checkpoint Remote Did Not Sync", listing common causes:
   &gt; * You are pushing from a fork, and fork detection skipped the organization checkpoint remote

   (This is the only reference to "fork detection" in the docs. The mechanism is never described further.)

The surrounding same-owner rule, verbatim:
&gt; * **Same owner**: checkpoints push normally. If your code is at `github.com/acme/app`, a checkpoint repo like `github:acme/app-checkpoints` works because both are owned by `acme`.
&gt; * **Different owner**: Entire won't push checkpoint data to that repository.

**c) "transfer" never appears in a repo-transfer sense.** All hits are about LFS "transfers", agent handoff, or org-ownership transfer before account deletion.

**d) Closest adjacent statement the docs do make** `[outside the assigned 10]`, https://docs.entire.io/cli/faq.md, explaining why trailers beat Git notes:
&gt; The trailer points to checkpoint metadata stored in the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch. Entire uses this approach because commit trailers survive common Git workflows better than metadata attached only to a commit SHA.
&gt;
&gt; Git notes have a few practical limitations:
&gt; * They are not pushed by normal Git pushes unless note refs are configured separately
&gt; * They are attached to commit SHAs, which change during rebases, amends, and cherry-picks
&gt; * Hosted Git platforms do not consistently support them in review and merge workflows

The "workflows" enumerated in that FAQ are rebase, amend, cherry-pick, squash, and hosted merge — **forking is not among them.** The only documented data-loss-on-workflow case is squash merge:
&gt; Some Git hosts can remove commit trailers during squash merges. If the `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer is removed, Entire cannot link the squashed commit back to its checkpoint metadata.

**Verdict for your team: the docs give you NO answer on fork behavior.** They do not say checkpoints survive a fork, do not say they are lost, and do not describe fork-time ref copying at all. The single fork-adjacent behavior documented ("fork detection skipped the organization checkpoint remote") concerns *pushing* checkpoints from a fork to a separate org checkpoint repo, not whether existing checkpoint data comes across with the fork. Anything beyond that must be verified empirically or with Entire support — do not source it from the docs.

---

## 5. Security &amp; privacy

Source: `security.md` and `delete-account.md`.

### Where data lives
&gt; Transcripts and checkpoint metadata go to a dedicated branch in **your** Git repository (`entire/checkpoints/v1`), not to a service Entire controls. The web app at [entire.io](https://entire.io) reads from that branch — what users see in the web app is the *already-redacted* content from the branch.
&gt;
&gt; Anyone with access to your repository can view the transcript branch. If your repository is public, that branch is public.

&gt; **Warning:** Redaction is best-effort, not a guarantee. For sensitive workloads — customer data, internal credentials, regulated material — use a **private** repository.

**Shadow branches (verbatim, important):**
&gt; **Shadow branches** (`entire/&lt;short-hash&gt;`) hold working snapshots during a live session. Metadata (transcripts, prompts, incremental checkpoint data, subagent transcripts) is redacted on write. **Code-file snapshots, however, are raw blobs of your working tree without redaction** — so any hardcoded secrets in your source files appear unredacted on the shadow branch. Gitignored files (e.g. `.env`) are filtered out of those snapshots as a partial defense. Shadow branches are local-only and **are not pushed by Entire**; do not push them manually, because unredacted source content would land on the remote.

&gt; **Two redaction passes for metadata.** … the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch contains only metadata, no source code.

**Commit trailers:**
&gt; Commits on your normal branches gain `Entire-Checkpoint: &lt;12-char-hex&gt;` and `Entire-Attribution: &lt;percent&gt; agent (&lt;n&gt;/&lt;m&gt; lines)` trailers. The trailers are public on push and live forever in commit messages. The hex is a random ID with no embedded PII; the attribution counts are aggregate numbers (no per-line content).

### What leaves the machine (exact egress table)

| When | Where | What's sent | Redacted? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `git push` of an Entire-enabled branch | Your Git remote (e.g. GitHub) | Already-redacted contents of `entire/checkpoints/v1` plus your code | Yes — redaction has happened by the time it's on the branch |
| `entire checkpoint explain --generate` (on-demand AI summary) | Whichever provider is configured via `summary_generation.provider` — typically the same agent that ran the session, e.g. Claude (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), or Gemini (Google) | Compacted transcript for the checkpoint being summarized | **Yes** — `redact.JSONLBytes` runs before the transcript is handed to the summary provider |
| Telemetry (unless opted out) | PostHog at `eu.i.posthog.com` | Command name, agent, flags-by-name, OS/arch, machine hash | N/A — no transcript or code data sent |
| GitHub auth (during `entire enable` flows that touch GitHub) | GitHub | Delegated to the `gh` CLI; Entire does not store its own credentials | N/A |
| Checkpoint remote (optional, opt-in `--checkpoint-remote`) | The configured separate Git remote | Redacted checkpoint branch only | Yes |

&gt; **External agent plugins.** Plugins are arbitrary executables on `$PATH` that the CLI invokes during agent sessions. They have the same filesystem access as the CLI itself. Only install plugins you trust.

### Telemetry
Captured per command: command name; selected agent name (or `auto`); whether Entire is enabled in the current repo; CLI version; OS and architecture; **names** of flags passed (**not** their values).
Not captured: "flag values, prompt text, transcripts, file paths, repository identifiers, GitHub usernames, source code, IP-derived location (the PostHog client is configured with `DisableGeoIP: true`)."
DistinctID: "a hashed machine identifier derived from `machineid.ProtectedID` — stable per machine, not directly mappable to a person."
Opt out (any one of): `--telemetry=false`; `"telemetry": false` in `.entire/settings.json` or `.entire/settings.local.json`; `ENTIRE_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT=1`.

### Secret redaction (always on, cannot be disabled)
Five built-in passes at condensation, all matches replaced with literal `REDACTED`:
1. **Entropy scoring** — 10+ alphanumeric chars, Shannon entropy above 4.5.
2. **Pattern matching via Betterleaks** (https://github.com/betterleaks/betterleaks) — ~260+ rules: cloud (AWS/GCP/Azure), VCS (GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket), payments (Stripe/Square), comms (Slack/Discord/Twilio), private-key blocks (RSA/DSA/EC/PGP/OpenSSH), generic credentials (bearer, basic auth, JWTs).
3. **Credentialed URI detection** — `user:password@host`, e.g. `REDACTED`, `REDACTED`.
4. **Database connection-string detection** — JDBC URIs, DB URLs (Postgres/MySQL/MariaDB/MongoDB/Redis), Postgres keyword DSNs, SQL-Server semicolon strings; whole string redacted when a real password is present.
5. **Bounded credential value detection** — `DB_PASSWORD`, `PGPASSWORD`, `MYSQL_PWD`, `REDIS_PASSWORD`, `MONGO_PASSWORD`, `MONGODB_PASSWORD`; JSON `password`/`passwd`/`pwd` when the object has both a host-like and user-like field.

False-positive skips (these are *coverage gaps*): JSON `signature` fields; keys ending `id`/`ids`; structural path keys (`filepath`, `file_path`, `cwd`, `root`, `directory`, `dir`, `path`); objects with `type` starting `image` or equal to `base64`; placeholder values by exact match or shape.

### User-defined redaction (from `v0.6.2`)
Go/RE2 regex — no lookarounds or backreferences. Inline via `redaction.custom_redactions` in `.entire/settings.json` (or `.entire/settings.local.json` for personal). Rule packs as YAML/JSON under `.entire/redactors/`; required fields `name` (matching filename stem), `version`, `rules` each with unique `id` and `regex`; extensions `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.json`. Personal packs under `.entire/redactors/local/` (kept gitignored by `entire enable`). `samples` self-tests log warnings but don't disable rules. No command needed — loaded on next CLI/hook invocation.

### PII redaction (opt-in, off by default)
`email` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]` (on when enabled); `phone` → `[REDACTED_PHONE]` (on); `address` US street → `[REDACTED_ADDRESS]` (off, more FP-prone). Allowlisted: `noreply@*`, `actions@*`, `*@users.noreply.github.com`, `*@noreply.github.com`. `custom_patterns` labels uppercase into the token.

### Documented redaction limitations
Best-effort; filenames/binary/deeply-nested may be missed; skip rules can hide secrets; custom rules are user-authored and invalid regexes are skipped not enforced; **"Custom redaction does not scrub source snapshots"**; "Users are ultimately responsible for reviewing what they push."

### Commit signing
Requires `commit.gpgsign = true` globally, a supported signer (GPG default, or SSH with `gpg.format = ssh` + `ssh-agent`), and `sign_checkpoint_commits` true (default). Best-effort: signing never blocks a commit; failure produces an unsigned commit plus a warning in `.entire/logs/`.

### Vulnerability reporting
security@entire.io — **do not** open a public GitHub issue. Acknowledgment within 48 hours; resolution within 90 days for criticals. Scope: the CLI (`entire`), official Entire GitHub repositories, entire.io services. Out of scope: third-party dependency issues, social engineering, DoS requiring local access, anything needing direct machine access. Local ReDoS and local resource exhaustion are tracked as bug reports at https://github.com/entireio/cli/issues.

### Retention and deletion (`delete-account.md`)
Deleted permanently: "your Entire account, your profile data, and the repositories connected to your personal account." Lost access: memberships in other organizations and granted roles — "the organizations themselves and their repositories are not deleted."

**Critical caveat, verbatim:**
&gt; Your Git repositories are not touched. Checkpoint data lives on the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch in your own repository, so deleting your Entire account does not erase it — delete that branch yourself if you want the transcripts gone.

Blocker: "You can't delete your account while you **own an organization**. Transfer ownership or delete each organization you own first."

Steps: Account settings → **Danger zone** → **Delete account** → type your GitHub username → **Delete my account** → signed out everywhere immediately, 7-day cooldown begins.

7-day cooldown: nothing erased yet; tokens stop working; signing back in does **not** cancel — you must click **Cancel deletion**. "Once the 7-day cooldown ends, deletion is **permanent and cannot be undone**."

On completion: personal data erased "across every region where we store it"; third-party processors (analytics, CRM) asked to erase — listed at https://entire.io/subprocessors. Retained where law requires: tax, accounting, security records (see https://entire.io/privacy).

**Repository content retention, verbatim:**
&gt; Repository content follows the repository, not your account: it is retained for as long as the repository exists on our services and is deleted when the repository (or the relevant history) is deleted.

Other data requests (access, correction, portability): legal@entire.io. Framed as GDPR right to erasure.

---

## Gaps — where the docs give no answer

1. **Fork behavior for checkpoint data.** Completely undocumented. See §4.
2. **Repo transfer between owners/orgs.** Never mentioned.
3. **Whether a fresh `git clone` brings the `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch or ref-based checkpoints.** Never stated.
4. **The actual ref path used by the ref-based checkpoint backend.** `refs/entire` appears nowhere in the docs.
5. **How "fork detection" works.** Named once, in a troubleshooting bullet, never explained.
6. **Repo auto-detection mechanics for mirror commands** — which remote, what happens with multiple remotes, or a documented `--repo` override for `entire repo`. Only `agent-help` mentions detection "from the Git remote."
7. **Mirror retention/deletion of Entire-side data after `entire repo mirror remove`** — the docs say only what it does *not* delete (GitHub repo, local `.git/config`).</result>

# Entire CLI Reference

Verified against **Entire CLI 0.10.2** (`go1.26.6`, `darwin/arm64`) on 2026-08-21. Where the published docs at `docs.entire.io` disagree with the installed binary, the binary is treated as authoritative and the difference is called out in a **⚠ Docs vs installed CLI** note.

## Global flag (all commands)

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--context` | `name` | active context | Act as this saved login name for this command only, instead of the active context (`entire auth contexts` lists them) |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for the command |
| `-v, --version` | — | — | Version for `entire` (root only) |

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** none of the fetched reference pages mention `--context`. It is present on every command in 0.10.2.

Environment variable documented in `entire --help` (not on any fetched page): `ACCESSIBLE` — set to any value to use simpler text prompts instead of interactive TUI elements (screen-reader friendly).

---

## `entire import`

Import pre-existing agent history into Entire (experimental).

```bash
entire import [flags]
entire import [command]
```

Subcommands (installed CLI):

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `claude-code` | Import existing Claude Code transcripts as read-only checkpoints |
| `codex` | Import existing Codex transcripts as read-only checkpoints |
| `copilot-cli` | Import existing Copilot CLI transcripts as read-only checkpoints |
| `cursor` | Import existing Cursor transcripts as read-only checkpoints |
| `factoryai-droid` | Import existing Factory AI Droid transcripts as read-only checkpoints |
| `gemini` | Import existing Gemini CLI transcripts as read-only checkpoints |
| `pi` | Import existing Pi transcripts as read-only checkpoints |

Flags on `entire import` itself: only `-h, --help`.

Flags on **each agent subcommand** (identical across all seven):

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--dry-run` | — | false | Report what would be imported without writing |
| `--path` | `string` | — | Override the transcript directory to import from |
| `--session` | `strings` | — | Import only these session IDs (repeatable) |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for the subcommand |

Examples (from docs):

```bash
entire import claude-code --dry-run
entire import claude-code
entire import codex --session SESSION_ID
entire import claude-code --path TRANSCRIPT_DIRECTORY
```

Notes (installed CLI long help): imports cover **the past month** for the current repo as read-only checkpoints; imported history is searchable and explainable but **imported sessions cannot be resumed**. Import honors checkpoint policy before scanning transcripts — if the configured `checkpoint_version` or `checkpoint_min_version` is unsupported by this CLI, import fails **even with `--dry-run`**.

Docs also state imported checkpoints are read-only, searchable, explainable, idempotent, non-rewindable, and not linked to Git commits.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:**
- Docs give the usage line as `entire import AGENT [flags]` and present `--dry-run` / `--path` / `--session` as flags of `import`. In 0.10.2 the usage is `entire import [command]` and those flags live only on the agent subcommands.
- Docs label the agent list "Supported Agents (CLI 0.7.8)". The same seven agents are present in 0.10.2.
- Docs say `--session ID` ("Import only this session ID. Repeatable."); binary spelling is `--session strings`.
- The checkpoint-policy failure note does not appear in the docs page.

---

## `entire investigate`

Run a multi-agent investigation. Agents take turns appending findings, evidence, and analysis to a shared findings document until quorum is reached. Labs entry: experimental.

```bash
entire investigate [seed-doc] [flags]
entire investigate [command]
```

Inputs (mutually exclusive): positional `[seed-doc]` path to a starting findings file, or `--issue-link &lt;url&gt;`. When neither is supplied and the spawn-time multi-agent picker fires, the picker collects an "Investigation prompt" that becomes the topic for the run.

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--agents` | `string` | — | Override configured agents (comma-separated) |
| `--allow-untrusted-seed` | — | false | Required to seed a non-interactive `--issue-link` run with attacker-influenced GitHub content |
| `--continue` | `string` | — | Resume an existing run by id |
| `--edit` | — | false | Re-open the investigate config picker |
| `--findings` | — | false | Browse local investigation manifests |
| `--issue-link` | `string` | — | GitHub issue or PR URL |
| `--max-turns` | `int` | `2` | Per-agent turn budget |
| `--quorum` | `int` | `0` | Approvals needed to terminate (`0` = all agents) |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for investigate |

Subcommands:

| Subcommand | Usage | Flags |
|---|---|---|
| `show` | `entire investigate show [run-id]` | `-h, --help` |
| `fix` | `entire investigate fix [run-id]` | `-h, --help` |
| `clean` | `entire investigate clean [run-id]` | `--all` (delete every investigation), `--force` (skip the confirmation prompt), `-h, --help` |

```bash
entire investigate leaderboard-route-investigation.md --max-turns 1
entire investigate --findings
entire investigate show RUN_ID
entire investigate fix RUN_ID
entire investigate clean RUN_ID
```

Full rationale for `--allow-untrusted-seed` (installed CLI): required to run a non-interactive `--issue-link` investigation, "otherwise refused: the seed is attacker-influenced GitHub content and agents run with permission/sandbox bypass".

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:**
- Docs list `--issue-link URL` under "Inputs" rather than as a flag; it is a normal flag (`--issue-link string`) in 0.10.2.
- Docs omit `--force` on `investigate clean`.
- Docs show `clean --all` as a separate subcommand row; it is `--all` on `clean`.
- Docs show run-ids as required (`show RUN_ID`); the binary declares them optional (`[run-id]`).

---

## `entire labs`

Explore experimental Entire workflows.

```bash
entire labs
```

Flags: only `-h, --help`.

Experimental commands listed by the installed 0.10.2 binary:

| Command | Description |
|---|---|
| `entire review` | Run a multi-agent review against the current branch |
| `entire investigate` | Run a multi-agent investigation against a topic, issue, or seed doc |
| `entire import claude-code` | Import existing Claude Code transcripts as local, read-only history |
| `entire tokens` | Analyze experimental token usage diagnostics |
| `entire tokens profile` | Aggregate token usage across committed checkpoints |
| `entire session tokens` | Show token usage and recommendations for a session |
| `entire blame` | Show which lines came from Entire checkpoints |
| `entire why` | Show why a line exists (commit, checkpoint, prompt, session) |
| `entire experts` | Show agent, skill, and tool provenance for files or topics |

```bash
# List experimental workflows
entire labs

# Open help for the currently listed review workflow
entire review --help
```

Note (docs): "`entire labs` is a discovery command. It does not run the listed workflows as nested subcommands." Use `entire review --help`, not `entire labs review --help`.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** the docs page mentions only `entire review` as the listed experimental workflow. The installed 0.10.2 binary lists nine entries (above), including `tokens`, `blame`, `why`, and `experts` — commands that are otherwise hidden from `entire --help`.

## `entire login`

Log in to Entire.

```bash
entire login [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--device` | — | false | Use the device-code flow (enter a code in your browser) instead of the default browser redirect |
| `--server` | `string` | `https://auth.entire.io` | Login server to authenticate against (rarely needed; the default serves all standard accounts) |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for login |

Docs note: "`entire login` stores an authentication token for the current Entire API base URL after login completes." Docs also describe an interactive prompt before launching the approval URL automatically, and printing the approval URL for manual access in non-interactive settings.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI — significant.** The docs page lists **no flags at all** and describes the device authorization flow (device code + browser approval) as the behavior of a bare `entire login`. In 0.10.2 the **default is a browser redirect**, and the device-code flow is opt-in via `--device`. `--server` is undocumented on the page.

---

## `entire logout`

Log out of Entire.

```bash
entire logout [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all-contexts` | — | false | Log out of every saved login (context), not just the active one |
| `--everywhere` | — | false | Revoke every session server-side, not just the current one |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for logout |

Behavior (installed CLI): by default this ends the active session only (server-side) and removes the active login from this machine. Other saved logins (contexts) remain and can still authenticate `git clone entire://…` against clusters fronted by their login server. `--everywhere` revokes every session on the active login server (all your devices). `--all-contexts` logs out of every saved login at once: each context's session is revoked server-side and the login is removed from this machine; combine with `--everywhere` to revoke every session on every context's login server. Without `--all-contexts`, logging out promotes the next saved login (if any) to active, so running `entire logout` repeatedly drains every saved login in turn.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI — significant.** The docs page lists no flags and describes logout as simply removing "the stored authentication token associated with the current Entire API base URL". 0.10.2 performs a **server-side session revocation** and supports `--everywhere` and `--all-contexts`, plus the context-promotion behavior — none of which is documented.

---

## `entire recap`

Summarize recent checkpoint activity.

```bash
entire recap [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--90` | — | false | Rolling 90 days |
| `--agent` | `string` | `all` | Agent id to show, or `all` |
| `--color` | `string` | `auto` | Color output: `auto`, `always`, or `never` |
| `--day` | — | default window | Today only (default) |
| `--insecure-http-auth` | — | false | Allow plain-HTTP auth (local dev only) |
| `--month` | — | false | This calendar month |
| `--static` | — | false | Print static output instead of opening the interactive recap |
| `--view` | `string` | `both` | Which columns to show: `you`, `team`, or `both` |
| `--week` | — | false | Last 7 days |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for `entire recap` |

By default `entire recap` opens the interactive recap view for today. Use `--static` for plain terminal output (scripts, transcripts, docs examples).

```bash
entire recap --static
```

```bash
# Today only
entire recap --day

# Last 7 days
entire recap --week

# This calendar month
entire recap --month

# Rolling 90 days
entire recap --90
```

```bash
entire recap --agent codex
```

```bash
entire recap --view you
entire recap --view team
entire recap --view both
```

```bash
# Open the interactive recap for today
entire recap

# Print a static recap for today
entire recap --static

# Print the last 7 days without color
entire recap --week --static --color never

# Show only your activity
entire recap --view you

# Show one agent's activity
entire recap --agent codex --static
```

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** none. This page matches 0.10.2 flag-for-flag, including defaults.

## `entire repo`

Manage Entire repositories.

```bash
entire repo [command]
```

Subcommands (installed CLI):

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `clone` | Clone a mirrored repository |
| `create` | Create a repository in a project |
| `delete` | Delete a repository by name or ULID |
| `get` | Show a repository by name or ULID |
| `list` | List repositories in a project |
| `mirror` | Manage GitHub-mirror placements on EntireDB clusters |
| `visibility` | Get or set a repository's visibility |

### `entire repo create`

```bash
entire repo create &lt;name&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--cluster-host` | `string` | jurisdiction default | Public host of the cluster to pin the repo to |
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |
| `--object-format` | `string` | server default | Git object format for the repository: `sha1` or `sha256` |
| `--project` | `string` | — | Owning project (name or ULID) **(required)** |

### `entire repo list`

```bash
entire repo list &lt;project&gt; [flags]
```

Navigation flags:

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all` | — | false | Fetch every repository instead of the first 1000 (slower on large projects) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `0` | Fetch and show only the first N repositories (0 uses the default fetch budget) |
| `--page-size` | `int` | — | Fetch a single page of at most N entries (1–500; the server may cap N further) and print the resume cursor |
| `--page-token` | `string` | — | Fetch the single page at this cursor (from a previous run's `nextPageToken`) |

Formatting flags:

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |
| `--no-pager` | — | false | Print directly to stdout instead of a pager for long output |

By default at most 1000 repositories are fetched; when the project has more, a note on stderr says so. Rows come in server order; this list has no local filters or sort. With `--json`, manual paging wraps rows in an `{items, nextPageToken}` envelope.

### `entire repo get`

```bash
entire repo get &lt;repo&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |
| `--project` | `string` | — | Owning project (name or ULID); required when `&lt;repo&gt;` is a name |

### `entire repo delete`

```bash
entire repo delete &lt;repo&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-f, --force` | — | false | Skip the confirmation prompt |
| `--project` | `string` | — | Owning project (name or ULID); required when `&lt;repo&gt;` is a name |
| `-y, --yes` | — | false | Skip the confirmation prompt (alias for `--force`) |

### `entire repo clone`

```bash
entire repo clone &lt;repo&gt; [target-dir] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--cluster` | `string` | — | Cluster host to clone from when the repo is mirrored on more than one (may belong to another auth context) |

```bash
entire repo clone /gh/entirehq/entire-api
entire repo clone /gh/entirehq/entire-api ./entire-api
entire repo clone /gh/entirehq/entire-api --cluster aws-us-east-2.entire.io
entire repo clone entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/entirehq/entire-api
```

With a `/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;` ref, looks up where the repo is mirrored: single cluster clones directly; multiple prompts you to pick (or pass `--cluster`). A full `entire://` URL already names the cluster and is passed straight through to `git clone` with no lookup (`--cluster` is ignored). `[target-dir]` is passed through to `git clone` either way.

### `entire repo visibility`

```bash
entire repo visibility get &lt;repo&gt; [flags]
entire repo visibility set &lt;repo&gt; &lt;public|private&gt; [flags]
```

Both take:

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |
| `--project` | `string` | — | Owning project (name or ULID); required when `&lt;repo&gt;` is a name |

`public` grants read-only (pull) access to any authenticated Entire user; push and management stay restricted to grantees. `private` restricts the repo to explicit grantees. Setting visibility requires manage permission on the repo.

### `entire repo mirror`

```bash
entire repo mirror [command]
```

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `collaborators` | List the users with access to a mirror (live GitHub-admin gated) |
| `create` | Register a GitHub mirror on a cluster |
| `get` | Show a repo's mirrors by owner/repo, or one mirror by ULID or clone URL |
| `list` | List repos you can see: existing mirrors and GitHub repos you could onboard |
| `remove` | Un-register a GitHub mirror from a cluster |
| `use` | Point this clone's git remote at an Entire mirror |

#### `entire repo mirror create`

```bash
entire repo mirror create [github-url] [cluster-host] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--no-wait` | — | false | Return once the placement is registered, without waiting for the initial clone |
| `--wait-timeout` | `duration` | `30m0s` | How long to wait for the initial clone to finish |

```bash
entire repo mirror create
entire repo mirror create github.com/octocat/hello-world
entire repo mirror create github.com/octocat/hello-world aws-us-east-2.entire.io
```

With no arguments, launches an interactive wizard: pick repos, pick one or more regions, then creates every (repo, region) mirror in parallel and prints the clone URLs. With a `&lt;github-url&gt;`, registers a placement and waits for the initial GitHub→EntireDB clone so `git clone` works on return. Idempotent on (upstream, cluster). When cluster-host is omitted, an interactive terminal offers a picker; non-interactive runs default to `aws-us-east-2.entire.io`.

#### `entire repo mirror remove`

```bash
entire repo mirror remove &lt;github-url&gt; [cluster-host] [flags]
```

No flags beyond `-h, --help`.

```bash
entire repo mirror remove github.com/octocat/hello-world
```

Other clusters' placements of the same upstream are unaffected. Cluster-host defaults to `aws-us-east-2.entire.io`.

#### `entire repo mirror get`

```bash
entire repo mirror get &lt;mirror&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |

```bash
entire repo mirror get octocat/hello-world
entire repo mirror get 01KS6KFJR2XS6PZ188MVYE07AN
entire repo mirror get entire://aws-us-east-2.entire.io/gh/octocat/hello-world
```

`&lt;mirror&gt;` is one of: `&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;` as shown in the `mirror list` NAME column (shows visibility, access, and the mirror on every cluster with per-cluster clone URL and status); a mirror ULID; or an `entire://&lt;cluster&gt;/gh/&lt;owner&gt;/&lt;repo&gt;` clone URL (a trailing `.git`, as pasted from `git remote -v`, is accepted). A clone URL resolves even when its cluster belongs to a federation other than the active auth context; owner/repo and ULID are looked up on the active context's login server.

#### `entire repo mirror list`

```bash
entire repo mirror list [flags]
```

Navigation flags:

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all` | — | false | Fetch the complete directory instead of the first 1000 entries (slower on large orgs) |
| `--limit` | `int` | `0` | Show at most N rows, applied after the local filters and sort (0 shows all fetched) |
| `--page-size` | `int` | — | Fetch a single page of at most N entries (1–500; server may cap further) and print the resume cursor |
| `--page-token` | `string` | — | Fetch the single page at this cursor |

Filtering &amp; sorting flags (applied only to fetched rows; combine with `--all` to cover the complete list):

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--access` | `string` | — | Filter by exact ACCESS (candidates only: `read`/`write`/`admin`) |
| `--available` | — | false | Keep only GitHub repos you could onboard as mirrors (drops existing mirrors) |
| `--cluster` | `string` | — | Keep only repos mirrored on this cluster, by slug or public host (drops onboardable candidates) |
| `--mirrored` | — | false | Keep only repos already mirrored (drops onboardable candidates) |
| `--name` | `string` | — | Filter by owner/repo substring, matching the NAME column (case-insensitive) |
| `--owner` | `string` | — | Filter by upstream owner login |
| `--private` | — | all | Filter by visibility: `--private` for private only, `--private=false` for public only (omit for all) |
| `--sort` | `string` | `name` ascending | Sort by column key (e.g. `name`, `clusters`; prefix `-` for descending) |
| `--status` | `string` | — | Filter by exact STATUS (mirrors: `ready`/`processing`/`failed`/`suspended`, matching any of a repo's placements; candidates: `available`/`owner-only`) |

Formatting flags:

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |
| `--no-pager` | — | false | Print directly to stdout instead of a pager for long output |

Sparse cells show `-`.

#### `entire repo mirror use`

```bash
entire repo mirror use [github-url] [cluster-host] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--cluster` | `string` | — | Cluster host to use when the repo is mirrored on several (same as `[cluster-host]`) |
| `--remote` | `string` | `origin` | Git remote to point at the mirror |
| `--upstream` | `string` | `upstream` | Remote to preserve the replaced URL under; empty to discard it |

```bash
entire repo mirror use
entire repo mirror use --cluster aws-us-east-2.entire.io
entire repo mirror use github.com/octocat/hello-world
entire repo mirror use github.com/octocat/hello-world aws-us-east-2.entire.io
entire repo mirror use --remote entire
entire repo mirror use --upstream ''
```

Rewrites the local git remote so fetch and push go through an Entire mirror instead of the forge. It only ever edits local git config — the mirror must already exist (`entire repo mirror create`); nothing server-side is changed.

#### `entire repo mirror collaborators list`

```bash
entire repo mirror collaborators list &lt;github-url&gt; [cluster-host] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output raw JSON instead of a table |

```bash
entire repo mirror collaborators list github.com/acme/widget
```

Lists the principals that can pull the mirror on the target cluster, with reader/writer role resolved from the control plane. The caller must be a live GitHub admin of the upstream (org repo) or its owner (user repo). Cluster-host defaults to `aws-us-east-2.entire.io`.

Docs note: "Mirror access follows GitHub collaborator permissions and syncs hourly," requiring administrative status in the upstream repository. Interactive mode activates when no arguments are provided.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI — significant.** The docs page omits, entirely: the `clone` and `visibility` subcommands; the `mirror get`, `mirror list`, and `mirror use` subcommands; `--json` and `--object-format` on `repo create`; the whole pagination/filter/sort flag surface on `repo list` and `mirror list`; and `--json` on `repo get`/`repo delete`-family commands. Everything the docs page does list matches the binary.

---

## `entire session resume` (docs page: "resume")

```bash
entire session resume [branch] [flags]
```

| Argument | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `branch` | Optional branch to resume the agent session from |

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-f, --force` | — | false | Resume from older checkpoint without confirmation |

With no argument, opens an interactive picker of stopped sessions across all worktrees so you don't have to remember which branch you left work on. Picking a session checks out its branch, restores its checkpoint session log, and asks whether Entire should start the agent. If the branch is already checked out in another worktree, you'll be pointed there instead.

With a branch argument, switches to that branch and resumes its session directly:

1. Checks out the specified branch
2. Finds the session ID from commits unique to this branch (not on main)
3. Restores the session log if it doesn't exist locally (an existing local log is kept as-is; use `--force` to overwrite it from the checkpoint)
4. In an interactive terminal, asks whether to start the agent; otherwise prints the command to resume the session

Notes: if the branch doesn't exist locally but exists on origin, you'll be prompted to fetch it. If newer commits without checkpoints exist on the branch (e.g. after merging main or cherry-picking from elsewhere), this operation will reset your Git status to the most recent commit with a checkpoint; you'll be prompted to confirm.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** the flag surface matches. Two additions in the binary's long help that the docs page omits: step 3's "an existing local log is kept as-is; use `--force` to overwrite it from the checkpoint", and "if the branch is already checked out in another worktree, you'll be pointed there instead". Also note **`entire resume` is not a command** — running it fails with `unknown command "resume" for "entire"`. The docs URL is `/cli-reference/resume.md` but the command is `entire session resume`.

---

## `entire review`

Run a multi-agent review against the current branch or a `--target` branch: several reviewer agents review the change in parallel, then a single judge consolidates their reports into the final verdict in a closing round. Reviews are saved as named profiles in Entire settings and clone-local preferences. On first run, guided setup writes a profile and asks before starting agents.

```bash
entire review [flags]
```

(The docs give the usage line as `entire review [PROFILE] [flags]`; the binary's usage line is `entire review [flags]`, and `--profile NAME` is documented as "also accepted as positional arg". Both forms work.)

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--agent` | `string` | — | Run one configured reviewer from the selected profile |
| `--agents` | — | false | List the reviewer agents you can pass to `--agent` for the selected profile |
| `--base` | `string` | first existing of `origin/HEAD`, `origin/main`, `origin/master`, `main`, `master` | Git ref to scope the review against |
| `--cleanup-worktree` | — | false | Remove a newly-created target worktree after a successful review (interactive runs ask when omitted) |
| `--configure` | — | false | Set up a review profile; shows available agents and accepts `--set-*` flags for non-interactive config |
| `--edit` | — | false | Re-open the advanced review profile skill picker |
| `--findings` | — | false | Browse local review findings; pass a handle to print one saved run |
| `--list` | — | false | List configured review profiles (reviewers and judge) |
| `--local` | — | false | With `--configure`: save the profile to `.entire/settings.local.json` (per-developer) instead of `.entire/settings.json` |
| `--model` | `string` | — | Override the model for the `--agent` reviewer (requires `--agent`) |
| `--models` | — | false | List the models each review agent advertises (optionally filtered by `--agent`) |
| `--profile` | `string` | `review_default_profile` or `general` | Review profile to run |
| `--prompt` | `string` | — | One-off instructions appended to this review run |
| `--set-agents` | `strings` | — | With `--configure`: reviewer agents for the profile (comma-separated) |
| `--set-judge` | `string` | — | With `--configure`: the consolidating judge as `agent[=model]` |
| `--set-model` | `stringArray` | — | With `--configure`: per-reviewer model as `agent=model` (repeatable) |
| `--set-output` | `string` | `local` | With `--configure`: where the verdict is delivered (`local` or `trail`) |
| `--set-slot` | `stringArray` | — | With `--configure`: a reviewer slot as `agent[=model]` (repeatable; same agent/model may repeat to run it multiple times) |
| `--set-task` | `string` | — | With `--configure`: the profile's canonical task text |
| `--target` | `string` | — | Branch, trail ID, or Entire trail URL to check out in a worktree and review |
| `--timeout` | `duration` | **none** | Optional hard cap per reviewer (reviewers otherwise run until they finish, like a skill invoked directly in a session). When set, it also bounds the consolidating judge; unset, the judge keeps its own 20m default |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for review |

```bash
entire review --configure
```

```bash
entire review --configure \
  --set-agents codex,claude-code \
  --set-judge codex \
  --set-output local
```

```bash
entire review general
entire review --profile general
entire review general --base origin/main
entire review general --prompt "Focus on auth, error handling, and missing tests."
entire review general --agent codex
entire review general --base origin/my-parent-branch
```

```bash
entire session attach SESSION_ID --review
```

Timeout detail (installed CLI): a positive `--timeout` value also bounds the consolidating judge, which otherwise keeps its own 20m default. The judge is never unbounded; its timeout or error fails the review with no verdict. A timed-out reviewer's siblings and the judge still proceed.

Note (docs): "`entire review --findings` only browses local findings. It does not apply fixes."

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI — significant.**
- **`--timeout` default is wrong in the docs.** Docs say "default: `10m0s`; `0` disables". The binary has **no default** — reviewers run unbounded until they finish; only the judge carries an internal 20m default.
- Docs omit `--target REF` and `--cleanup-worktree` entirely. Both exist in 0.10.2, and `--target` changes the command's scope (review a branch/trail in a worktree rather than the current branch).
- Docs describe `--set-output local` as if `local` were the only accepted value; the binary accepts `local` or `trail`.
- The binary's attach hint is `entire session attach --review &lt;id&gt;`; the docs example is `entire session attach SESSION_ID --review`. Both are valid orderings.

---

## `entire search`

Search checkpoints, commits, and sessions using hybrid search (semantic + keyword), powered by the Entire search service. Add `--code` to search code content instead. Requires authentication via `entire login`.

```bash
entire search [query] [flags]
```

`entire checkpoint search` is an equivalent alias.

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all-repos` | — | false | Search all accessible repos instead of just the current one |
| `--author` | `string` | — | Filter by author name |
| `--branch` | `string` | — | Filter by branch name |
| `--case-sensitive` | — | false | Case-sensitive code search (only with `--code`) |
| `--code` | — | false | Search code content across repositories |
| `--compact` | — | false | Trimmed JSON output for agents: id, repo, files touched, score, match snippet, and a truncated title instead of the full prompt (implies `--json`) |
| `--date` | `string` | — | Filter by time period (`week` or `month`) |
| `--json` | — | false | Output as JSON |
| `--limit` | `int` | `10` | Maximum number of results (per page for checkpoint search, total for `--code`) |
| `--page` | `int` | `1` | Page number (1-based) |
| `--repo` | `strings` | current repo | Filter by repository (`gh/owner/repo`, `et/proj/repo`, `owner/repo`, ULID, or `*`); repeatable and comma-separated |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for search |

Examples from the installed binary:

```bash
entire search "retry backoff" --json
entire search "retry backoff" --json --compact
entire search "auth timeout author:alice date:week"
entire search --code "parseToken"
```

Examples from the docs page:

```bash
entire search "auth token refresh"
entire search "auth token refresh" --repo entireio/cli
entire search "repo:* auth token refresh"
entire search "retry logic" author:alex branch:main date:month
entire search "auth token refresh" --json
```

### Inline filters

```bash
entire search "retry logic author:alex date:month branch:main repo:entireio/cli"
```

Supported inline filters: `author:NAME`, `date:week|month`, `branch:NAME`, `repo:OWNER/NAME`, `repo:*`.

### Code search

```bash
entire search --code "refreshAccessToken"
entire search --code "refreshAccessToken" --repo entireio/cli
entire search --code "repo:* refreshAccessToken"
entire search --code "refreshAccessToken" --json
```

Results are grouped under a `repo:path` heading with line-numbered matches beneath it. Code Search matches exact text and is currently case-insensitive: "The CLI accepts `--case-sensitive`, but the search backend does not yet apply it." In `--code` mode, `repo:` is the only supported inline filter — `author:`, `branch:`, and `date:` tokens are treated as literal query text, and the `--author`, `--branch`, `--date`, and `--page` flags do not apply. In interactive search, press `4` to switch to the **Code** tab.

Detail-fetching (installed CLI only): fetch full detail for a single result with `entire checkpoint explain &lt;id&gt;`, or add `--full` to that command to pull the checkpoint's entire session transcript. For a checkpoint hit from another GitHub repo, add `--repo &lt;owner/name&gt;` to `entire checkpoint explain` (requires the full checkpoint ID; unrelated to `search`'s `--repo` filter).

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:**
- Docs omit `--compact` (agent-oriented trimmed JSON, implies `--json`). It exists in 0.10.2.
- Docs state "In CLI versions 0.9.0 and earlier, `entire search` was hidden from `entire --help` but remained runnable." Confirmed resolved: `search` is listed under "Sessions &amp; Checkpoints" in 0.10.2's `entire --help`.
- Docs omit the `entire checkpoint explain` / `--full` follow-up guidance.

---

## `entire session` (alias: `entire sessions`)

View and manage agent sessions tracked by Entire.

```bash
entire session [command]
```

Aliases: `session`, `sessions`.

| Subcommand | Description |
|---|---|
| `adopt` | Adopt an active session from another worktree |
| `attach` | Attach an existing agent session |
| `current` | Show the active session for the current worktree |
| `info` | Show detailed session information |
| `list` | List all sessions |
| `resume` | Resume a stopped session (interactive picker, or by branch) |
| `stop` | Stop one or more active sessions |
| `tokens` | Show token usage and optimization recommendations for a session |

```bash
entire session list                      List all sessions
entire session info &lt;session-id&gt;         Show session details
entire session info &lt;session-id&gt; --json  Output as JSON
entire session tokens &lt;session-id&gt;       Show token usage
entire session stop                      Interactive stop
entire session current                   Active session for cwd
entire session attach &lt;session-id&gt;       Attach an external session
entire session adopt &lt;session-id&gt; --from ../repo  Adopt a moved session
entire session resume &lt;branch&gt;           Resume from a branch
```

### `entire session list`

```bash
entire session list [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output as JSON |

Lists all sessions tracked by Entire, including ended sessions. For active sessions only, use `entire status`.

```bash
entire sessions list           List all sessions across all worktrees
entire sessions list --json    Same list as a metadata-only JSON array
```

### `entire session info`

```bash
entire session info &lt;session-id&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output as JSON |
| `--transcript` | — | false | Stream raw agent transcript bytes to stdout |

Shows agent, model, status, worktree, timing, token usage, checkpoint linkage, and files touched. Works for both active and ended sessions.

Output modes: default is a human-readable summary; `--json` is a metadata-only JSON envelope (no transcript bytes); `--transcript` streams the live raw agent transcript bytes to stdout in the agent's native format (JSONL for Claude/Cursor/Codex, JSON for Gemini). The snapshot is bounded to the file size observed at open. JSONL streams have a trailing partial line trimmed; JSON documents are emitted intact.

```bash
entire sessions info &lt;session-id&gt;
entire sessions info &lt;session-id&gt; --json
entire sessions info &lt;session-id&gt; --transcript &gt; session.jsonl
```

### `entire session current`

```bash
entire session current [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output as JSON |
| `--transcript` | — | false | Stream raw agent transcript bytes to stdout |

Resolves the session writing checkpoints in this directory right now, preferring sessions from the current worktree and falling back to the most recent session if no state matches. Equivalent to running `sessions info` on the session ID returned by `FindMostRecentSession`.

```bash
entire session current
entire session current --json
entire session current --transcript &gt; session.jsonl
```

### `entire session tokens`

```bash
entire session tokens [session-id] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--agent-brief` | — | false | Output compact next-step guidance for agents |
| `--current` | — | false | Prefer the current worktree's most recent session |
| `--json` | — | false | Output as JSON |

When no session ID is provided, Entire reports on the most recently active session, preferring the current worktree and falling back to the newest session. The report uses token and context data Entire already captured for the session. Use `--agent-brief` when an agent needs compact guidance for the next step.

```bash
entire session tokens
entire session tokens --current --agent-brief
entire session tokens --json
```

### `entire session stop`

```bash
entire session stop [session-id] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--all` | — | false | Stop all active sessions |
| `-f, --force` | — | false | Skip confirmation prompt |

Marks one or more active sessions as ended. Fires `EventSessionStop` through the state machine with a no-op action handler, so no condensation or checkpoint-writing occurs. To flush pending work, commit first.

```bash
entire sessions stop                     No sessions: exits. One session: confirm and stop. Multiple: show selector
entire sessions stop &lt;session-id&gt;        Stop a specific session by ID
entire sessions stop --all               Stop all active sessions
entire sessions stop --force             Skip confirmation prompt
```

### `entire session attach`

```bash
entire session attach &lt;session-id&gt; [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `-a, --agent` | `string` | `claude-code` | Agent that created the session (see `entire agent list` for registered agents, including external) |
| `-f, --force` | — | false | Skip confirmation and amend the last commit with the checkpoint trailer (best-effort; if the amend fails the checkpoint is still created and the trailer is printed for manual paste) |
| `--review` | — | false | Tag the attached session as an agent review |
| `--skills` | `strings` | — | Optional: declare which review skills were run in this session. Only used with `--review` |

Attaches an existing agent session that wasn't captured by hooks. Creates a checkpoint from the session's transcript and links it to the last commit. Use when hooks failed to fire or weren't installed when the session started, or to attach a research session. If the last commit already has a checkpoint, the session is added to it; otherwise a new checkpoint is created. With `--review`, the first user prompt in the transcript is recorded as the review prompt. Works with any registered agent, including external agents enabled via `external_agents` in settings. If `--agent` doesn't locate a transcript, Entire auto-detects the agent from the transcript and prints the detected agent name.

### `entire session adopt`

```bash
entire session adopt [session-id] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--force` | — | false | Replace an existing local state file for the same session |
| `--from` | `string` | — | Source worktree that already tracks the session |
| `--yes` | — | false | Confirm same-store adoption and replacement without prompting |

Useful when an agent starts in one repository or worktree, then moves and makes changes in another. Adoption moves the live session state into the current repo and seeds it with the current repo's uncommitted file changes so the next commit can be linked normally. When the source and target share a Git session store, adoption moves the same session state file to the current worktree and requires `--force` or `--yes`.

```bash
entire session adopt 019ed5fe-ec49-7a72-89fd-f38e323f5448 --from ../cli
entire session adopt --from /path/to/source/worktree
entire session adopt --from ../source-worktree --yes
```

### `entire session resume`

See the `resume` section above.

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** the sessions page names all eight subcommands correctly but documents almost none of their flags. Specifically undocumented there: `--transcript` on `info` and `current` (a substantial capability — raw transcript streaming), `--agent`/`--force`/`--review`/`--skills` on `attach`, and `--force`/`--yes` on `adopt`. No contradictions found — only omissions.

|---|---|---|---|
| `--detailed` | — | false | Show detailed status for each settings file |
| `--json` | — | false | Output as JSON |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for status |

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** the docs page lists only `--detailed`. 0.10.2 also has `--json`. The docs one-liner is "Show the current Entire status for this repository"; the binary's is "Show whether Entire is currently enabled or disabled".

## `entire version`

Show build information including the CLI version, Git commit, Go version, and OS/architecture.

```bash
entire version
entire --version
```

Flags: only `-h, --help`.

Docs sample output:

```
Entire CLI v1.2.3 (abc1234)
Go version: go1.21.0
OS/Arch: darwin/arm64
```

Actual output from the installed binary:

```
Entire CLI 0.10.2
Go version: go1.26.6
OS/Arch: darwin/arm64
```

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** the docs sample shows a `v` prefix and a parenthesized Git commit hash (`Entire CLI v1.2.3 (abc1234)`). 0.10.2 prints **no `v` prefix and no commit hash** — just `Entire CLI 0.10.2`. If you parse this output in scripts, the docs format is not what ships. (The docs values themselves are placeholders, but the shape differs.)

## `entire why`

Explain the commit, checkpoint, prompt, and session behind a file or line. Target a specific line with `&lt;file&gt;:12` or the `--line` flag.

```bash
entire why &lt;file&gt;[:line] [flags]
```

| Flag | Arg | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--json` | — | false | Output explanation as JSON |
| `--line` | `string` | — | Explain a specific line, for example `12` (same as `&lt;file&gt;:12`) |
| `-h, --help` | — | — | Help for why |

Examples from the installed binary:

```bash
entire why src/auth.go:42
entire why src/auth.go:42 --json
```

Examples from the docs page:

```bash
entire why overview.mdx
entire why overview.mdx:12
entire why overview.mdx --line 12
entire why overview.mdx:12 --json
```

**⚠ Docs vs installed CLI:** none — flags, argument forms, and defaults match. Note `why` is not listed in `entire --help`; it is discoverable only via `entire labs`.
